# Issues

**The register of everything open in this repo.** Defects, gaps, and questions
awaiting a ruling. One id per item, stable forever.

**This file is the source of truth for what is open.** Nothing is open unless it
is here. Other documents no longer carry their own open-item lists — they link
here. Long analysis may stay where it was argued (`packages/*/PROJECT_STATE.md`,
`IDEAS/*.md`); the **Detail** column points at it. What must
never happen again is an item existing in exactly one document that nobody reads.

**What belongs here:** shipped behavior that is wrong, a gap that blocks
something, a question that needs a decision. **What does not:** features and
roadmap (`IDEAS/`), settled rulings (`DECISIONS.md`), traps that are correct
behavior you must know about (`CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards), history
(git, `FJS-D675`).

**Closed items age out to [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).**
§ Closed below keeps the recent ones; the archive keeps the rest, verbatim.
Together they hold every id ever issued, and an id is never reused — so a
`FJS-###` named in a comment, a handoff, or a package file resolves in exactly
one of the two files. Search both before concluding an id is unknown.

## Conventions

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Id** | `FJS-###` for a defect or gap, `FJS-D##` for a decision. **Never reused, never renumbered.** Closing an item moves it to § Closed with its id. |
| **Sev** | `S1` blocker — data loss, a security hole, or cannot ship · `S2` high — silently wrong, costs a day to find · `S3` medium · `S4` low or cosmetic |
| **Status** | `open` — verified open on the date shown · `stale?` — inherited from an older audit and **not re-verified**; probe before acting · `contested` — the recorded diagnosis is doubted, see the note |
| **Verified** | The date somebody last ran it. A `stale?` row's date is when it was *written*, not confirmed. |
| **Blocked by** | A row that cannot start until another closes says `blocked by FJS-###` in its own text, and nowhere else. `fli next` takes it out of the ranking until that row closes and weighs the blocker up. Write it only where the order is real — a row that merely relates to another cites it. |
| **By hand** | A row a headless session cannot finish — a run longer than a session lasts, a check only the owner can make — says `by hand` as its own segment of its links cell. `fli next` keeps it ranked and marks it; `fix:loop` skips it. |
| **Env** | A row whose fix is the environment and not the code says `env: <cause>` as its own segment of its links cell, cause one of `port` · `shared db` · `stale process` · `timing` · `real machine` · `network` (`FJS-D1118`). The segment names where the FIX lives, not where the symptom shows. `fli next` keeps it ranked and marks it; `fix:loop` skips it; `fli register:check` refuses a cause outside the list (`env-cause`) and warns on a title that reads like one with no segment (`env-smell`). |
| **Seam** | A row whose bug crosses a named handoff says `seam: <name>` as its own segment of its links cell, the name from the root `CLAUDE.md` § Bridge index key list (`FJS-D1135`). `env:` names where the fix lives; `seam:` names which handoff the bug crosses. Written by hand; `fli register:check` refuses a name the key list does not hold (`seam-name`). |

**A row has exactly the columns its table's header names**, and `fli
register:check` fails on anything else (`row-shape`). Both ways of getting it
wrong are silent: a row with FEWER columns is one pasted from the other table,
and the reader infers a shape from the cell count, so it is given a status
nobody wrote and no other rule can see it; a row with MORE has the excess
**dropped when the file is rendered** — in § Closed that is the *How* column, so
the row displays with no citations while they sit in the file. Links belong
inside the last declared column, joined with ` · `, and a `|` that is part of a
sentence is escaped `\|`.

`stale?` is load-bearing. The eight rows inherited from the 2026-07-31 audit
(now `IDEAS/review-coherence.md`) were re-probed on 2026-08-10 and **five of the eight were wrong**:
two were already fixed (conduit's secrets, `admin:generate`), one was never
reproducible (conduit's version skew), and two were right about a defect and
wrong about what it was — sierra's prefetch is not an auth *bypass* but a
cached 401, and caravan's row named three bugs of which two were fixed.
**Treat a `stale?` row as a lead, not a fact** (`VERIFYING.md`).

**The register also goes stale in the closing direction, which nothing here was
watching for.** `FJS-043` was fixed in the tree with a comment describing the
old defect in the past tense, and stayed `open` for eight days because closing
is a second action nobody owed. A row's `Verified` date is evidence about when
somebody last *looked*, not about whether the defect survives — 22 rows carried
`open` at 2026-08-02 when this audit ran, which is the same fiction `stale?`
exists to name, wearing the other label.

| <a id="fjs-009"></a>FJS-009 | repo | **CI has never gone green on a runner.** Done when `bun run ci` exits 0 in a fresh clone of the COMMITTED tree, run from a directory the Docker daemon can read (under snap Docker, not `/tmp`: [FJS-1365](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1365)) and with `BUN_INSTALL` pointed at a scratch directory so the run leaves the machine's global links alone ([FJS-1364](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1364)). Green on the runner is the owner's check after a push, not this row's, and nobody without admin rights can read the runner's logs. The annotations are public, though: `GET /repos/frontierJS/frontierjs/commits/<sha>/check-runs`, then its `annotations_url`, names every failed phase. **The runner's four failures on `573cfd30`** were a seams snapshot, an exports snapshot, 90 dead links and the refused deploy's health race. All four are fixed in the working tree and not yet committed: `packages/cli/core/tree.js`, `register-check.js`, `seams.js`, `commands/deploy/_module.md`, and the css `prepack`. **The fresh-clone run of 2026-09-26** (`0559c29d` plus those fixes) failed 7 ways. Four are rows: [FJS-1367](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1367) `tutor:access`, [FJS-1368](#fjs-1368) basecamp's `schema.d.ts`, [FJS-1369](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1369) the website and orion, and [FJS-1365](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1365) `pause queues`, which is local-only. `repo-atlas` and `repo-report` were stale because ISSUES.md moved after they were generated, so regenerate them in the commit. `packages/cli` had 3 failures in the full run that do not reproduce when its suite runs alone, so re-measure it once the rest is green | open | 2026-10-22 | [scripts/ci.mjs](scripts/ci.mjs) · wave 0.1 · blocked by FJS-1365 · blocked by FJS-1367 · blocked by FJS-1368 · blocked by FJS-1369 · Re-probed 2026-10-10: the four cited blockers are closed, and `bun run ci -- --phase scaffold` is red on the published `@frontierjs/litestone@1.1.9` refusing `driver trail` (FJS-2084), so no committed tree goes green until litestone and auth are republished · blocked by FJS-2084 · 2026-10-20: the runner never reached a verdict — every push since at least 8ec4856d was CANCELLED at timeout-minutes 30 (check-run annotations), and a full run is 43-49 min serial here; ci.yml now caps at 90 (GitHub's ceiling is 360). Fresh clone of 37460641 (BUN_INSTALL scratch, under $HOME) was red 7 ways, all fixed: lives-in and the proofs report probed sibling checkouts on disk (now graded off a ../ spelling, proofs.js kind outside); example's app snapshot raced getOrCreate across parallel generators (tenant.js, tenant-create.test.ts six-process case); haven workshop frames stale against the ledger; print-kit blamed FJS_CHROME under an injected find; tutor:fleet (FJS-3353, systemd-run --user when not root). That exposed the course pass: tutor:tools' replay red because the GUI ran repo-scope rules (home-path-literal) over a lone app — server.js now adds the repo scope only at a workspace root (server.test.js). Second fresh clone of 563f7abd (without the server.js fix): left are that replay plus packages/cli (2), packages/mesa (files.spec Enter reads the typed path, AbortError) and packages/ui, each green when run alone — the full-run-only failures this row already names; the run log truncates output so cli's and ui's failing cases are not recorded. Next: a runner verdict on the next push, then whether the user manager exists there for tutor:fleet · [ci.yml](.github/workflows/ci.yml) · [server.js](packages/cli/core/server.js) · Run 39839669308 (6f5e0faa): two runner-only causes fixed. Deploy cycle and tutor:deploy made the server db dir as the runner (uid 1001) where the image runs as 1000, so migrate could not open /db/app.db; both now hand it over through makeContainerDir in packages/cli/core/tutor.js (removeContainerWritten takes it back at teardown), and 05-backup no longer mkdirs the backups dir on the host. tutor:app and tutor:ui probed 127.0.0.1 while vite bound localhost, ::1 first on the runner; the tutor's startWeb binds --host 127.0.0.1 and points the proxy at 127.0.0.1. All four green locally; the uid split is only provable on the runner · Run 39864514148 (90207c86): deploy cycle, tutor:deploy and tutor:app green on the runner. tutor:tools red everywhere: the widened migration-history rule graded the lesson's Note, added by db:push — 01-app now writes it with db:migrate add_note. tutor:ui reached Chrome and timed out launching it while the ui suite's Chrome started; mesa/src/drive.js's timeout now carries Chrome's stderr so the next run names the cause |

| <a id="fjs-3533"></a>FJS-3533 | basecamp | **Nothing upgrades an enrolled machine's Outpost: the unit pins the version it was installed with, so a fix shipped in a new Outpost reaches no machine until someone edits the unit by hand** | open | 2026-10-27 | yuma's `/etc/systemd/system/outpost.service` runs `bun x --bun @frontierjs/outpost@0.1.5`. Publishing a static App there failed with *path 'assets/_...404_-U15rT7nd.js' has a segment that is not a plain file name* — Vite's chunk for a `[...404]` route — which 0.1.5 refuses and [static.js](packages/outpost/src/static.js) has allowed since 7ae59353 (2026-10-23). The way forward was `sed` over the unit, `daemon-reload`, restart. Basecamp records `outpostVersion` off each heartbeat and knows the version it installs (`OUTPOST_VERSION`, [enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts)), so it can see the gap and offer nothing. Proposal: (1) each finding carries its fix as data beside the hint — a Variables patch or an App patch — so the command can apply it; (2) a never-released App (no deployment, no port, no own Variables) is detected without a flag and opens a walkthrough: ask the Domain once, derive both URLs from it, generate ENCRYPTION_KEY unprinted, apply the mechanical patches, re-run the preflight; non-interactive prints the plan; (3) the same declared inputs (defineEnv, schema database blocks, health route, listen port) create the App right the first time, so the preflight becomes drift detection. Keep Dockerfile digest pins (a repo edit), a required credential and widgets/ out of auto-fix; the web/ static App IS created, with the Domain on it and the API's prefixes routed back (`FJS-D1547`, overriding this row's first draft). | <a id="fjs-3125"></a>FJS-3125 | litestone | **A model with a @seals move has no word for 'frozen from CREATE', so an exam attempt's identity columns (userId, quizId, startedAt) stay rewritable by the sitter for the whole sitting — @immutable there means frozen at the seal** | open | 2026-10-16 | Measured by the moodle stressor (#10) Phase 2.1, measure/attempt-data.mjs § what a student can still rewrite on an OPEN attempt, createTestEnv over the real schema. Attempt declares userId/quizId/courseId/startedAt @immutable and a @seals move (submit); a student's own client then accepts update({ data: { quizId: <another quiz> } }), { startedAt: <yesterday> } and { userId: <another student> } on the open attempt (all three REWRITTEN, read back by asSystem). The row policy admits the write (userId == auth().id && status == 'inProgress'), the seal has not happened, and FJS-D167 priced exactly this: '@immutable changes meaning on a sealing model'. What it leaves is a draft whose ownership and quiz the sitter can move: quizId defeats the per-quiz maxAttempts count, userId hands a sitting to a classmate (or onto their unique slot). FJS-D568 added the LATER freeze (@immutable(after: move)); the EARLIER one has no spelling, and a row policy cannot compare old with new, so the app cannot write it either. Expected: a word (or an argument to @immutable) for 'frozen from create' that a sealing model can carry beside the seal-scoped one · [DECISIONS.md FJS-D167](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d167) · [FJS-D568](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d568) · [measure/attempt-data.mjs](../fjs-prototypes/moodle/measure/attempt-data.mjs) · blocked by `FJS-D1280` |
| <a id="fjs-3124"></a>FJS-3124 | junction · client | **A client subscription under a service's FILENAME spelling is deaf: `client.service('log-chunks').on('created')` receives nothing while `.find()` under the same name works, because FJS-D159's alias covers calls and an event is keyed by the name the client subscribed under** | open | 2026-10-16 | Found by the ci-runner stressor (Phase 2, a live log). `api/src/services/log-chunks.service.ts` derives the service `logChunks` (FJS-D159); `/api/log-chunks` and `/api/logChunks` both answer 200, and `client.service('log-chunks').find()` reads rows. The server publishes the frame as `logChunks created` (measured with a wrapped `app.channels.sendGraded`: `sendGraded ["log-chunks"] logChunks created {...}`), and the client listens for `${name} created` with the name it was handed. Measured on one run that wrote 56 chunks from a job: a listener on `service('log-chunks')` received 0 frames, a listener on `service('logChunks')` received all 56. No error anywhere; the page renders the stored rows once and then never moves, which is the stale-list symptom FJS-701 exists to make visible. The `channel:` string is a third spelling (`'log-chunks'`, the channel NAME a connection joins) and is unrelated, so three kebab/camel spellings of one service sit side by side and only one of them is live. A `.mesa` resource goes through Sierra's `serviceNameFor` (camelCase) and is fine, which is why nothing in the repo's own apps shows it; an app that writes `client.service` by hand against its file names is the one that does. Two answers: the client canonicalizes through the manifest's service names before it keys an event (the alias then covers subscriptions as D159 says it covers calls), or `service()` on an alias refuses a subscription by name. Workaround in the app: web/src/lib/follow-log.js names `logChunks`. · blocked by `FJS-D1342` |

| <a id="fjs-3081"></a>FJS-3081 | caravan | **cancel() of a running job marks the row cancelled while the handler's process tree is alive — ctx.signal reaches the handler and nothing a handler is handed reaches a child's children, so a job that spawned sh -c leaks its grandchildren with the row saying cancelled** | open | 2026-10-16 | Measured in the ci-runner stressor (fjs-prototypes/ci-runner PLAN.md 0.3), standalone createCaravan over a scratch jobs.db, pollInterval 200, three handlers each spawning sh -c 'sleep 120 & sleep 120 & wait' (one shell, two grandchildren), then cancel(id) while running. (1) handler ignores ctx.signal: cancel() returns true, row.status is cancelled at once, abort fired in the handler after 1ms, and 500ms later the shell AND both grandchildren are alive. (2) handler does child.kill('SIGTERM') on abort: the shell dies, both grandchildren survive reparented to init — the shell is what spawn() returns and a signal to it stops at it. (3) handler spawns with detached:true and does process.kill(-pgid,'SIGTERM') on abort: the group is empty 500ms later. So the row's word is true only for a handler that spawned into its own process group and kills the group, and nothing in JobContext, caravan's docs or the CLAUDE.md says so — the hazard list stops at 'a handler that ignores it keeps running'. Phase 1 of the same stressor built the group version (api/src/core/runner.ts: detached spawn, SIGTERM -pgid, grace, SIGKILL -pgid, sweep on exit) and measured it over two API instances on one jobs.db against a 10-process tree with a SIGTERM trapper: same-instance abort after 5–6ms, cross-instance after 288–511ms (4 samples, bound pollInterval 1000ms), every tree empty by ps afterwards. What the group kill cannot reach is a child that left the group (setsid sleep 300 survived; a cgroup is the answer there). This is a ruling wearing a bug report: whether Caravan hands a handler a child-process helper (spawn in own group, kill group on abort, sweep on exit) or stays signal-only and documents that a handler that spawns owns its process group. Options are written in ci-runner/PLAN.md § Q1. · blocked by `FJS-D1372` |

Keeping the contract (the origin writes back what onconsider hands it, and onfinalize hands its own rows back on every trigger) gives the copy on drop, but mid-drag the origin drops the chip and reflows, and with the pointer outside every zone it holds a hidden gap. Ignoring the origin's onconsider keeps the chip in place, lands the drop and survives a drop back into the origin, and nothing in dnd.js promises it: the header says both callbacks write the list back. The port keeps the contract. With conduit alone the app had two choices: max_concurrent, which bounds in-flight calls and not their rate, or its own pacer — serpgrid's pace() gives each agency an even slot of its minute, in this process only, so a second process doubles the rate. | <a id="fjs-2154"></a>FJS-2154 | litestone | **No aggregate reads a member of a Json column, and metering names the member in data (BillableMetric.fieldName), so Lago's whole usage query, SUM((properties->>field)::numeric), is spellable only as asSystem().sql, under no policy** | open | 2026-10-09 | Measured 2026-10-09, lago stressor Phase 2. _sum over a Json column is refused (docs/aggregation.md, What an aggregate may name); a named aggregate takes a column name, not a path; a typed Json where (FJS-D176) needs the member declared, and here each tenant's metric declares its own. So the engine either reads the window's properties into JS and folds them (lago's usage.ts, exact in BigInt, under the caller's client) or runs raw SQL as the system. The arithmetic is not the problem: SQLite 3.53's compensated SUM over json_extract matched the exact six-place sum on every case up to 1M events, failing only past 2^53 millionths. Measured: create and createMany refuse a non-entry status as asSystem() with or without system: ['status'] — naming a transitions field lifts only a move's @system marking (FJS-D502 keeps the move), so it is no path either; walking each row's moves with update() works but rewrites @updatedAt on every hop, so a moved row loses its history · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/sstime/PLAN.md) · Seen again in maidtech (Phase 1, Contact): `system.contact.create({ data: { kind: 'customer' } })` → TransitionViolationError, and the move owes 2,519 production customers. Walking them by the declared move (what a factory does) is worse here than losing @updatedAt: `convert` is the move the reference's `listeners.leads_converted` fired on, so once Q4's actions listen for it, moving a customer converted in 2019 announces, audits and acts on a conversion today, once per row · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/maidtech/PLAN.md) · **Measured in maidtech's move, 2026-10-10 (account 25, 4,396 contacts in 2.8 s)**: D502's own path was taken, not the walk: every row created at `lead` by `createMany` as the system (every validator run), then the 148 clients placed by one `sys.sql` UPDATE of `kind` alone, `RETURNING id` for the count. What it cost here: nothing a validator would have caught (the statement writes one closed value), `updatedAt` kept, no announcement — and no audit entry for the placement either: the trail holds five batch creates and nothing says 148 of those rows are customers. So this third caller does not NEED D502's B (`lift: ['transitions']`); it would buy the placement an audit row. Action history (29,053 reference rows, all terminal) cannot be created as succeeded/failed/cancelled — TransitionViolationError even through asSystem().createMany — so the move creates at the initial state and places by raw UPDATE, D502's path. But the initial state is the one @@commitment(start, on: executeAt) owes a move from, and every history row's executeAt is in the past: between the create and the placement each row is a scheduled push the commitment sweep would START, re-sending a years-old lead to a live CRM. The move closes it by creating and placing each 1,000-row batch inside one sys.$transaction; nothing in the schema, the move tooling or fli check says the window exists. A placement that arrives with the create (D502's B) is what makes history moves safe on a committed model · [actions.ts](../fjs-prototypes/maidtech/db/move/actions.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D1066` · etsy stressor (#11), 0.4 measured the cost of the only legal path: db/seed-market.ts creates 10,000 listings at the entry (draft) and walks each to active with `transition(id, 'publish')`, 200 per shop inside one $transaction — 34.2 s, 3.42 ms per move, two thirds of a 51 s seed whose createMany half took 17 s. SEED_PUBLISH=sql takes the FJS-D502 bypass (`asSystem().sql` UPDATE) instead. A seed is the second caller after a migration that needs to land rows mid-machine. |

| <a id="fjs-1955"></a>FJS-1955 | junction | **bearerClaim has no socket half: a header() bearer's broadcasts are graded with no claim unless the app hand-writes channels({ claims }) repeating bearerClaim's lookup and liveness, the token then rides the upgrade URL that FJS-D486 moved the session out of, and a revoked grant's open socket has no disconnectSession** | open | 2026-10-10 | Measured 2026-10-08, chatwoot stressor Phase 2: a chat widget on the host's origin, so the grant is a header (no cookie crosses, FJS-1503's cookie() form is out). A service call over the socket works: bearerClaim reads the per-frame meta.headers its describe() admits. A broadcast has no frame, the visitor's connection is principal null, and every 'messages' frame to them was refused until the app wrote channels({ claims }) re-reading the grant by digest on every frame (that per-frame read is what makes a merge's re-pointed grant reach the open socket on its next frame, pinned). The token for it has to be on the upgrade: a browser sets no header there, and the subprotocol slot D486 gave the session is read by extractToken as a session, so the visitor's rides /ws?visitor=<token>. Revoking a grant (an agent's merge) left its socket open and silent until the app walked app.channels.connections for a digest it stashed at connection; disconnectSession matches conn.user.sessionId and a bearer's conn.user is null. | <a id="fjs-1810"></a>FJS-1810 | litestone | **A view can declare neither a key nor a relation, so a UNION view — the natural spelling of one ordered read over two models — can be neither keyset-paged nor guarded by its parent's policy** | open | 2026-10-06 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 2 (PLAN Q11): a thread is Message + Call in one time order, paged by cursor. `view TimelineItem { … @@sql("… FROM message UNION ALL … FROM call") }` parses, but (1) a view field takes no attribute (`id String @unique` is 'Expected IDENT, got @'), so the view has no key, and findManyCursor refuses `orderBy [{at:desc},{id:desc}]` as 'not a total order … declares no unique column'. Offset is the only paging left, and a row arriving between two pages repeats the first page's last row (pinned, then deleted with the design). (2) a view cannot declare `conversation Conversation @relation(…)`, so its read cannot be `check(conversation)` — the rule every row in a thread delegates to. It was gated 8 and the service re-proved the thread on the caller's client before an asSystem() read: the policy restated in code. Both other spellings had a cost the view would not: a header table with @@arc (every write path owes a header row in a transaction, and a missed one is a row missing from the thread with nothing saying so) and two keyset scans merged in the service (~40 lines and a two-part cursor, minted with table.cursorFor, which is not in index.d.ts). | <a id="fjs-1806"></a>FJS-1806 | conduit · caravan | **A webhook naming a subject that does not exist yet can only be parked by the app, and nothing ends a park that will never resolve: a send whose response was lost leaves its receipts parked forever, and the text marked failed while the carrier delivered it** | open | 2026-10-15 | Measured 2026-10-06 in the quo stressor, Phase 1 (PLAN Q3). A fixed parser will not reach these rows by itself: capture.ts:71 counts words != null as having a copy, so a link check never recaptures a 0-word page; staleParses (reparse from snapshot) would, if PARSER is bumped. | <a id="fjs-1466"></a>FJS-1466 | litestone · junction | **`@@fts` has no way to say *match without the marks*. None of its tokenizers folds Greek accents or Hebrew points, and `tokenize:` takes a name and nothing else. So the fold is app code on both sides, a loader that writes a shadow column and a service that folds the query, and the declared `$search` directive, which does neither, answers `βίβλος` as typed with 0 rows.** | open | 2026-09-28 | **Measured** 2026-09-28, *remnant, the legacy-port stressor*, Q3, on the real corpus (31,100 verses, 443,808 words). **Every existing route, one at a time:** (1) the four tokenizers: 0 of 6 probe words (`γενεσεως βιβλος Ιησου בראשית ברא אלהים`) against the text as written. Under the default `unicode61`, Hebrew points are separators, so `בְּרֵאשִׁ֖ית` indexes as the fragments `ב`, `אש`, `ית`. (2) A tokenizer argument: `tokenize: "unicode61 remove_diacritics 2"` is refused at parse (`Expected IDENT`), and it would not help: over NFD text in plain FTS5 it folds 2 of 6, because FTS5's diacritic table stops at U+0331, so the perispomeni (U+0342), the iota subscript (U+0345) and every Hebrew point and accent (U+0591–U+05C7) survive. (3) **The nearest declaration that exists**: `function fold(s: String): String { @@expr("replace(…)") }` plus `textFolded String @fold(text)`. It takes 199 nested `replace()`s (the 147 precomposed letters and 52 marks this corpus holds, 3.7 KB of SQL), and it agrees with the JS fold on all 443,808 words and hits 6 of 6. But it writes in 24.1 s against 8.8 s for a JS-folded column (9.0 s for `@@fts` over the raw text), it is keyed to this dataset's characters (a data release bringing one more is silently unfolded), and the query side still needs a JS twin: a schema function is inlined into DDL, not a callable SQL function, and bun:sqlite registers none. **What remnant shipped** ([fold.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/core/fold.ts): NFD, strip `\p{M}`, drop transliteration's syllable dots): five shadow columns written by the loader, and the query folded in [search.service.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/src/services/search.service.ts). Over HTTP every probe and all four of Q3's queries (`G0976`, `βίβλος`, `biblos`, `record`) find Matthew 1:1, median 1–8 ms. **What it costs:** nothing ties the shadow to its source, so a write of `text` that forgets `textFolded` leaves the index answering the old text. Adding it to a loaded corpus took a 46 s refold through the client, because SQL cannot compute the fold, while the same fold at load time is free. And **every other entrance misses**: `GET /api/verses?$search=βίβλος` → 0 rows, `biblos` → 0, and only a pre-folded `βιβλος` → Matthew 1:1. **And it is not only accents**: the corpus writes ί as U+1F77 in all 2,138 lemmas that carry it, while a Greek keyboard types U+03AF, which is canonically the same letter in different bytes. So an unnormalized index misses even a correctly accented query. **Candidate**: a word on the index, `@@fts([text], fold: marks)` (NFD, strip marks), with litestone keeping the shadow on every client write, as it already stamps `@default(ulid())` in JS, and folding the query inside `search()`. Then `$search`, a Resource's search and a console all agree. Measured for Greek and Hebrew only; by FTS5's own table the same holds for any mark above U+0331 (read, not measured) · [remnant PLAN.md Q3](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/PLAN.md) · [remnant search.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/remnant/api/test/search.test.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D1049` |

C is neither: its frames leave the pipeline after the first. | <a id="fjs-1246"></a>FJS-1246 | example | **`example`'s payroll has no input shape for a MEASURED hour: `annualGross` is `rate * hoursPerWeek * 52` and `hoursPerWeek` is an `Int` contracted constant, so a workforce product's whole output can reach a payslip only by being averaged back into the contract it was measured to replace.** | open | 2026-09-22 | **Graded, not ported** — PLAN.md § Q6 in the connectteam stressor, against the shapes `example/api/src/domain/payroll/` demands and with nothing copied into that tree. An approved timesheet (`@@transitions`, `@seals`, lines carrying `onDate`, `kind`, `minutes` and a `rateSnapshot` frozen at the rate in force on the day) was converted into `PayWindowRow` and run through transcribed copies of `annualGross` and `periodShare`. **It goes, and everything that makes it a measurement is lost on the way.** Spring fortnight: 420 measured minutes, **€84.00 actually earned**, → `{ basis: 'hourly', rate: 1200, hoursPerWeek: 4 }` → `annualGross` €2496.00 → `periodShare(·, 26)` **€96.00 — out by €12.00**. Autumn fortnight: 540 minutes, €108.00 earned → `hoursPerWeek: 5` → €3120.00 → **€120.00, out by €12.00**. **Four refusals, and only the fourth is one a type checker catches.** (1) `hoursPerWeek` is an `Int @gte(0) @lte(168)`, so a fortnight holding one 7-hour night is **3.5 h/week and is stored as 4** — the rounding alone is the whole €12.00. (2) `PayWindow` holds ONE `rate` for a window while a timesheet holds a `rateSnapshot` per LINE, so a raise mid-period is a fact about a day at one end and a fact about a window at the other, and neither shape can express the other without losing the split. (3) `PayComponentKind.overtime` is in `EARNING_KINDS` and `draftPayslip` emits exactly one earning line, always `basicPay`; `overtime` is reachable only through `arrears.ts`, which is a CORRECTION to a previous period — so a timesheet that HAD overtime lines would have nowhere to put them. The record's own observation (*an enum value nothing in the application produces*) measured from the other side, and the timesheet builder cannot produce one either, because overtime is a rule about a WEEK and no row in either schema holds one. (4) `employeeId: number` against a uuid. **The loop is therefore not closable at this joint**, and the reason is a modelling one rather than a bug: `example` prices a CONTRACT and a workforce product establishes a MEASUREMENT, and the only bridge available re-derives the contract from the measurement and then pays the contract. Whether the fix is a third basis (`measured`, whose lines ARE the gross), or a `Payslip` that can be drafted from lines rather than from a window, is the open question. Nothing here is a defect in what `example` ships — it is the input shape being the one thing that was taken on faith · [employment.ts § annualGross](example/api/src/domain/payroll/employment.ts) · [payslip.ts § draftPayslip](example/api/src/domain/payroll/payslip.ts) · [handoff.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/src/domain/payroll/handoff.ts) · [hours.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/test/hours.test.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D1344` |

Opportunistic, each sub-µs to a few ms: R10 policy SQL recompiled per query, R12 per-call allocations, P8 a new $schema per client so junction's JSON-schema cache misses per tenant, P11 validate()'s find-by-name inside per-field loops, P12 DDL regenerated per tenant create and per boot hash, B6 clearBindings with no params, B7 the JSONL cold load (uncapped heap, no early stop at limit), B8 the LRU re-link per hit, S5 Studio inlining each row's JSON twice, S10 /api/refs as 17 synchronous git calls. **The method gap is unchanged**, and the audit found one more piece of it: the trail's counter and @sequence statements bypass $tapQuery, so statement counts under-report by design, and a tap on the raw handle would make the bench honest | open | 2026-10-09 | [litestone bench/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md](packages/litestone/bench/PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md) § Re-verified |

| <a id="fjs-3595"></a>FJS-3595 | basecamp | **The `ntfy` blueprint's `NTFY_AUTH_TOKENS` hint leaves out that the token's user must be a row of `NTFY_AUTH_USERS`, and that `tk_` takes exactly 29 characters — either miss restart-loops the container with the reason only in `docker logs`** | open | 2026-10-29 | Found running the blueprint's arguments by hand on `thod` (`IDEAS/phone-ping.md` 0.1): `basecamp:tk_…:label` with no `basecamp` user loops on *user basecamp is not provisioned*; a 28-character token loops on *token invalid*. The hint reads `name:tk_<29 letters or digits>:label` and `NTFY_AUTH_USERS` says `name:hash:admin`, so a sender who is not an admin has no example. Fix: the tokens hint says *name must also be a user above, role `user`*, and `db/blueprints.js` notes carry the two failures; a `generate` for a valid `tk_` is the second half (`ntfy token generate` is the reference). |

| <a id="fjs-3126"></a>FJS-3126 | litestone | **A sealed child refuses every writer, asSystem() included, so a graded exam cannot store its verdict beside the answer: AttemptAnswer.earnedPoints can never be written, and the per-answer grade needs a model outside the sealed set** | open | 2026-10-16 | Measured by the moodle stressor (#10) Phase 2.1: Attempt.answers is @sealed and submit is @seals; the grade is written after the seal (submitted -> graded). sys.attemptAnswer.update({ data: { response } }) and { data: { earnedPoints: 100 } } both throw SealedDocumentError 'Cannot update an AttemptAnswer of Attempt … it is at submitted'; the attempt's own score/status/gradedAt write is fine. FJS-D167 says asSystem() does not lift a seal on purpose, so this is the designed edge, but the shape it makes is a want without a word: the thing that grades a document must annotate its parts, and 'sealed' covers the annotation along with the content. Spellings today: drop the column (what moodle does: the grade is Attempt.score alone), or an AnswerGrade model outside the sealed set. Neither shows the student which answer lost points without a second table. The candidate is a column marked as the verdict of the seal (writable at a named later move, as @immutable(after:) is for an immutable one) · [measure/attempt-data.mjs § the seal](../fjs-prototypes/moodle/measure/attempt-data.mjs) · [FJS-D167](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d167) · [FJS-D568](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d568) · blocked by `FJS-D1274` |
| <a id="fjs-3108"></a>FJS-3108 | caravan | **A wildcard cron's minutes missed while the process was down leave no record anywhere: the restarted scheduler fires the current minute only, jobs.db has no row for the missed one, and the app's Poller nextPollAt keeps pointing at it** | open | 2026-10-16 | statuspage PLAN 1.4 (2026-10-16, probe * * * * *, PORT=7250): API SIGTERMed at 17:34:50, back at 17:36:25 — Check has rows for 17:34 and 17:36 and none for 17:35; jobs.db has cron:probe:29869534 and :29869536 and no :29869535; Monitor.nextPollAt said 17:35 was due until the 17:36 probe overwrote it. Nothing says the fire was owed and not made: the gap is a FACT (no probe ran) that only a reader diffing scheduledFor can see, and the uptime view silently counts the minute as neither up nor down. Correct per FJS-D144 (a wildcard follows the wall clock and a past minute cannot be probed), so the ask is reporting, not replay: on start, a scheduler that can see the last fire it made (jobs.db holds it) could announce or record the minutes between it and now — a missed-fire hook, or a row with a status the app can map to a 'no check' Check. Measured beside it: a restart that stays under a minute (SIGTERM :50, back :15) loses nothing — the first tick fires the current minute, row lands with lag 15.7 s. · blocked by `FJS-D1327` |
| <a id="fjs-3085"></a>FJS-3085 | sierra | **A static build emits sitemap.xml, robots.txt and llms.txt and has no feed step, so a blog's RSS is an app closeBundle plugin that re-reads the rows the index page just read** | open | 2026-10-16 | ghost site/ (2026-10-16): the index, the tag pages and the sitemap come out of the build from the routes, and feed.xml does not — postbuild/ has sitemap, robots, llms, markdown-pages, redirects, 404 and manifest and nothing that writes an RSS or Atom document. The app wrote site/config/feed-plugin.js, a plugin whose closeBundle runs the same publishedPosts() read the index .meta.js ran a moment earlier and serializes twenty items by hand (XML escaping, pubDate, guid). Two consequences: a second reader of the rows is a second place the published set is decided, and the feed is outside the static-safety tap — a load() that reads a gated model into the feed is not refused, since no route declares it. Expected: a feed:[{ route or query, count, path }] config key in the sitemap's position, or a .meta.js export the build serializes as RSS, so the feed is one of the pages a row feeds (FJS-3034) and under the same gate check. · Second sighting, ghost 2.4: _redirects from the Slug history is the same shape — an app read of the database outside the static-safety tap — and it cannot be a sierra.config plugins: closeBundle like the feed, because those run at step 12, after Sierra's step 3 has written dist/_redirects; it is a Vite generateBundle plugin emitting the asset as site-kit's siteRedirects does (site/config/redirects-plugin.js). | <a id="fjs-1567"></a>FJS-1567 | portal | **Portal, the search stressor: page capture does not follow a <meta http-equiv=refresh> redirect, and a capture that reads 0 words is still status ok, so a redirect stub or a client-rendered page is kept as an empty copy with nothing to say so** | open | 2026-10-20 | Found seeding the knight persona (2026-09-29). https://www.conventionalcommits.org/ answers 200 with a 208-byte page whose only content is a meta refresh to /en/; https://www.inkandswitch.com/local-first/ answers 200 with 152 bytes refreshing to /essay/local-first. [fetch.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/fetch.ts) follows HTTP redirects only (redirect: 'manual', each hop re-checked by the SSRF guard), and nothing in api/src/domain/pages reads a refresh. Both were kept as status ok, words 0, finalUrl the stub. A client-rendered page does the same: https://chromedevtools.github.io/devtools-protocol/ (content in a <template>, filled by script) is ok with 0 words; not a parser defect, but indistinguishable from one on the page. [capture.ts](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/pages/capture.ts) writes status ok for any parse, and capture.ts:71 (hasCopy = words != null) then treats the empty copy as kept, so a link check never retries it. Fix: follow a same-document meta refresh (delay 0, http(s) only) as one more guarded hop; and write a 0-word parse as failed (for example 'the page had no readable text'), keeping link live, so the page shows the capture failed and offers Capture again. Seen with FJS-1566, whose empty copies are the same symptom from a different cause · Not reproduced 2026-10-20: the portal source is absent from this machine · blocked by `FJS-3363` |

Today the nearest thing is accidental: `fli dev` prefixes come from bun run --parallel ([new.md:265](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md#L265)), which colors by script ORDER, so dev:web is yellow in one app and cyan in the next. **Where it would help:** (1) fli dev log prefixes and its banner (slot · web 8003 · API 8103), each surface and port in its color; (2) every realm-tagged log line -- litestone, caravan, junction $.log -- so a scrolling terminal sorts itself; (3) fli ci phases, fli proves / prove, fli check findings, fli next and fli ws:atlas, tagged by realm or surface; (4) fli new and fli make:* output naming what they created; (5) a FrameworkError's terminal print naming the realm it was thrown in; (6) the browser tab in dev -- a tinted favicon per surface so web and site tabs differ, the Sierra error overlay border, the dev banner; (7) Litestone Studio chrome in the Data color, Junction devtools in API, each registerDevService entry in its own; (8) the VS Code extension -- file icons for .lite (Data) and .mesa (UI), folder colors for api/ web/ site/ db/, the status bar; in @frontierjs/css it is a tone for DEV chrome only and never reaches a shipped app's styling (Invariant 13); color is never the only signal -- a label or glyph goes with it, NO_COLOR and 16-color terminals degrade cleanly, and the palette is checked for color-blind separation; "surface" already means a background layer in @frontierjs/css (surface.css), so the naming must not add a third sense. **Open questions (decision-rules):** realm as hue family and surface as a shade inside it (web, site, widgets, extension and desktop are all UI) or a distinct hue per surface; | <a id="fjs-1498"></a>FJS-1498 | litestone | **A counter keyed by a unique pair has no one-statement spelling: `upsert` refuses `{ increment }` in its update half (FJS-D27 put the operators on `update`/`updateMany` only), so `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET n = n + 1`, the write every aggregate table is made of, is three statements and a caught conflict in app code.** | open | 2026-09-29 | **Measured** 2026-09-29, *jazzhr stressor* Phase 2: spelling A of Q1 is an `IntakeCount { source, quarter, count }` bumped on every application. `upsert({ where: { source, quarter }, create: { …, count: 1 }, update: { count: { increment: 1 } } })` is refused: *"increment" changes a value that is already there, so it belongs on update, not upsert*. The refusal is deliberate (the method's fast path reads `excluded`, the slow path calls `update()`, and only one could apply an operator), and the message names no way out. What the app wrote instead ([pipeline.ts `bumpIntake`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts)): `updateMany` with the operator; if the count is 0, `create`; if that throws on the unique pair, `updateMany` again. Correct under concurrency only because of the retry, and three round trips for one fact. Also met on the way, and a GOOD refusal: an operator on a column with `@gte` is refused with *state the bound as `@check`*. Candidate: the fast path emits `SET "count" = "count" + excluded…` for an operator, and the slow path is refused for an operator instead of the whole method, or the message names the three-statement shape · [FJS-D27](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d27) · [pipeline.ts](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/api/src/lib/pipeline.ts) · blocked by `FJS-D1111` |

The miss is silent and fails open: the `@@gate` is not found and the model is served to anyone.* Junction's AGENTS.md says the opposite in as many words — *`order-lines.service.ts` becomes the service `orderLines` over `OrderLine`* — and `example/` ships ten hyphenated services. **Measured**, connectteam Phase 5, with `model:` deliberately absent: `kiosk-devices.service.ts` served `/api/kioskDevices`, and `KioskDevice @@gate("6.6.6.6")` answered **403 to an ADMINISTRATOR(5)** and 401 to a stranger — the gate was found and enforced, so *fails open* is not what happens. The advice is still right (`example` states `model:` on every one, and this app now does too), but a rule whose message describes a hole that is not there costs the reader a measurement to find out, and this is the rule most likely to be met on the first day of a new surface · [checks.js § service-model](packages/cli/core/checks.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1234"></a>FJS-1234 | litestone · caravan | **A predicate that depends on a ZONE held in a column cannot be a `where`, so deferred work over per-row wall-clock times is a full scan of the window: every candidate row is read and all but a few are discarded in application code.** | open | 2026-10-20 | Found building reminders on the calendly stressor (PLAN.md § Q9). The product rule is *08:00 on the morning of, in the INVITEE's zone* — the one people actually want, as against *24 hours before*, which is easy precisely because a duration needs no zone. **The query that should exist cannot be written.** `booking.inviteeTimeZone` is a column, so *has 08:00 passed for this row* is a calendar calculation per row against a different zone per row. `where` has no zone-aware operator and no index can serve one, so the sweep bounds what it can — `startsAt` between now and now+48h, an ordinary instant comparison — and asks the real question in JavaScript. **Measured on a small database: `considered 60, sent 2`.** The ratio is set by the horizon and the spread of zones, not by how many reminders are due, so it does not improve with scale — it is 2 sends from 60 reads today and 2 from 60,000 on a real calendar. **Re-measured at workforce scale on connectteam 2026-09-22, and the claim holds with one correction.** 4,000 published shifts over a fortnight, 600 people, 8 sites, a 48-hour horizon, a five-minute cron. ONE fire at 07:00Z — 08:00 where half the workforce is, the most productive instant of that day — **considered 566, sent 96**, discarding **264 pending + 206 tooLate** on the predicate that cannot be lowered and **0 past** on the one that can, in **773 ms**. Over a whole day the same window is re-read 288 times for the same 173 reminders the employer owes: **165,744 rows read, 173 sent — 1 send per 958 rows**, and **~180 seconds of CPU a day** to do it (24 fires measured at 621 ms each across the block holding both zones' 08:00; the read total is arithmetic on the measured per-fire figure, the sends are measured). **The correction is which of the two terms matters.** Moving the same 600 people from 2 zones (the brief's: Lisbon and the Azores, ONE hour apart) to 12 spread around the world left `considered` **identical at 566** and moved the sends only from 96 to 81 — spreading people over more zones spreads their 08:00s over the clock rather than changing how many there are. **So the cost is set by the horizon alone, and the spread is second order**: an employer with one hour of spread pays a global calendar's price. That removes the one escape a reader might infer from this row — that a geographically tight product is cheap. **And the cheap fix this row floats does not reach this case.** A `@generated` column holding the reminder instant fails here for a stronger reason than *cannot do arithmetic*: the zone is `User.timeZone`, which is **two relations** from `Shift` (`Shift → Employee → User`), and *which* morning is itself undecided between `Shift.onDate` (the day the manager typed, in the SITE's zone) and `startsAt` read in the person's — two columns the schema does not relate. Measured: the same shift is `tooLate` for a Lisbon employee and `pending` for an Azorean one, with reminder instants a DAY apart. **`tooLate` is a second consequence worth naming**, at 206 of 566 rows in one fire: a rule stated as a wall clock has shifts it can never remind at all — every shift starting before 08:00 local, which in a field workforce is the early turn. A rule stated as a duration has no such case, which is precisely why building that one would have measured nothing. · driven in connectteam `api/test/reminders.test.ts` § 1, § 6 **This is [FJS-1214](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1214)'s gap in a second place and the consequence is different enough to be its own row.** 1214 is about DECLARATION — a recurring wall-clock window cannot be spelled in `.lite`. This is about QUERY: the same absence of zone awareness means a zoned predicate cannot be pushed down, and the cost lands on background work rather than on a form. The arithmetic exists and is correct — `fromWall`/`partsIn` in toolbelt answered every DST case in `api/test/reminders.test.ts` first time, including a Tokyo booking whose reminder falls on the previous UTC date — so what is missing is not the maths but anywhere to put it that the database can see. **The cron half is not a defect and is worth stating so the row is not read as one.** `handle(…, { cron, timeZone })` binds ONE zone per schedule (measured: `registrations()` returns `timeZone: null` for an unzoned expression), which is right — a schedule is a statement about the app, not about a row. It is *because* that is right that the work has to become a sweep, and the sweep is where the missing predicate bites. **What would close it:** a zone-aware comparison litestone can lower — the same word [FJS-1214](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1214) asks for, reused in `where` — or, far cheaper and worth considering first, a `@generated` column holding the reminder instant, which fails today for the reason `Booking.endsAt` already documents in this app's schema: a generated column cannot reach across a relation or do arithmetic on one. **What is NOT a finding, measured and cleared:** caravan's dispatch door. `delay` is a duration and the `jobs` table stores `run_at`, an instant, which looked like `FJS-D143`'s category error one layer down — it is not. The conversion is exact (measured: `run_at` landed on the target instant, off by 0 ms), and a `runAt` option does not exist rather than being silently ignored, so TypeScript refuses it at the call site; reaching it needs a deliberate `as any`. The queue contributed durability, one-fire-per-cluster and retries, and none of Q9's difficulty came from it · [caravan/src/cron.ts](packages/caravan/src/cron.ts) · [FJS-1214](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1214) · [FJS-D143](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d143) · blocked by `FJS-D908` · blocked by `FJS-D1108` · 2026-10-20: FJS-D908 was ruled A on 2026-10-13 (a zone-aware operator the client lowers), so that blocked by no longer holds; FJS-D1108, what exactly D908 means, is still owed and still holds the row · [FJS-D908](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d908) |
| <a id="fjs-1231"></a>FJS-1231 | ui | **`<Select>` given a `value` that matches none of its options renders blank and says nothing — which is indistinguishable from *nothing is chosen*, and is how a wrong handler presents itself.** | open | 2026-09-21 | Reported on the calendly stressor as *selecting a day makes the selection disappear*. **The cause was the app's**, and this row is not a complaint about the contract: `<Select>` and `<Input>` wire `on:change={onchange}` / `oninput?.(e)` straight to the DOM and their headers say *Native event callbacks*, so a handler written as `(v) => Number(v)` receives an Event, `Number(Event)` is `NaN`, and the control then holds a value no `<option>` has. The browser renders that as an empty box. **What is missing is the sentence.** The component knows, at render time, that it was handed a non-null `value` matching none of its normalized options — it has already built `flatOptions` — and it renders blank instead of saying so. Nothing else catches it either: the page compiles, `fli check` is green, and there is no console output, so the only signal is a control that silently discards what you pick. **The kit already holds this exact principle** and applies it one case over. `optionsNote` exists because *an empty list and a list nobody could fetch render identically, and a person [cannot tell]* ([utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js)), and `truncationNote` exists for the same reason. A value that matches no option is the third member of that family and the only one with no note. A `console.warn` naming the component, the value and the option count would have turned a blank box into a one-line answer; a visible note is probably wrong here, since the state is usually a bug rather than something to tell an end user about. Worth pairing with [FJS-1230](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1230), which recommends the same remedy for a different silent discard, and with [FJS-1208](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1208). Three components in one kit now drop what they were given without a word, which is a house style rather than three bugs · [Select.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/Select.mesa) · [utils.js:260](packages/ui/utils.js) · [FJS-1230](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1230) |

| <a id="fjs-1178"></a>FJS-1178 | sierra | **A resource's `offlineQuery` is only registered once its MODULE is evaluated, and a route's modules are code-split — so the screen nobody has opened, which is the one the declaration exists for, declares nothing until somebody opens it.** | open | 2026-09-16 | `createResource` runs at import (Invariant 18) and `declareOffline` runs with it, so a boot that imports no resource warms nothing. `example` answers it with one line in `web/src/main.js` — importing the resource after `virtual:sierra`, which is what builds the client it is created against — and that is a real answer rather than a workaround, since WHICH resources are worth the entry chunk is a bundling decision the app owns. What is owed is the framework half being decidable at all: a glob over `src/resources/` is derivable (Invariant 19 already fixes what that directory holds) and `offline:` already exists in `sierra.config.js` for the shell, so `offline: { resources: true }` is the shape. **Two things make it more than a glob**, and they are why it is filed rather than built: `createResource` needs a client, so an eager import must be ordered AFTER `initJunction` or every resource module throws at boot; and every resource so imported lands in the entry chunk, which is exactly the number `FJS-D302` grades · [offline.js](packages/sierra/src/resource/offline.js) · [main.js](example/web/src/main.js) · blocked by `FJS-D1268` |

| <a id="fjs-1078"></a>FJS-1078 | cli | **`serviceForModel` hand-rolls Invariant 2's model name and disagrees with the owner on 7 of 12 real service names — in a function one command calls.** [`FJS-975`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-975) closed 2026-09-07 by giving `@frontierjs/toolbelt/inflect` the shape half and consolidating six copies, and it names `split(/[-_]/)` in the cli as one of them. `core/app-schema.js:302` is a survivor it did not reach, with a regex that handles no separator at all: `addresses` → `Addres`, `people` → `People`, and `journal-entries` → `Journal-entry`, which is the exact `Order-item` failure that row describes — not an identifier. `modelName` answers `Address`, `Person`, `JournalEntry`. **Two facts keep it at S4 and both belong in the row**: one command calls it, `fli admin:generate` (`commands/admin/generate.md:453`), which is corrected 2026-09-13 from *nothing calls it* — a grep over `.js` alone misses a command written as markdown, and every hyphenated service in `example` states `model:` explicitly — `product-variants.service.ts` carries a comment saying it is STATED rather than derived — so no app here reaches the wrong answer today, and `admin:generate` over a hyphenated service without `model:` would. That is also what makes it worth filing rather than fixing in passing: a latent copy of a consolidated fact is how the consolidation comes undone, and the next caller inherits it silently. The fix is one line pointing at `modelName`, since deleting it would break `admin:generate`. | open | 2026-09-12 | [app-schema.js](packages/cli/core/app-schema.js#L302) · [inflect.js](packages/toolbelt/src/inflect/inflect.js#L376) · [FJS-975](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-975) |
| <a id="fjs-1060"></a>FJS-1060 | example | **`verify` failed twice in six runs, both times on the FIRST run against a freshly started API, and at two different assertions.** Observed rather than diagnosed, which is the honest state and the reason it is filed at all. `subDetail.stopRenewing` waited out on `.popover button.danger` once, and `form.customerOptions` on `[role="option"].length > 1` once; the four runs against an already-warm API all passed 61/61. **The underlying features were then verified working by direct probe** — the customer picker offers 8 options on the same page, at the same point in the drive, signed in the same way — so what is flaky is the drive rather than the app, which is the distinction that makes it worth a row instead of a shrug. Both failures are `waitFor` timing out at its 8s default. The suspicion is that a just-started API is answering slowly enough on its first reads to outrun it, which `web/test/lib/preflight.mjs` cannot currently see: it asks reachability and FRESHNESS, and *ready to answer a health check* is not *ready to serve a relation read*. Two data points and two different symptoms is not a diagnosis, so nothing is changed on it. **It matters because it is the app's headline drive**: one failure in three on a cold server is the rate at which people learn to re-run rather than read, which is [FJS-729](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-729)'s complaint one tier up and over a browser instead of a unit suite | open | 2026-10-20 | [verify.mjs](example/web/test/verify.mjs) · [preflight.mjs](example/web/test/lib/preflight.mjs) · [FJS-729](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-729) · fix-loop 2026-10-18: cold API measured, not the cause — fresh `bun run api` answers /health at ~250ms, first login 172ms (hash), then every relation read (customers, orders, subscriptions, products) 0–7ms on the first AND second pass, so a first-read-slow API does not reproduce. The cold server left unmeasured is vite (first transform of the page graph, a dep re-optimize that reloads the page); a stray reload is now caught by `lib/reload.mjs` (watchReloads in verify.mjs, uncommitted) and names itself, so the next cold failure should say which. No Chrome here (FJS-3264), so the drive itself was not run; nothing changed · fix-loop 2026-10-20: drive RUN cold, three times, and green each time (71/71) — fresh api + web started by a script, db:seed first, once with node_modules/.vite kept, twice with it deleted (a cold dep optimize, no reload seen), the last under 2×nproc busy loops (30s against 5.5s, no waitFor timed out). Chrome IS here: FJS_CHROME=~/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-1181/chrome-linux/chrome; reload.mjs is committed and would have named a stray reload. Not reproducible, so nothing changed; the next red cold run names its own cause |
| <a id="fjs-966"></a>FJS-966 | mesa | **A CDATA section is refused, and in an `<svg>` subtree it is legal HTML that an exporter wrote.** `<![CDATA[ … ]]>` is valid in XML and, per the HTML parsing spec, inside foreign content — an `<svg>` or `<math>` subtree — where it becomes a text node. Mesa refuses it everywhere, and since [FJS-882](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-882) the refusal at least names it and says what to write instead. **The open question is whether it should be accepted in a foreign subtree**, which is where it actually arrives: an SVG pasted out of an exporter. Accepting it is not a parse tweak — it means deciding whether `{` interpolates inside one, and the honest answer is that it should NOT, which makes a CDATA section a literal-text escape hatch and therefore a language feature with a rule of its own. That may well be worth having; it is not a diagnostic. **Cost of NOT doing it is one paste and one error message**, which is why it is filed rather than built. Svelte is no help here — it has no CDATA handling at all, so `<![CDATA[` reaches `tag_invalid_name` and is reported as an invalid element name | open | 2026-09-07 | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-882](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-882) · blocked by `FJS-D1348` |

The fix is to plumb the tag's own start into `parseAttributes` and add it back; the marker machinery is already there and the emit site is one line. Left undone rather than half-done: the `srcStart` was wired first and silently produced NOTHING, because `prop.start` is undefined, which is a no-op that reads from the outside exactly like a working feature | open | 2026-09-06 | [compiler.js](packages/mesa/src/compiler.js) · [FJS-943](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-943) |

| <a id="fjs-907"></a>FJS-907 | mesa | **A derivation chain of a few thousand links overflows the native call stack in the memo pull path, after the flush budget stopped being the limit.** [FJS-851](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-851) made the budget per-node, so a chain settles at any length the flush can reach — measured, 1000 and 1200 now settle where 1000 used to be declared cyclic. At 20000 the flush itself completes and `read` → `_recompute` → `read` overflows instead, somewhere between 2000 and 5000 links. **Measured 2026-09-13 on a plain memo pull, and the ceiling is the ENGINE's**: node overflows between 2000 and 5000 links, bun holds 5000 and overflows by 20000 — so the same app has a different limit on each runtime it is documented to run on. | <a id="fjs-774"></a>FJS-774 | junction | **`ctx.locals` carries an index signature, so the typed escape from `$` catches a typo on the app object and not on the call.** [`FJS-D200`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d200) rules `$` a closed set the framework owns and points app state at `ctx.locals`, which is the right seam and is the weaker half of the pattern it names. `ServiceContextLocals` is exported, so an app types its own key by declaration merging exactly as Invariant 5 prescribes for `app.own()` — verified under `--strict`, `$.locals.branding?.logo` resolves. But the interface also carries `[key: string]: unknown`, so `$.locals.brandng` is legal and answers `unknown`, where `app.brandng` is an error because `App` has no index signature. A misspelled read is silent on the surface the ruling just made the recommended one. **Not fixable by deleting the signature**: `paginate` and `db` are written by framework hooks and app code writes ad-hoc keys today, so removing it breaks every unaugmented app. Wants the shape that keeps both — a declared key typed and an undeclared one still writable — which is a different mechanism from the one `app.own()` uses. Sits against [`FJS-D199`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d199), which rules that junction refuses an unknown AUTHORING key by name; a locals key is per-call state rather than authoring, so the two are not in conflict, but they are the same question asked of two surfaces and they answer differently | open | 2026-09-04 | [context.ts `ServiceContextLocals`](packages/junction/src/core/context.ts) · [`FJS-706`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-706) · `FJS-D200` · Invariant 5 · seam: `$` · blocked by `FJS-D1349` |
| <a id="fjs-747"></a>FJS-747 | junction | **The suite exits 1 with `0 fail` and no test named, roughly once in ten runs.** Seen three times across 2026-09-02/03, always the same shape: `bun run test` reports `1893 pass · 0 fail`, prints no `(fail)` line anywhere, and exits non-zero — so CI would go red naming nothing. **Two things this row used to say are measured wrong** (2026-09-05, bun 1.3.11). *The stated hypothesis*: an unhandled rejection escaping a test cannot be it — bun installs its own `unhandledRejection` listener, so `process.listenerCount('unhandledRejection')` is **1** inside `bun test`, junction's `listenerCount === 0` guard therefore never installs its rejection handler here at all, and an escaped rejection is attributed by bun to whatever test is running as a `(fail)`, which is the opposite of the observed shape. *The proposed instrument*: a preload registering `process.on('unhandledRejection')` **never fires** — confirmed with the preload loading (it printed) and the rejection still going to bun's own reporter. Following the row as written produces an instrument that is silent for the reason it was built. **What is true instead**: `uncaughtException` has no bun listener, so a started app DOES take that one inside a test process, and `crashStop()` turns a stray throw into an async `process.exit(1)` behind `app.stop()` — a race whose result depends on whether bun force-exits first. All 27 junction test files that `start()` also `stop()`, so nothing is left dangling. **What is ruled out and what is left**: a throw landing while a test runs is reported by bun either way (paired, with and without a started app — identical), and one landing after the last test is swallowed silently at exit 0 because bun force-exits before the timer fires. The remaining window is a throw landing BETWEEN files, where no test is in scope to attribute it to. `process.exitCode = 1` reproduces the reported shape EXACTLY (`1 pass · 0 fail`, no `(fail)` line, exit 1) and nothing in `src/` or `test/` writes it, so it is a shape match rather than the cause — a dependency doing it would look the same. **12 consecutive clean runs** on 2026-09-05, so it is still not reproducible on demand | open | 2026-09-05 | [test/](packages/junction/test) · [app.ts `crashStop`](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-3546"></a>FJS-3546 | mesa | **The bench measures the keyed table only: the signal-favoring, dbmon and effectful-list workloads `IDEAS/performance-regression-watch.md` § Mesa's slice marks *next* have no fixture** | open | 2026-10-27 | Each is a `.mesa` file and a vanilla twin running one workload from `bench/fixtures/shared/`, with the same two correctness checks before its numbers count. effectful-list is the one that exercises `destroy()` per row, the FJS-890 shape · `IDEAS/performance-regression-watch.md` § Mesa's slice · `FJS-D532` |
| <a id="fjs-3547"></a>FJS-3547 | mesa | **How many compile diagnostics carry `file:line:col` is unmeasured since the 2026-09 audit found 0 of 12** | open | 2026-10-27 | Waves 1–4 moved the error drain and raised the overlay, and none counted locations after. Probe: compile each refusal in `test/compile-errors.test.js` and `test/parser-refusals.test.js` and count the messages naming a position; file what the count says · `FJS-830` · `FJS-866` |
| <a id="fjs-3548"></a>FJS-3548 | mesa | **Nothing feeds the compiler input it did not expect: the corpus is fixed files and the mutants stub the runtime, so a differential fuzz over `.mesa` source has no runner** | open | 2026-10-27 | Shape: mutate a corpus file one token at a time, compile, and require either a diagnostic in `analysis.errors` or output acorn parses (Invariant 15) — a silent empty program is the C9 failure the audit found. Trigger: the next compile-errors regression · `FJS-888` · `scripts/corpus.mjs` · `test/mutants.mjs` |
| <a id="fjs-3549"></a>FJS-3549 | sierra | **The CSP drive runs in Chromium only, which applies a cloned template's `style=` that Firefox refuses under the same policy** | open | 2026-10-27 | A CSP claim proved in one engine is a claim about that engine. The Firefox run needs `playwright-core` and a second launcher beside `openChrome()`; until then the README sentence about CSP names Chromium · `packages/sierra/test/fixtures/widget-site/test/host-csp.html` · `FJS-D532` |
| <a id="fjs-3550"></a>FJS-3550 | mesa | **The bench leak ceiling fails open one run in three: Chrome releases ~3.5 MB of its own at a moment no `collectGarbage` controls, and a small leak hides behind it** | open | 2026-10-27 | Measured 2026-10-27 over ten runs, either fixture. A gross leak (a mount's worth per cycle) always trips the 5% ceiling; a listener-per-row leak can pass when the release lands in the same window. The number that repeats is a COUNT: live owner nodes after five cycles equal to the count after one, read from the runtime the way DOM mutations are read from a MutationObserver. Needs a runtime export the production fixture can reach · `bench/run.mjs` § the leak ceiling · `FJS-890` |

---

## Needs a decision

These are questions, not defects. A ruling moves to `DECISIONS.md` and closes the
row here. **This table is the questions with an id; most open questions are argued
inside an IDEAS paper's `## Open questions` and stay there.** `fli decisions` reads
both as one queue, and a question becomes pickable — by `fli decide` or on `fli gui`
— once its bullet carries lettered options and a `**Recommend X**` line. A row's
cell cannot hold options, so they go in the paper its Detail links.

| <a id="fjs-d1131"></a>FJS-D1131 | litestone · junction | **Is a foreign wire format (Bitwarden's Type: 2, RevisionDate, Object: "cipher" — names and values fixed by a client somebody else wrote) the plugin's alone, or may the schema point at it — @map renames the column and nothing a caller touches, @label is a human title, an enum member carries no value** | vaultwarden Q1's pre-run guess is 'a ruling, not a word' · [open-questions.md § Cannot say C8](../fjs-prototypes/vaultwarden/discovery/open-questions.md) · Measured, vaultwarden Phase 1 (bw 2026.10.0 logging in with TOTP against the real app, measure/bw-login.mjs green). The client asked for exactly nine things to log in, sync and list: POST /identity/accounts/prelogin/password, POST /identity/connect/token (form-urlencoded; grant_type password with twoFactorToken/Provider/Remember in the SAME request when a code is given, and refresh_token), GET /api/config, GET /api/sync, GET /api/accounts/revision-date, POST /api/accounts/key-management/user-key-id, plus the legacy prelogin paths. All nine are raw routes in one app plugin (api/src/bitwarden/plugin.ts); no service was touched and the model names never reach the wire. Four things the framework had to be worked around on: (1) the access token must be a JWT the client decodes (sub, email, sstamp, exp), and Junction resolves every Bearer through auth.verifySession, so an Object.create(auth) wrapper unwraps a sid claim — six lines, verifySession IS the seam, cleared; (2) the OAuth error JSON (invalid_grant + error_description, 400) is translated by the plugin in a try/catch around auth.login, a second thrown-value→HTTP translator beside toResponse (Invariant 4) with no per-route hook to hand the owner a dialect; (3) /identity/* sits outside apiPrefix and so on app.http.router, which drops the request scope (FJS-3104); (4) Junction serves no TLS and the client refuses http (FJS-3105). The pre-run guess holds as far as it goes — an app plugin over raw routes — and the ruling should say what the framework owes it: the request scope on every route it registers, and a way for a plugin to own the error translation for ITS routes; the schema points at nothing (Type: 2, RevisionDate stay the plugin's). |

## Closed

Kept so a stale document cannot reopen them. Newest first.

**Older closures live in [ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md).** This
section holds the recent ones and is trimmed past ~40 rows by `fli register:archive`;
the archive holds the rest, verbatim. An id is never reused, so a `FJS-###` named anywhere in the repo
resolves in one file or the other.

| Id | Title | Closed | How |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="fjs-3497"></a>FJS-3497 | basecamp — **The App picker labels each app by `name` alone, and the seed holds one app per environment under the same name, so a path route's App list shows identical rows and a person cannot tell which environment they picked** | 2026-10-30 | App declared no @@label, so sierra guessed `name` and the seed's one-app-per-environment rows read identically. Fixed with `@@label([name, environment.name])` (a list label with one hop already exists). The picker sends `$populate` for the hop, which the hand-written `apps.find` never read and junction refused (picker empty), so `find` now reads it and adds the named relations to its include. Proved by services.test.ts (`$populate` red with the read stubbed, green), schema.test.ts (App label parts) and verify:screens 313/313, whose DNS route check now picks by the full "name · environment" label. · [schema.lite](packages/basecamp/db/schema.lite) · [dns/index.mesa](packages/basecamp/web/src/routes/dns/index.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-3582"></a>FJS-3582 | litestone — **`{ not: 'a' }` compiles to `!= ?` and drops the null rows, while `{ notIn: ['a'] }` keeps them** | 2026-10-30 | Scalar `not` compiled to a bare `col != ?` and the matcher read it as false on a null row, while `notIn`, the array form `not: [..]` and the JSONL driver kept null rows. Fixed at the owners: litestone `query.js` column `not` and the typed-Json/extensible leaf `not` now write `(col != ? OR col IS NULL)` (a typed-Json `not: [..]` was bound as one placeholder and is now membership), toolbelt `match.js` answers true on a null, and the JSONL driver reads `not: null` the right way round (it was inverted) and `not: [..]` as membership. Proved by `in-list.test.ts` § a null member is the IS NULL test (`not` scalar, empty string, array, null across column, typed Json, jsonl and matcher), plus the updated match spec and extensible count. Aggregate HAVING `not` left as SQL. |
| <a id="fjs-3607"></a>FJS-3607 | ui — **Drawer has a fixed width and no header slot: no `resizable`, bindable `width`, `minWidth` or `action` snippet** | 2026-10-30 | Per FJS-D1654: Drawer takes resizable, bindable width (px), minWidth and an action header snippet; the handle is .drawer-handle in drawers.css, a focusable role=separator with aria-valuenow/min/max, a 32px arrow nudge and drag() with snap-back on pointercancel; left and right drawers only. Separator role in toolbelt/aria, part in css vocabulary.js, inset ring in focus.css. ui drive: Drawer 27/27 (drag both sides, arrow, floor, bind:width, aria), ARIA kit 12/12; ui 2304, toolbelt 989, css 627 green (shadow red is c6b02687 avatar --data-accent, not this). · [Drawer.mesa](packages/ui/components/overlay/Drawer.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-3617"></a>FJS-3617 | ui · css — **Every app writes its own icon component; tpm has three, so the kit documents one Icon per app and widens `icon.css` past `i-heroicons`** | 2026-10-30 | Per FJS-D1655: icon.css sizes any i-* class (Lucide, Tabler) not only i-heroicons, in the selector, its header, feedback.css, css docs/conventions.md and the guide; packages/ui/AGENTS.md gains § Icons with the one app Icon.mesa (compiled, output parses). css core-gaps 41/41 with an i-lucide case; ui agents-md green. · [IDEAS/branded-icons.md](IDEAS/branded-icons.md) · [icon.css](packages/css/src/components/icon.css) |
| <a id="fjs-3218"></a>FJS-3218 | junction — **chatwoot widget.test.ts pins the pre-FJS-1742 frames (a stranger told 'removed <id>'), so it fails or asserts the oracle until moved to expect no frame** | 2026-10-30 | widget.test.ts's FJS-1742 case now expects no frame to the stranger: it waits for the reply's sent-update to land, then asserts no 'messages removed' (nor any frame carrying the id or content) reaches the other-org visitor, as quo's ring.test.ts already does. The string mode had not shipped as a default. |

| <a id="fjs-369"></a>FJS-369 | junction — **There is no service-level audit trail, so an app that wants one re-derives junction's own announce rule.** `@@log(audit)` records a WRITE and `db.$audit()` records an EVENT; neither answers *what did this CALL do*, which is the question an operator asks — and the verbs that matter are custom methods (drain, deploy, cancel, trigger), which no write-level trail names. basecamp's answer is **220 lines** in `core/hooks.ts`: a `before: { all }` pre-image hook and an `after: { all }` writer, sharing one `recordable()` that **restates junction's own dispatch predicate** — everything except `find`/`get`, opting out on `ctx.dispatch === false`. Restating a rule the framework owns is the tell: `callService` is the single announcement point and it already decides this. What basecamp got right is what a framework version needs and what it had to reach for by hand — the model off `app.services` (the REGISTRY, not `app.service(name)`, which carries no `model` and produced a diff-less row for every event until it was found), `db.$protectedFields(accessor)` for redaction rather than a list of names, both sides re-read through the system client because a service may answer a projection and a scoped read strips protected columns. A plugin owning this would be small, and every app that needs a trail today writes the 220 lines or ships without one | 2026-10-29 | junction's `auditPlugin({ model, except, columns })` at `@frontierjs/junction/audit` (`FJS-D873`): the write predicate is `isReadMethod` plus `ctx.dispatch === false`, junction's own; the pre-image, the system re-read of both sides, `$protectedFields` redaction and the registry-read model moved out of basecamp `core/hooks.ts` (−222 lines); the model's column contract is checked at boot and a required column nothing writes is refused by name. Basecamp configures it with `columns.workspaceId`. Also fixes API-key calls filed as `actorType: user` (basecamp compared `'api_key'`, junction stamps `'apiKey'`). Proved by `packages/junction/test/audit-plugin.test.ts` (15, real client) · junction 3358/0 · basecamp 1101/0 · [basecamp core/hooks.ts § The application audit trail](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/hooks.ts) · [FJS-154](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-154) · [core/service.ts](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-3598"></a>FJS-3598 | notifications — **The ntfy driver sent what ntfy cannot carry: an em dash in a title never left (`fetch` refuses a header byte above Latin-1), an accent arrived as garbage, a body of 4 KB or more became a `.txt` attachment or a refusal, and an action label with a comma or quote mis-parsed** | 2026-10-29 | Found by the first live phone ping (`IDEAS/phone-ping.md` Phase 2, title *Test ping — Skunkworks*). In [ntfy.ts](packages/notifications/drivers/ntfy.ts): `headerValue` RFC 2047-encodes any non-ASCII Title, Tags, Click and Actions value (ntfy runs `maybeDecodeHeader` on every header); `fitBody` cuts a body to 4095 bytes on a character boundary with an ellipsis (ntfy's `Peek` makes 4096 an attachment); Actions go as a JSON array, not the comma-and-semicolon shorthand, capped at ntfy's three. Proved live on a throwaway ntfy.sh topic read back by poll: title and link decoded, 5 KB body arrived as 4095-byte text with no attachment, label `Open, "now"; please` intact, fourth action dropped. Notifications 126/126, typecheck clean. No test row: the driver has none yet and the plan is still experimental. |

| <a id="fjs-3569"></a>FJS-3569 | caravan — **A dispatch by NAME before the handler registers landed on `default`, and a row parked on a queue no worker claims for that name waited in silence** | 2026-10-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D1635`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1635). `insertJob` remembers the id of a string dispatch with no handler and no stated queue until the job files have loaded; `registerHandler` moves each to the handler's queue, attempts and retry delay (`rehome` in `src/db.ts`). What nothing moves is counted: `stats().queues.<q>.stranded`, pending rows whose name this process serves on no handler for that queue, so `/metrics` carries it. | <a id="fjs-3593"></a>FJS-3593 | junction — **A service request carried two correlation ids: the transport opened the request scope around the handler and `crudHandler` opened a second one, minting another id** | 2026-10-29 | `_execute` wrapped every route in `enterRequest`, then `crudHandler` called `enterRequest` again with no `requestId`, so a second `mintCorrelationId()` ran and an audit row written from a raw route never matched one written by a service in the same request. Found by the junction perf audit: two `randomUUID` per `GET /items/:id`. `crudHandler` now re-enters the open scope as the bridge's frozen principal (`reenterAs`) and opens its own only when none exists; one id per request, measured. Same change: a Response junction built is finalized in place (`own`/`isOwned` in `bridge.ts`), helmet stamps from the transport with no middleware, `protectedFieldsFor`/`primaryKeyOf` cache per client, litestone's statement cache skips the regex and the LRU reorder on a hit. `bench-ab --only junction`: geomean 0.80x, get-1 0.67x. |
