| <a id="fjs-d1442"></a>FJS-D1442 | notifications — **Who owns Web Push's browser half: Sierra's generated sw.js plus a notifications client subscribe helper, a notifications-generated worker, or the app?** | 2026-10-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D1442`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1442): **A** — Sierra owns the worker, notifications owns the call: `sierra.config.js` gets `push: true`, `swSource` adds the `push` and `notificationclick` handlers (and writes a worker even when `offline` is off, with an empty precache), and `@frontierjs/notifications/client` exports `subscribeToPush({ vapidPublicKey, save })` which registers nothing itself, calls `pushManager.subscribe`, and hands `subscription.toJSON()` to the app's `save` (its own row, per the driver header). One worker per origin, which the platform allows anyway. Cost: Sierra learns the payload shape `webPush` sends, a dependency in spirit from Sierra to notifications' `PushMessage` (Invariant 1 holds, since it is a documented wire shape and not an import). · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) · [IDEAS/web-push.md](IDEAS/web-push.md) |

| <a id="fjs-3583"></a>FJS-3583 | litestone — **A string that is not a date is compared as text against a `DateTime` column: `{ gte: '@today-7' }` answers 0 rows and `{ lte: '@today-7' }` 1, with nothing refusing it** | 2026-10-28 | Refused in litestone's `buildWhere`, not at the junction bridge (owner's choice, so every caller is covered and junction's existing `ValidationError` → 400 carries it). `buildFilterKindMap` ([schema-maps.js](packages/litestone/src/core/schema-maps.js)) now marks a `DateTime` as `instant` and a `String @date` as `day`; `refuseNonDate` ([query.js](packages/litestone/src/core/query.js)) refuses, by name, a string under `equals`/`not`/`gt`/`gte`/`lt`/`lte`/`in`/`notIn` or a bare value that is not an ISO prefix (`2024`, `2024-01`, a day, a `T` instant). A space-separated `2024-01-15 09:30` is refused too, since it sorts wrong against stored `T` text. `contains` stays unchecked: a month is a real substring. Enforced by `test/litestone.test.ts` § *a string operator on a column that is not text*. Closes `IDEAS/filter-controls.md` 2.7. |
| <a id="fjs-d1544"></a>FJS-D1544 | outpost · basecamp · cli — **Where does a fleet box keep its files — one name under the FHS split, one root folder, or as it is — and does app data move from Docker volumes to bind mounts?** | 2026-10-29 | Ruled [`FJS-D1544`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1544): **E** — A, plus `/frontier-outpost/`, a directory holding only symlinks: `config -> /etc/outpost`, `data -> /var/lib/outpost`, `logs -> /var/log/outpost`. The real directories stay where systemd's `*Directory=` keys make and own them, and no code reads the link folder; it is the one place a person on the box looks (`cd /frontier-outpost/data/apps/<app>/data && sqlite3 app.db`). The links point out from the real directories, not into them, because systemd's sandbox and `StateDirectory=` would otherwise have to resolve a symlinked state directory, which nobody here has proved. Cost: `tar` and `du` do not follow a link without `-h`/`-L` (Outpost's own backup reads the real path), `rm -rf /frontier-outpost` removes only links so uninstalling stays `outpost-uninstall`, and one top-level directory is added, holding nothing but links. A bind-mounted front folder was weighed and left out: `tar` would work unflagged, but `rm -rf` on it deletes the data. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

(2) The client: `applyPlan` ([basecamp/deploy.md](packages/cli/commands/basecamp/deploy.md)) posts to the canonical `/domainRoutes`, and the scope it names is `domainRoutes:write` (`keyScope` spells it so; the prose said `domain-routes:write`). (3) The helper: `basecamp()` ([basecamp/_module.md](packages/cli/commands/basecamp/_module.md)) refuses a `text/html` answer of any status, naming the path and asking whether the proxy routes it — a GET used to take the SPA's 200 `index.html` as a `null` body. Probed against a stub answering HTML; cli suite 4025 pass. The idempotent `/domains` and `/domain-routes` writes `FJS-D1599` needs are `FJS-3529`'s |

(A) core/tags-on-npm.js tagsNpmLacks(): every member's <name>@<version> tag at its current version, and INSTALL_PINS (Basecamp's OUTPOST_VERSION), asked of registry.npmjs.org by fetch; fli done reports each npm lacks, never CI (real tree: 21 asked, all held, 0.5s); an unreachable registry is a note. | <a id="fjs-d1600"></a>FJS-D1600 | cli — **Does the generated .dockerignore name what the API image takes (ignore everything, then un-ignore api/ and db/) instead of listing what it leaves out?** | 2026-10-28 | Ruled [`FJS-D1600`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1600): **A** — an allowlist derived from the COPY lines: `*`, then `!api/`, `!db/`, `!deploy/generated/`, `!tsconfig*.json` and `!web/src` when the API reads it. Below those come the excludes for inside the included directories: `**/node_modules`, the SQLite and sidecar globs, `db/backups/`, `**/*.jsonl` (rather than the logger paths read from `schema.lite`: same coverage, nothing to parse), and the test globs. | <a id="fjs-d1599"></a>FJS-D1599 | cli — **Does fli deploy's first-release setup become a reconcile — every write idempotent and gated on what is missing — or keep its never-released gate and report how far a failed run got?** | 2026-10-28 | Ruled [`FJS-D1599`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1599): **A** — a reconcile: setup reads the App, the web App, the Domain and its routes, computes the writes that are MISSING, and applies each one idempotently (an upsert, or a read before a create). The gate becomes "anything missing" rather than "never released", and a Domain on the web App with no route to the API is a preflight finding whose fix is a route write, so preflight and setup share one plan (`releasePlan` in `release-preflight.js`; `neverReleased` goes, and nothing is kept beside it). A field that DIFFERS from the plan is a finding, never a write, so a hand edit on Basecamp is never overwritten. Cost: every Basecamp write setup makes needs an idempotent form (`/domains` and `/domain-routes` first, `FJS-3530`'s path), and the plan has to read the routes too. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

| <a id="fjs-3567"></a>FJS-3567 | caravan, basecamp — **A dispatch of a job DEFINITION before its handler registered ignored the queue, attempts and retry delay the definition states: the restored hub's `restore:reconcile` sat `pending` on `default` forever, and no found replica was ever recorded** | 2026-10-27 | `app.ts` dispatches while the app is being built, before caravan's autoload registers `restore-reconcile.job.ts` on `fleet`, and `insertJob` read only the registration. It now falls back to the definition's own `queue`/`maxAttempts`/`retryDelay` (`src/index.ts`); a string name still has nothing to fall back to. Found by `verify:hub-restore`, which reads the job row off the queue's database: `restore:reconcile`, queue `default`, `pending`. Row in `caravan/test/declaration.test.ts`, red without the fix (`default`, not `fleet`); caravan 379/0, the drive green. A NAME dispatched early still lands on `default` (`FJS-3569`). 4.5's row called `reconcileFound` directly, so the dispatch was the one hop no test had run. |
| <a id="fjs-3566"></a>FJS-3566 | basecamp — **The image and the compose stack set `DATABASE_URL=/db/basecamp.db` and no `AUDIT_PATH`, so the audit trail opened in the container's own layer and started empty after every swap** | 2026-10-27 | `api/src/core/env.ts` derives `AUDIT_PATH` as `audit.db` beside `DATABASE_URL` when only the latter is set (`:memory:` excepted), in `process.env`, so the schema's `env("AUDIT_PATH")` and the litestone CLI a boot spawns for a staged restore both read it — the rule `jobsDatabasePath` already follows for the queue. Probed through `createBasecampDb()`: absolute `DATABASE_URL` alone puts `audit` beside it, relative alone and neither keep `<package>/db/audit.db`, both stated keep `AUDIT_PATH`. `db/README.md` § 3 and `core/db.ts`'s header say so; a bare `litestone` command loads no env.ts and still needs both. Not run: `bun run image:up`, since a dev server holds 8020. A compose stack that ran before this has its old trail in the replaced container. |
| <a id="fjs-3564"></a>FJS-3564 | sierra, basecamp — **A resource's client-side validation refuses a create whose required column a server before/create hook fills: setting a variable on an App answered *Environment is required* and never reached the API** | 2026-10-27 | Ruled `FJS-D1562`: the schema says who fills a blank, and `@default` already means *not required, never not writable*. `Variable.environmentId` is `@default(app.environmentId)`, a new one-hop argument form litestone stamps as the caller before the parent check (`applyPathDefaults`), and `ApiKey.userId` is `@default(auth().id)`; both leave create-mode `required` for the browser and the boundary alike. `scopeFromPayload` no longer fills the column, `ApiKey.mesa` drops `validate: false`, and `VariablesEditor` sends the app alone again — the repro · [write-data.js](packages/litestone/src/core/write-data.js) · [schema.lite](packages/basecamp/db/schema.lite) · [variables.service.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/services/variables/variables.service.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-3561"></a>FJS-3561 | cli — **`fli basecamp:deploy` tags the image and records `commitSha` from `HEAD` while building the working tree, so a release made from uncommitted changes names a commit whose tree it did not build** | 2026-10-27 | Fixed in git-deploy 1.6: the command reads `git status --porcelain` before anything is written into the tree and, when it answers, marks the tag `fjs-<app>:<sha12>-dirty` and the row `commitSha: <sha>-dirty` (`git describe --dirty`'s convention), warns that a rollback to it rebuilds a different tree, and the Releases tab and the release page keep the suffix after the seven-char cut (`shortSha`, `web/src/lib/displays.js`). |
| <a id="fjs-3559"></a>FJS-3559 | basecamp — **A git-built container release records no commit: the `/build` step's reply carries `commit_sha`, the step output says `built 2903b27`, and `Deployment.commitSha` stays null, so the Releases tab shows `—` for every push or button release** | 2026-10-27 | Fixed in git-deploy 1.5: the build branch of `runStep` returns `commit: reply.data.commit_sha` (full sha), the push Receiver passes the payload's `after`, `head_commit.author` and `head_commit.message`, and `deployments.create` fills `branch` from a git source when the caller named none. | <a id="fjs-3560"></a>FJS-3560 | outpost — **A git App whose `source.repo` changed keeps building the old repository: the Outpost's `build` reuses the clone at `work/<appId>` and fetches from whatever `origin` it was first cloned with, so a push to the new repository releases the old one's HEAD and reports success** | 2026-10-27 | Fixed in git-deploy 1.8: `build` reads `git remote get-url origin` off the clone at `work/<appId>` and, when it is not `source.repo` or there is no origin, removes the directory and clones `source.repo` fresh, the shape `siteBuild` already uses; a matching origin is fetched, reset and `clean -fdx`, so nothing a previous checkout left behind joins the build context. Three cases in `outpost.test.js` assert the argv. |
| <a id="fjs-d1549"></a>FJS-D1549 | litestone · junction · cli — **Should FileStorage default to local disk in production, persisted beside the database and served by junction, when no provider is bound?** | 2026-10-27 | Ruled [`FJS-D1549`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d1549): **A** — a production local default: unbound, the plugin stores under `db/files` (the one mount a deploy keeps, and D493's backup and replication scope), junction serves it read-only under `apiPrefix` (`/api/files/:key`), and `publicBase` derives from `FRONTIER_APP_URL`. The client installs `FileStorage` itself when the schema has a File column and no plugin handles it, the way it installs `CapabilityPlugin` for `@@capabilities`, so an app writes nothing. With no options, `FileStorage` reads the framework's own `FILES_PROVIDER`, `FILES_BUCKET`, `FILES_ENDPOINT`, `FILES_KEY`, `FILES_SECRET` and `FILES_PUBLIC_BASE` names, so the deploy can read the same names. The deploy preflight (`envCheck` / `deploy:doctor`) warns, never refuses, on every deploy whose schema has a File column and whose server env leaves `FILES_PROVIDER` unset. The warning names the columns, the folder on the box, and the two fixes: `FILES_PROVIDER=local` to accept the default, or `r2`/`s3` with a bucket and keys. Setting either silences it. Costs: a static route in junction, a backup that grows with uploads, a disk that is now shared by the db and the files, and served files are public by key (an unguessable `:uuid`, the same exposure as an R2 `publicBase`, but now on the app's own origin and CSP). A bound provider later re-points refs by `FJS-D875`, but bytes already on disk need copying. The plugin costs an app with no File column nothing, because it is never installed. On a model with no File column and no relation to one, each hook returns after one map lookup (`external-ref.js`). · [file-storage.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js) · [local.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/providers/local.js) · [05-backup.md](packages/cli/commands/deploy/_steps-docker/05-backup.md) · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-3554"></a>FJS-3554 | mesa · cli — **Shift+alt over an open modal `<dialog>` painted nothing and the ask panel opened beneath it: the inspector's box and the ask panel mounted on `<body>`, under the top layer and inert** | 2026-10-27 | Reported on remnant's Research sheet (kit `Drawer`, `showModal()`); a press on the panel landed on the backdrop and closed the sheet. The inspector's box and badge ([inspect-client.js](packages/mesa/mesa-vite/inspect-client.js)) and ask's panel, tab and text box ([vite-ask-client.js](packages/cli/core/vite-ask-client.js)) mount in the topmost `dialog:modal`, the kit's `overlayHost()` rule (`FJS-D904`), and follow a modal that opens or closes under an open panel (a MutationObserver on `open`, since showModal() fires no event); `__fjsInspect.ignore(el)` keeps a picker's panel from being picked once it sits inside the dialog. Proved by a throwaway Chrome probe (box parent is the dialog, a pick inside it lands, a pick-mode press on an ignored panel stays the panel's) and `test:browser:vite` inspect 30/30; no drive opens a modal yet. |
| <a id="fjs-3516"></a>FJS-3516 | basecamp — **The `/servers/:id` edit drawer offers no `ipAddress`, so a Server row enrolled or imported without one can never be given one** | 2026-10-26 | The drawer's `only=` ([servers/[id]/index.mesa](packages/basecamp/web/src/routes/servers/[id]/index.mesa)) left the address out on the ground that a heartbeat or Sync overwrites it — true only of a provider machine (`providerServerId`); on Basecamp's own box (FJS-3515) and an imported one nothing else writes it. **Fixed, uncommitted**: the drawer offers `ipAddress` when the row has no `providerServerId`; `servers.patch` already took it. | <a id="fjs-3510"></a>FJS-3510 | sierra — **Match system on thod's and serpgrid's account pages never followed the OS again: they read and removed the bare `<app>_theme` key while `persisted()` stores `<prefix>:<key>`, and the pick they made first was stored** | 2026-10-26 | After a reload a picked theme showed *Matching your system* with no card checked; pressing it saved the light/dark theme as a pick and cleared a key nobody wrote. | <a id="fjs-3499"></a>FJS-3499 | cli — **Every scaffolded app was pinned to dev slot 0, so a second app was refused at the first one's 8000: `readsPortVar` judged the API by text and a junction API never spells `FRONTIER_PORT_BE`; a remembered slot was also reused with no check that another app now owned it** | 2026-10-25 | Six apps shared slot 0 in `~/.fli/sessions.lock`. Fixed in `core/ports.js`: a surface whose manifest depends on junction follows the slot; a slot two apps remember is kept by the running holder, else the elder claim, and the other is reallocated; `fli ps` warns on a shared slot and `--clean <name>` forgets one app; the `fli dev` refusal names who holds each port (this app's own run, another app's slot, or a process outside any slot) · [ports.js](packages/cli/core/ports.js) · [dev.md](packages/cli/commands/utils/dev.md) |
| <a id="fjs-3493"></a>FJS-3493 | sierra — **A prerendered page never loads the app's `web/src/lib/sugar.js`, so a template calling `.toMoney()` throws at build** | 2026-10-25 | `main.js` is imported by `virtual:sierra` in the browser; `packages/sierra/src/build/prerender.js` renders in node and imports no `main.js`. thod and basecamp prerender no money screen today, so nothing fails yet. | <a id="fjs-3484"></a>FJS-3484 | cli — **`cloudflare/_module.md` gives `zone` and `account` only a `defaultValue`, so every `cloudflare:` command that does not declare them prints a blank `--zone`/`--account` row in `--help`** | 2026-10-24 | `fli cf:purge --help` showed a blank `--account`, `fli dns --help` both, and `cloudflare:tui` takes neither. The fix the row first proposed, a `type` and `description` in `defaults.flags`, would have offered every command a flag only some read. Worse: each command's own `defaultValue: ''` overrode the module's `${CLOUDFLARE_DEFAULT_ZONE}`, so the documented env default never reached `cf:dns` or `cf:purge`. Fixed: `defaults:` is gone from the cloudflare module; `cf:dns` and `cf:purge` read `flag.zone \|  \| CLOUDFLARE_DEFAULT_ZONE` themselves, and `cf` reads `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` from the inherited env. `github/_module.md` had the same shape (a blank `--org` on `github:clone`/`github:prs`, which take none) and lost its `defaults:` too, since `github:create` already reads `GITHUB_DEFAULT_ORG` itself. Proved by `--help` on each command and a `cf:purge`/`cf:dns --dry` with the env set and `cf` off the PATH, which now reach the zone lookup · [_module.md](packages/cli/commands/cloudflare/_module.md) · [flags.js](packages/cli/core/flags.js) |
| <a id="fjs-3483"></a>FJS-3483 | cli — **fli admin:generate writes admin/index.mesa with `.card` in its own <style>, so a fresh app's fli check warns css-class-collision (Invariant 13) against the kit's Card before the owner has written a line** | 2026-10-24 | Same shape as FJS-3088. | <a id="fjs-3531"></a>FJS-3531 | cli — **`fli deploy` released the BUILD machine's image id, which a machine on Docker's containerd image store never holds, so every release there pulled a tag no registry has: *pull access denied*** | 2026-10-27 | Seen on NELA / Preview, release 76ce52c6: built here on Docker 29.8 `overlay2` as `sha256:5f7f9a94…` (config digest), loaded onto yuma (Docker 29.9, `driver-type: io.containerd.snapshotter.v1`) where the same tag is `sha256:3ccbf268…` (manifest digest). Outpost's `pull` ([docker.js](packages/outpost/src/docker.js)) found no image by the released id and pulled. [deploy.md](packages/cli/commands/basecamp/deploy.md) said a save/load round trip keeps the id, true only between two classic stores. **Built, uncommitted**: after each load the command asks that machine's daemon for the tag's id and releases it; machines disagreeing on the id are refused by name, since one release names one digest. Compiler suite 469/469; the next release of `app` on yuma went green (owner's run, 2026-10-27). Not checked: `fli deploy:local`'s and `deploy/`'s own ship paths were not checked for the same assumption |
| <a id="fjs-3527"></a>FJS-3527 | cli — **`service-as-system` scans only `services/`, so a helper moved to `domain/` by FJS-3518 is no longer graded** | 2026-10-27 | Cause: the scan was `services/` alone, and FJS-3518 moved `scopes.ts` and `job-schedule.ts` into `domain/`; FJS-3528 had already hidden both behind `app.db!`. A plain widen was measured first, over example, every package and the 37 linked prototypes: +71 findings, 28 of them ELA false positives, because a `domain/` helper takes the client as a parameter (`mergeTasks($.db, …)`) and a bare parameter names whatever the caller passed. Fix: [checks/data.js](packages/cli/core/checks/data.js) scans `domain/` too, and there grades only a client the file names itself (`app.db`, an import or a column-0 const, a call's answer) — +24 findings, every one in basecamp, quo or sstime. Basecamp's 17 were traced to their callers and each is a correct reach: job- or boot-only, the installation tier behind `requireSystemAdmin`, or an id a service first read through `db()` (`deployInScope`, `grantedSite`); all sit in [check-baseline.json](packages/basecamp/check-baseline.json) `allow` with the reason. quo's 2 and sstime's 1 are in their own repos and unanswered. Cases *a domain helper reaching for the app client is graded* and *a client a domain helper is handed is the caller's* in [checks.test.js](packages/cli/test/checks.test.js) |
| <a id="fjs-3528"></a>FJS-3528 | cli — **`service-as-system` read no receiver through a non-null assertion, so `app.db!.asSystem()` was skipped rather than graded** | 2026-10-27 | Cause: the receiver is read backwards from the dot, and a `!` was neither stepped over nor stripped, so the read answered an empty receiver and the call was skipped — and `$.db!.asSystem()` read as `$.db!`, which no longer matched the request client. Basecamp spells the app client `app.db!`, which hid `scopes.ts` and `job-schedule.ts` before FJS-3527 moved them and hid `edge`, `hub` and `metrics-store` in `services/`; all three are correct reaches and now sit in [check-baseline.json](packages/basecamp/check-baseline.json) `allow`, where `fli check` reads them (hub moved out of `scripts/ci-allowances.json`, which `fli check` does not read). Fix: [checks/data.js](packages/cli/core/checks/data.js); *a non-null assertion does not hide the receiver* in [checks.test.js](packages/cli/test/checks.test.js) |
| <a id="fjs-3518"></a>FJS-3518 | basecamp — **Basecamp's `api/src/core/` held 45 files and 9,717 lines, of which 13 set up what the framework ships, and `fli check` graded nothing inside `core/` but a plugin, so `FJS-D625`'s *domain logic moves to `domain/`* was prose there** | 2026-10-27 | Cause: `FJS-D128`'s text still said domain modules sit "beside app.ts in `src/`" after `FJS-D625` gave them `domain/<area>/`, and [packages/basecamp/CLAUDE.md](packages/basecamp/CLAUDE.md) called `core/` "the one-owner modules"; a reader of either filed domain logic there. **Check built** (`0849894c`): `app-layout` warns on a `core/` file `app.ts` never reaches, directly or through another `core/` file ([checks/app.js](packages/cli/core/checks/app.js) `srcLayoutFindings`). Measured over example and 30 prototypes before writing: no setup file (db, gate, auth, env, channels, mailer, principal) fires; 22 domain modules in prototypes do. It misses domain logic `app.ts` imports itself (basecamp's `workbench`, `site-preview`, `site-checkout`, `dependencies`, `app-source`, `notify`), which only a reading of the code tells from setup. Basecamp adopted 22 at `app-layout: 31`. D128 amended and the CLAUDE.md line rewritten. **Moved**: `workbench*.ts` to `domain/workbench/` and `site-*.ts` to `domain/site/`, names kept, baseline 31 → 24; `notify.ts`, `delivery.ts` and `services/notification-preferences/kinds.ts` to `domain/notification/`, `alerting.ts` to `domain/alert/`, 24 → 21; `app-source.ts`, `release-record.ts`, `runtime.ts` and `variables.ts` to `domain/release/`, 21 → 17; `posture.ts`, `reachability.ts`, `server-metrics.ts` and `host-machine.ts` to `domain/host/` and `probe.ts` to `domain/monitor/`, 17 → 12; `local-git.ts` and `local-ssh.ts` to `domain/local/`, 12 → 10; `preview.ts` to `domain/workbench/workbench-preview.ts`, 10 → 9; `registers.ts` to `domain/register/`, the helpers `scopes.ts` to `domain/api-key/`, `disk-report.ts` to `domain/cleanup/`, `job-schedule.ts` to `domain/job/` and `backup-archives.ts` to `domain/backup/`, and the four operator scripts `fli` runs (`enroll-host`, `provision-host`, `host-record`, `bearer-send`) to `domain/host/` by the owner's call, with `fli deploy`'s `docker exec` path and `fli basecamp:*`'s `runProvision` moved with them; 9 → 0 and the baseline entry dropped |
| <a id="fjs-3520"></a>FJS-3520 | cli — **A freshly scaffolded app fails its own `bun run check`: biome's `noSelfAssign` refuses `items[index] = items[index]` in the generated `sugar.js`** | 2026-10-27 | Found proving `FJS-3519`. The `reset` array helper in [scaffold/web.js](packages/cli/core/scaffold/web.js) `makeSugarJs` reassigns a row to itself so a reactive list sees it change; `lint` (`biome check --error-on-warnings`) failed on it, so `check` stopped before typecheck. The owner confirmed the self-assign is how a Mesa list learns a row changed, so the line stays. **Fixed:** the line carries a `biome-ignore lint/correctness/noSelfAssign` stating why, in the scaffold and in basecamp's `sugar.js`. Proved by a fresh `fli new` app whose `bun run check` exits 0 |
| <a id="fjs-3519"></a>FJS-3519 | mesa — **Every app carries the same viewer-zone date binding as a scaffolded `web/src/lib/datetime.js`, and 28 of 32 prototype copies are byte-identical to `makeDatetimeJs()`, so the template changes and the copies never follow** | 2026-10-27 | Cause: `@frontierjs/toolbelt/datetime` reads no clock (`FJS-D26`), so `createDatetime({ timeZone: viewer, now: Date.now })` and its `at`/`ago` were generated into each app ([scaffold/web.js](packages/cli/core/scaffold/web.js) `makeDatetimeJs`, imported by `makeSugarJs`). Nobody edits the file; calendly, ela, quo, basecamp and example only ADD helpers over the same `dt`. **Fix** (owner chose `@frontierjs/mesa/browser` over sierra and toolbelt): the kit exports `dt`, `at`, `ago`; the scaffold stops emitting the file; screens, `sugar.js`, example and basecamp move in the same change, as does the `createDatetime` doc example ([datetime.js](packages/toolbelt/src/datetime/datetime.js) `createDatetime`). The 28 prototype copies keep working, since `createDatetime` stays, and nothing flags them as stale. Ruled `FJS-D1543`: every host, the server zone under a server render, no scaffolded file. Plan step: [IDEAS/browser-kit.md](IDEAS/browser-kit.md) 1.7 **Fixed:** [datetime.js](packages/mesa/src/browser/datetime.js) exports `dt`, `at`, `ago`, typed with the server-zone answer in `browser.d.ts`; `makeDatetimeJs` is deleted, the scaffolded `sugar.js` and account page import the kit, and basecamp keeps its own four patterns over the kit's `dt`. Example had no binding to move: its `lib/datetime.js` is the shop's calendar. Proved by a scaffolded app (build, `fli check`, typecheck, tests) and the date words under bun in two `TZ`s |
| <a id="fjs-3517"></a>FJS-3517 | junction — **`sys(fn)` at the app prompt runs as nobody in an app that declares no `createApp({ system })`** | 2026-10-27 | `app.runAs(null)` makes the principal `opts.system ?? null`, so in Basecamp, which declares none, a service called inside `sys(() => app.service(…))` is graded STRANGER(0) and refused. That contradicts the prompt's help text, *the app's own principal, every gate off*. `sys.<model>.<verb>()` had the same defect, and it was fixed in [tools/call.ts](packages/junction/tools/call.ts) by switching to `asSystem()`, first seen on yuma as `FeatureFlag.create requires level 4, user has level 0`. `sys(fn)` can't take that fix, because a service builds its own client off the request's principal. Two candidate fixes, each a decision: Basecamp declares a `system` principal graded SYSADMIN, or `runAs(null)` with nothing declared yields a system client (this widens every job's authority). **Fixed, uncommitted, by B (owner, 2026-10-27)**: Basecamp declares `SYSTEM` (`id: 'basecamp'`, `isSystemAdmin`) in [core/gate.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/core/gate.ts), with `actsAsApp(user)` beside it. Six places that read *no user* as *the app is acting* now take that predicate: `runsAsApp`/`runsAsCaller`/`runsEitherWay` in `jobs/context.ts`, `actor()` in `core/resource.ts`, `scopeToWorkspace`/`requireWorkspaceRole` in `core/hooks.ts`, `jobInScope` in `jobs.service.ts`, and edge `syncStep` and its hook. Without the predicate, every `runsAsApp` cron job would have thrown, and `runsAsCaller` would have accepted the app as a caller. Proved: through the prompt, `sys(() => app.service('servers').find())` with `--tenant` answers 6, and with the declaration removed it answers `Unauthorized`. Basecamp suite 997/0, typecheck at its baseline of 7. `IDEAS/app-console.md` 2.3 |

| <a id="fjs-3512"></a>FJS-3512 | cli — **`basecamp:create` sat on *Enrolling … in basecamp's fleet* for about seven minutes with nothing printed, so a working run read as a hung one** | 2026-10-26 | ops1, 21:43–21:49 UTC: the install script's first security-update pass (apt-daily-upgrade, ~30 unattended-upgrade steps on a fresh DigitalOcean image) is deliberate, but `machine.run` holds the script's output unless `--verbose`, so an echo there never reaches the operator. Fixed: `enrollOutpost` prints that the install's security updates take several minutes before it runs the command, and the script echoes the same before the pass for a person who pasted it · [_module.md](packages/cli/commands/deploy/_module.md) · [enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-3513"></a>FJS-3513 | basecamp — **a deploy to Basecamp's own box failed at *Upload files* with a 60s timeout: the Outpost advertised the DigitalOcean anchor address, which the API container cannot reach** | 2026-10-26 | ops1, release eaef929a: the host row has no `ipAddress`, so the install script fell back to the default route's source, which under a reserved IP is the anchor 10.17.0.5; policy rule 101 routes every reply from it out eth0, so the container's SYN-ACK never returned. Fixed: the fallback takes docker0's address first, which a container reaches without leaving the box (the pin, not the hostname, authenticates the Outpost); ops1's `outpost.env` was patched the same way and the target re-registered at `https://172.17.0.1:8180` · [enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-3511"></a>FJS-3511 | basecamp — **The Outpost install command passed `ENROLL_TOKEN` in `sudo env`'s argv, and sudo writes every command line to the system journal, so a live enrollment token sat in the box's journal** | 2026-10-26 | Found on ops1's first real `basecamp:create`: `journalctl` held `sudo[…]: COMMAND=/usr/bin/env … ENROLL_TOKEN=bcen_…`. The token is single-use and short-lived, but a failed install leaves it valid for `ENROLL_WINDOW_MS` and readable by the `adm` group. Fixed: `installCommand` now writes `{ echo export …; curl -fsSL …/install.sh; } \| sudo bash`, so the four inputs go ahead of the script on bash's stdin and `echo` is a builtin; probed with a stand-in `install.sh` · [enrollment.ts](packages/basecamp/api/src/providers/compute/enrollment.ts) · [verify-provision.mjs](packages/basecamp/web/test/verify-provision.mjs) |
| <a id="fjs-3384"></a>FJS-3384 | jetty — **A code-only island bundles 179 kB** | 2026-10-22 | Cause was not the main entry: a code-only island's auto-gen entry always imports runIsland, whose mount() reaches @frontierjs/mesa/runtime through a dynamic import, and the island build inlines every dynamic import, so 172 kB of Mesa rode into a bundle that only runs main(). islandsViteConfig now takes the island's source path and a pre plugin (codeOnlyIslandPlugin, vite-config.js) swaps runtime/mount.js for a throwing stub when the file holds exactly one defineIsland({...}) whose object literal has no app key; a spread, computed key, config held in a variable or parse failure keeps the real mount, so a wrong guess costs bytes and never a UI. Code-only island now 20 kB; test/phase10.test.js builds a code-only island (under 30 kB), an app island and an opaque-config island (both keep mount). |
| <a id="fjs-3385"></a>FJS-3385 | jetty — **buildManifest has no way to carry commands, so an extension cannot declare a keyboard shortcut** | 2026-10-22 | baseManifest never read a commands key, so a shortcut had no path to the manifest. Fix: config.commands is emitted verbatim as manifest.commands (same shape in Chrome and Firefox, omitted when empty), the manifest's own spelling rather than a passthrough bag. Proved by the new 'manifest — commands' group in packages/jetty/test/phase6.test.js (red before, green after; full jetty suite passes); verify:extension cannot run here (no Chrome, open FJS-3235). |

The drive's two unbounded fetches (the anonymous GET and the cleanup DELETEs) now carry AbortSignal.timeout(5000), so a real hang fails the drive by name instead of never reporting · [verify-live.mjs](example/web/test/verify-live.mjs) · [verify-live.mjs](example/web/test/verify-live.mjs) |

Six packages had never run: mcp, outpost, oracle, site-kit, create-frontier and `website/` (found by CI once the exit was read). Each has a tsconfig now; the five JS ones keep `checkJs` off as email-kit and print-kit do, because on it reports 107, 427, 794 and 13 errors in oracle, outpost, site-kit and create-frontier, almost all TS7006 implicit any. mcp is TypeScript and measured 43, fixed to 0 rather than baselined: 25 were tests typing `app` as `Record<string, never>` (now junction’s `App`), the rest test casts, a duplicated `profiles` key in `profiles.ts`, `Bun.Build.Target` that is `CompileTarget`, and three casts in `plugin.ts`. Proved: the stub-tsc tests for both exits; `bun run typecheck` clean in all six and exit 2 without a tsconfig; mcp `bun run test` 186 pass · [typecheck.js](packages/cli/core/typecheck.js) · [typecheck.mjs](scripts/typecheck.mjs) · [typecheck.js](packages/cli/core/typecheck.js) · [typecheck.mjs](scripts/typecheck.mjs) · [typecheck-baselines.json](scripts/typecheck-baselines.json) |

| <a id="fjs-3221"></a>FJS-3221 | basecamp — **api/test/edge.test.ts sync writes the ingress A record twice under the full basecamp suite and passes alone** | 2026-10-25 | Cause: the sync block's beforeAll walks three servers through checkIn and drain. Litestone delivers a $tapEvents tap on setImmediate, so the fleet tap's pushAppsOn ran after the App was placed and queued an app-mode domain:dns job per Domain (api, taken, moved). Their pushes raced the sync tests' own, and setRecords reads and then writes, so two at once each posted the A record. Fix: beforeAll lets the walk's taps run before placing anything, so the moves find no App to push. In production a duplicate heals on the next set, and Cloudflare refuses an identical record. Proof: run with a probe in the job, no app-mode push before the job section; edge.test.ts 53/53; full basecamp suite 992/992 twice. · [edge.test.ts](packages/basecamp/api/test/edge.test.ts) |

Fix: new warn rule anonymous-create-auth-default in advise.js (skips optional columns, relations, the tenancy-generated stamp, and models with no gate); catalog, reference and assistant snapshots regenerated. Re-probe: glow('{"a":1,"b":2,"c":3}', {language:'json'}) gives three separate <b> keys; pinned by glow.spec.js 'a JSON key is its own token' (toolbelt suite 726 passing). · [glow.js:95](packages/toolbelt/src/glow/glow.js#L95) |
| <a id="fjs-2223"></a>FJS-2223 | toolbelt · litestone — **geo point() accepts '', ' ', [] and true as coordinates: isPoint({lat:'',lng:''}) is true (Null Island)** | 2026-10-11 | geo's `point()` read coordinates with `Number(...)`, so '', ' ', [] and false became 0 and true became 1 (Null Island); `boundingBox` radius had the same hole (`true` = 1 m). Now a local `asNumber` (same rule as `/units`: a number or a non-blank string) feeds both. Proved by the new `geo.spec.js` row (red before, green after); toolbelt 726, litestone point.test.ts 49, sierra 2049 pass. `fli prove` ran only the css `code` drive, which cannot start here (no Chrome). · [geo.js:47-48](packages/toolbelt/src/geo/geo.js#L47) · [query.js:2305](packages/litestone/src/core/query.js#L2305) |
| <a id="fjs-2221"></a>FJS-2221 | toolbelt · sierra — **inflect words() is quadratic on a run of capitals: humanize('A'×40000) takes 2 s** | 2026-10-11 | Cause: `([A-Z]+)([A-Z][a-z])` in words() retried from every start of a capital run (quadratic). | <a id="fjs-2217"></a>FJS-2217 | toolbelt · ui — **coerceLike and convertTo read numbers with Number(), so typing 0x1F into a number cell stores 31, 0b101 stores 5 and ' 12 ' stores 12** | 2026-10-11 | coerceLike and convertTo read numbers with Number(), which also takes 0x/0b/0o literals and padding. Both now go through one decimal-grammar helper (sign, digits, fraction, exponent; finite) in json.js; coerceLike keeps ' 12 ' and 0x1F as text, and convertTo keeps its deliberate trim of a string but no longer reads hex/binary/octal. Proved by two new json.spec.js rows (red before, toolbelt suite 724 passing). · [json.js:283](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js#L283) · [Json.mesa:429](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa#L429) |
| <a id="fjs-2216"></a>FJS-2216 | toolbelt · ui — **markRuns takes indices from toLowerCase() and slices the original with them, so markRuns('İİ ab', 'ab') marks an empty hit and leaves ab unmarked** | 2026-10-11 | markRuns read indices off hay.toLowerCase() and sliced the original with them; İ lowercases to two units, so every hit after one shifted. Now lowers per code point and maps each lowered unit back to its character's span in the original. Proved by the new json.spec 'a character whose lowercase is longer does not shift the hit' (toolbelt suite 722 passing). · [json.js:1170](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js#L1170) · [Json.mesa:561](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa#L561) |
| <a id="fjs-2215"></a>FJS-2215 | toolbelt · litestone — **evaluate reads a bare field without columnOf while read() applies it, so on a belongsTo owner == auth() is true and owner == auth().id is null; the SQL half also matches nothing, so the policy silently admits no row** | 2026-10-11 | Cause: the policy's bare `field` node was read two ways. `evaluate` (predicate.js) read `own(record, node.name)` while every operand position went through `columnOf`, and `compileSql`'s `field` case emitted the relation's own name, which SQLite reads as a string literal. So on a belongsTo `owner == auth().id` was null in JS (create refused) and matched nothing in SQL (read empty), while `owner == auth()` worked. Fix: the JS `field` node now reads through `read()`, and the SQL `field` case maps a belongsTo to its foreign key (`fieldColumnName`). Chosen over refusing at parse because `owner == auth()`, `owner == null` and `owner in …` already read the key, so refusing one spelling would split the language. Proved by the toolbelt `columnOf`/`auth().id` spec and the 'a belongsTo field read through a claim' case in policy-interpreters.test.ts (read and create), both red before; toolbelt, litestone and sierra suites pass apart from load flakes that pass alone. · [predicate.js:233](packages/toolbelt/src/predicate/predicate.js#L233) · [policy.js:1724](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js#L1724) |

Both now refuse by name with a TypeError (non-string id/event, data JSON cannot carry); .d.ts says so. Proved by the new sse.spec.js row (red before), toolbelt 720 pass, junction 2877 pass, sierra event-stream pass. | <a id="fjs-2267"></a>FJS-2267 | litestone — **`loadMigrationSql` caches a file's SQL by `mtimeMs`, so a migration file rewritten within one mtime tick of its last load reads back as the OLD text — a BLOCKED section deleted by the reviewer is still refused in that process** | 2026-10-11 | Cause: loadMigrationSql keyed its parsed-SQL cache on statSync().mtimeMs, so a file rewritten inside one mtime tick (or with its mtime restored) read back as the old text. Fix: read the file each time and key the cache on the text itself (the split is what the cache saves; the read is cheap), dropping the stat. Proof: new migrations-fixes test 'a rewritten file reads back as its new text even when its mtime did not move' (utimesSync pins the old mtime; red before, green after) and the old BLOCKED-section test now passes deterministically. · [migrations.js:527](packages/litestone/src/core/migrations.js#L527) · [migrations-fixes.test.ts:179](packages/litestone/test/migrations-fixes.test.ts#L179) |

| <a id="fjs-2303"></a>FJS-2303 | litestone — **`PRAGMA page_size = 8192` runs after the database is already in WAL mode, where it does nothing, so every database `createClient` makes is 4096** | 2026-10-11 | Cause: openSqliteConnections issued PRAGMA page_size = 8192 after applyWal, and the switch into WAL writes the header that fixes the size, so SQLite accepted the pragma and kept 4096. S3: every response now carries X-Studio-Rev (generation bumped by Studio's own write routes, PRAGMA data_version and schema_version for outside writers, mtimes of the schema file and migration files), api() records it, and showTool reloads Overview, Perf, Migrations and Stats only when it moved (loadIfMoved). S7: diffAgainstSchema memoizes on the handle's schema_version and /api/advise on the parsed schema's identity. S8: driftLoad returns while document.hidden and runs again on visibilitychange. S9: the page and /api/catalog are built and gzipped once, carry an ETag and answer 304, and every other JSON body over 1 KB is gzipped (downloads stay streamed). The CDN stylesheet is filed apart. | <a id="fjs-1410"></a>FJS-1410 | junction · litestone — **A custom method's ANSWER has no declaration: a `methods:` entry takes `input` and `gate` and nothing for what comes back, and the client's `invoke()` answers `Promise<unknown>`, so a `type` in `db/schema.lite` can describe a method's payload and never its result.** **Measured** 2026-09-27 in *Portal, the search stressor*. Discovery drafted `type Hit { url, title, snippet?, provider }` for the thing the app serves most, a search result. `litestone types` emits `interface Hit` for both audiences, and nothing can point at it: `MethodDeclaration` is `{ method, input?, gate? }` ([service.ts](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts)), `ServiceTypes` maps `usages` to the `Usage` row, and `invoke()`/`call()` return `unknown`. By Phase 2 the declared type had **drifted from the served answer** (`provider` beside `providers: string[]`, `domain`, `score` and `rule`) and no check noticed, because no check connects them. The shape lives in TS in the app's `domain/search/merge.ts`, and the terminal reads `res.hits` untyped; the `web/` page will too. So the answer a Resource or `<Table>` would bind to has no seed-side owner. Wanted: `{ method, input, output: 'SearchResult' }`, reaching the client's typed call and the surface snapshot as `input` already does. Neighbour of [`FJS-706`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-706) `core-11` (`app.service('x')` is `Promise<unknown>` on the server), and of [`FJS-1254`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1254) (a `type` reaches the browser registry and the UI realm cannot address it) | 2026-10-11 | A `methods:` entry had no word for its answer. Added `output: 'Type'` beside `input` (`collectMethodOutputs`, `Service._outputs`, `describe().outputs`, `SurfaceService.outputs`), reported in the surface snapshot as `**output**` and as the OpenAPI 200 body of the custom-method operation (`$ref`, or a titled `oneOf` for several). It documents the answer and does not validate it. Proved by the 'a declared output is reported' block in `junction/test/method-input.test.ts`. The client's typed `invoke()` is split out as FJS-2656. · [service.ts `MethodDeclaration`](packages/junction/src/core/service.ts) · [client/index.ts `invoke`](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [portal api/src/domain/search/merge.ts `Hit`](../fjs-prototypes/portal/api/src/domain/search/merge.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-373"></a>FJS-373 | cli · css — **`fli check` grades one corner of Invariant 13 and not the one an app breaks.** Corrected 2026-09-13: `css-token-undefined` (error) refuses a styled value naming a token the stylesheets do not define; the raw-color ratio below is still graded by nothing. *Style with a tone and a treatment, never a color* is enforced inside `packages/css` (a literal `font-size` outside `tokens.css` fails its suite) and nowhere in an app. Measured on `KOBAMI/my.maid.tech`, 56k lines written to this framework's ideas: it HAS the semantic layer — `theme-primary`, `theme-danger`, `.btn.outlined`, `.btn.danger`, arrived at independently — and uses **578 raw color utilities against 156 semantic ones**, about 4:1 (`text-gray-500` 74, `bg-gray-100` 54, `text-red-600` 13). The vocabulary was not rejected; it was out-competed by whatever was quicker under deadline, which is what a rule with no check always loses to. `fli check` is the place — it already owns everything derived from the seed while Biome owns generic JavaScript (`FJS-D33`), and it reads `.mesa`, which Biome does not. Open question the rule has to answer before it can exist: an app may legitimately run UnoCSS alongside `@frontierjs/css` (`packages/css/README.md` § Using it with UnoCSS), so the check counts and reports a ratio rather than refusing a class — a warn naming the worst files, in the shape of `package-root-md`, which warns and names rather than failing because the rule cannot tell a stray from the next necessary thing | 2026-10-11 | Invariant 13's raw-color corner had no check: css-raw-literal reads a .mesa <style>, css-token-undefined reads var(), and nothing read a class attribute, where my.maid.tech's 578 raw utilities against 156 semantic ones lived. | <a id="fjs-1124"></a>FJS-1124 | litestone — **Every `factories.<model>.asSystem()` starts its sequence at 1, and the unique-retry loop hides it until the seventh row.** A chain method clones the factory and the clone copies `_seq` from the ORIGINAL, which never builds and so never advances — every clone generates `user1@test.com` first. `createOne` retries a unique violation up to `UNIQUE_RETRIES` (5), advancing the clone's own seq each time, so the Nth fresh call silently makes N−1 failed inserts and the seventh throws `UniqueConflictError: User: email "user6@test.com" is already taken`. Measured 2026-09-13 on a `fli new --auth` app: seven calls of `env.factories.user.asSystem().createOne()` answer user1…user6 then throw; one held `asSystem()` handle is clean. The failure looks like a fixture bug in whichever test crosses the seventh row, and `docs/testing.md`'s own first sample (`env.factories.doc.withParents().createOne()`) and `tutor:test`'s lessons use the fresh-chain-per-call shape. `testing.js` already knows the hazard in its own callers (one clone per model, § "every chain method clones from the ORIGINAL, whose seq never moves") — the fix is for the chain to share the sequence rather than for each caller to hold a handle. The scaffold's `api/test/access.test.ts` holds one handle and says why. | 2026-10-11 | Already fixed by FJS-1779's shared sequence box (Factory constructor; _clone copies the box by reference), so a fresh asSystem() chain no longer restarts at 1. Probed ten fresh chains on a unique-email model: Email 1..10, no retries. Regression test in test/factory-schema-rules.test.ts goes red when _clone copies the counter by value. · [seeder.js `_clone`](packages/litestone/src/seeder.js) · [`buildOne`/`createOne`](packages/litestone/src/seeder.js) |
| <a id="fjs-629"></a>FJS-629 | example · junction — **There is no way to remove a person.** **The gap this row was filed for has closed** — a `methods: [{ method, gate }]` entry grades a custom method now (`customMethodGrade`, [`FJS-1087`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1087)), which is the third candidate below — so what is left is `example`'s `users.remove` itself, still unbuilt, and `verify:users` still leaving an account behind. As filed: `example`'s `users` service declines DELETE deliberately — auth keeps `Credential` and `Session` at `@@gate("8")` with `userId` as a bare String and no relation behind it, so removing a `User` through the ORM leaves live sessions authenticating against a row that is gone. The correct operation is `IAuth.deleteUser(id)`, which clears credentials, sessions and the address's pending verification tokens and then removes the row — and it cannot be reached from a service without becoming a CUSTOM method, which is where the gap is. **`gateAuth` grades CRUD against the model's `@@gate` and says nothing about a custom method** — junction states it in the source: *a method the map does not name is not gated here*. So `users.remove` would have to establish its own authority, and the only tools available are `if (level < 5)`, which is the seed's rule written a second time in a place nothing keeps in step (the mistake `payments.refund` documents at length and refuses to make), or a hand-rolled hook. `payments.refund`'s own answer does not generalize: it asks `db.order.transitions(row)`, which works because a refund IS a declared move, and removing a person is not one. **The near miss is what makes it worth filing.** `@@gate` is single-valued, so an `extend model` may not restate it — which is why `create` had to be expressed as `@@allow('create', auth().isAdmin)` instead, and that worked precisely because create is a CRUD operation the gate map names. There is no counterpart for a method the app invents. Three candidate shapes and they are different features: a per-method gate in the seed (`methods { remove @gate(5) }`), extending `@@capabilities` to cover a non-move custom method (`FJS-D149` ruled that a non-CRUD action earns a capability *by being a move* — this is the case that is not), or a declared `gate:` on the `methods:` entry beside `input:`, which is the cheapest and keeps the declaration in the service where the method is. **The cost while it is open is measurable**: `verify:users` leaves one account behind on every run and cannot sweep it, so every count in that drive is a delta or a re-read rather than an absolute | 2026-10-11 | Already built, in the wrong place: @frontierjs/auth's `people` service ships `remove` at declared gate 5 (it calls `IAuth.deleteUser`, clearing credentials, sessions and pending tokens) and example mounts it, so no per-method gate on `users` was needed — the `methods: [{ method, gate }]` form of FJS-1087 is what grades it. What was left was the drive: `verify:users` now sweeps its own accounts through DELETE /people/:id (shopper 403, admin 200, a removed person's session 401) and first sweeps earlier runs' `<letter>-<6>@shop.test` leftovers, which had piled to 132 rows and pushed the seeded people past the drive's $limit=100 read. Stubbing the door to /users reds four checks; 105/105 on private ports. users.service.ts and the drive header now point at people.remove. · [users.service.ts](example/api/src/services/users.service.ts) · [litestone.ts `gateAuth`](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [payments.service.ts](example/api/src/services/payments.service.ts) · [FJS-D149](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d149) |
| <a id="fjs-2262"></a>FJS-2262 | litestone · sierra — **`FJS-D366` ruled `@rank(scope:)` on 2026-10-05 — litestone owning default-to-end, `(rank, id)` order and `rebalance()`, sierra `resource.move()` — and nothing parses `@rank`, so every board, backlog and inbox lane mints, jitters and bounds its own order keys in app code** | 2026-10-11 | Built FJS-D366 end to end. Toolbelt: @frontierjs/toolbelt/rank — between, jittered, after (the shortest later key: the append default grows a digit about every sixty appends, not six) and spread (even, fixed-width keys for a rebalance); test/specs/rank.spec.js walks 2000 random drops and checks byte order. Litestone: @rank(scope: col \| [cols]) parsed, refused by name (bare, optional, non-String, a second owner, a relation as scope, jsonl), x-rank in JSON Schema, not required on create; a create with no key, or an update moving scope without one, lands at the end of the scope inside the write's unit; a stated key must be isRankKey; an orderBy naming a rank appends the key in the same direction; updateMany moving scope without rank refused; upsertMany keeps a conflicting row's key unless its scope moves; rebalance({ where, field }) spreads each scope through update(), so each row is announced and @version bumps. test/rank.test.ts (25, the tie-break mutant dies), docs/rank.md (6 fences green). Suites: toolbelt 716, litestone 6925 (one migrations-fixes flake under parallel load, green alone), sierra 2044 and test:safety. · [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d366) |
| <a id="fjs-770"></a>FJS-770 | litestone — **The set of framework claims a policy may NAME and the set `FrontierGateGetLevel` actually GRADES by are two hand-kept lists in two files, and nothing compares them.** `FRAMEWORK_CLAIMS` in `src/core/policy.js` is what `buildClaimSet` admits; `gradeStanding` in `@frontierjs/toolbelt/gate` is what reads those fields off the principal, and litestone's gate plugin defaults to it. The comment on `FRAMEWORK_CLAIMS` states the split deliberately — the plugin is optional, so the list is about what a schema may name rather than what is installed — which is sound, and leaves the two free to drift with no artefact watching. **A field added to the grader and not to the list makes `auth().newField` a startup throw naming the model**, on a claim the framework itself grades by: loud, but wrong, and the error tells the app to declare a framework claim through `createClient({ claims })`. The reverse is harmless. Not measured against a real seventh field; the drift is read off the two sources. Found while correcting `FJS-D181`, whose body had restated the six by hand as a third copy (now removed). **[`FJS-D197`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d197) put the grader in the kit, and that did not close this row for free**: the kit exports `gradeStanding` and no list of the fields it reads, so `FRAMEWORK_CLAIMS` is still a hand copy — the fix is the kit naming its fields beside the function that reads them | 2026-10-11 | Cause: `FRAMEWORK_CLAIMS` restated the six principal fields `gradeStanding` reads as a hand copy in litestone. Fix: `@frontierjs/toolbelt/gate` exports `STANDING_FIELDS` beside the grader and `policy.js` spreads it into `FRAMEWORK_CLAIMS`. Proof: the kit spec reads `gradeStanding` through a Proxy and compares the touched fields to `STANDING_FIELDS` (a seventh field added to the grader reds it); `auth-claims.test.ts` checks `buildClaimSet` admits every one and refuses a near miss. Toolbelt 717 and litestone 6926 pass. · [policy.js:134](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js#L134) · [toolbelt gate.js:114](packages/toolbelt/src/gate/gate.js#L114) · [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d181) · [FJS-D197](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d197) |
| <a id="fjs-914"></a>FJS-914 | litestone — **Validation runs ahead of the gate, so a caller the gate refuses still learns a model's column names.** The `@guarded` half is FIXED (2026-09-05) and this row is what is left. As filed: `sortableKeysFor`/`filterableKeysFor` walked every field and excluded relation, `@computed`, `@transient`, `@edge`/`@scoped` and opaque types and nothing else, and the set reached a caller in both message branches — the list (`Sortable: …` / `Valid fields: …`) and one name at a time through `suggestKey`, which is the branch a real typo takes. Measured as `$setAuth(null)` reading ``Sortable: createdAt, email, id, notes, ssn`` off a model gated `@@gate("1.1.5.9")` that refused the same caller a legitimate read one line later. **What was done**: `_guardedNames`/`_shownSet` narrow what a refusal DISCLOSES at four sites — `checkOrderBy`, `checkWhereKeys` and both bridges (`$checkWhere`, `$checkOrderBy`, which junction's `autoFilter` answers a 400 from). Legality is untouched **deliberately**: `$checkWhere` states that validity is a question about the schema and every flavor must answer it identically, so filtering the legal set would make one caller's standing decide another's 400. The two bridges are called outside any call in progress, where `ctx.isSystem` has nothing to answer with, so they narrow for EVERY caller — the safe direction, and right, since a boundary's caller is never the system context. `test/guarded-disclosure.test.ts`, 5 tests, **3 red with `_shownSet` stubbed to identity**; suite 4334 pass / 0 fail. **What remains, and why the row stays open**: a below-gate caller still learns the ORDINARY column names, because validation runs before the gate — S3 now rather than S2, since what crosses is no longer a column `FJS-D205` rules out. Fixing it means moving the gate ahead of arg validation, which is unsized: `AccessDeniedError` is raised from several places and the reorder changes what a legitimate developer with a typo sees. **Not a defect and left alone**: naming a `@guarded` column EXACTLY is still refused in its own sentence, which already argues the inference channel — *ordering by it leaks the ordering of every row at once* | 2026-10-11 | Cause: key validation (`guardArgs`) ran before `GatePlugin`, so a caller the gate refuses read the model's ordinary column names in `Sortable:` / `Valid fields:` / `Did you mean`. Fix at the owner: `GatePlugin.onInit` publishes `ctx.gateCheck`, and `withArgValidation` answers a `ValidationError` with the gate's refusal first (read, create, update, delete by verb, `search` and `create` included). Only the failure path pays, and a caller the gate admits still hears the typo. `test/guarded-disclosure.test.ts` +8 tests (7 red with the check stubbed); `elegance-fixes` combinator test moved to `updateMany` since VERIFIED is below Lead's delete gate; litestone suite 6891 pass / 0 fail · [client.js `_shownSet`](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [test/guarded-disclosure.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/guarded-disclosure.test.ts) · [FJS-D205](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d205) · [FJS-553](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-553) · [FJS-554](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-554) |
| <a id="fjs-946"></a>FJS-946 | junction — **An `around` hook that FORGOT `next()` is indistinguishable from one that answered `null` on purpose, so a broken pipeline is a 204.** Measured: an `around` whose body never calls `next()` leaves `ctx.result` null and the call answers 204 with the method never invoked and nothing said. Split out of [`FJS-706`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-706)’s `core-5` rather than closed with it, because it is **not the same shape as the two silences beside it** — short-circuiting IS what the phase is for, and an `around` that deliberately answers `null` is a legitimate hook one character away from the broken one. So there is nothing to grade at `start()`: the question is per CALL, at run time. **The answer already exists one realm over** — `@frontierjs/toolbelt/hooks`’ `hookContext`/`answered`, which sierra and jetty both use for exactly this, and which junction does not. Adopting it changes junction’s `around` signature and therefore the shipped hook contract, which is why it is a row and not a slice | 2026-10-11 | An around that never called next() left ctx.result at its initial null, which the pipeline answered as a 204 with the method never run. Fixed at the three context builders (bridge.toContext, bridge.internal, app makeCtx) by wrapping them in toolbelt's hookContext, so result remembers whether it was ASSIGNED, and runPipeline now throws a GeneralError (500) carrying hookChainMessage when the around chain ends unanswered; an around that assigns ctx.result, null included, is untouched, and no around signature changed. Added toolbelt/hooks.d.ts for junction. Proved by test/around-forgot-next.test.ts (forgot next, swallowed error, answered null, normal next), red before the fix. · [hooks.ts](packages/junction/src/core/hooks.ts) · [toolbelt/hooks](packages/toolbelt/src/hooks) · [FJS-945](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-945) |
| <a id="fjs-2575"></a>FJS-2575 | auth · basecamp — **No way to change a signed-in account's email: User.email is admin-only by policy and auth had no request/confirm flow for a new address.** | 2026-10-10 | `account.requestEmailChange` (password re-asked, rate-limited with the other reauth methods, refused to support/mcp) mints an `emailChange` Verification carrying `userId` and mails it through `onEmailVerificationRequested`; `verifyEmail` spends it, moves the address verified, purges tokens for the old one, and tells the OLD address via `email.changed`. Basecamp settings gained the form. `test/email-change.test.ts`; basecamp `verify:screens` § a new email address |
| <a id="fjs-1024"></a>FJS-1024 | junction — **A raw route's path parameter spelled `:id` registers as a LITERAL static segment, so the route answers 405 forever and every function behind it stays correct.** Junction's router parses `{name}` (`PARAM_PATTERN` in `src/transport/router.ts`); anything else is a static segment, so `a.post('/servers/:id/enroll', …)` registers a path whose second component is the three characters `:id` and which nothing ever requests. The failure is a 405 rather than a 404 — `allowedMethods` finds the CORS `OPTIONS /*` and reports `allow: OPTIONS` — which sends a reader looking at their verb. **Silent in the way that matters**: basecamp's outpost-enrollment route shipped this way (it is `/servers/{id}/enroll` now, so that app is fixed and the class is not), with a hand-written 401-for-everything refusal, a constant-time compare, a single-use burn and a fifteen-minute window all correct, and no request could reach any of it. It was found by the first test that went down real HTTP, which is [`FJS-349`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-349)'s shape one layer out — that one was a route with no credential check whose comment claimed one; this is a credential check with no route. **The colon form is the spelling every other framework in the ecosystem uses**, so it is what a person writes from memory, and there is nothing to catch it: the router is happy to hold a static segment named `:id`, and only a request proves otherwise. What is owed is a refusal at REGISTRATION naming the brace form — a segment beginning `:` is never a path anybody meant | 2026-10-11 | Cause: Router.add parsed only {name}, so a ':id' segment registered as a literal static segment and the route answered 405 forever. Fix: parsePath in router.ts refuses any segment beginning ':' at registration (HTTP and WS paths alike), naming the route and the brace spelling. Proof: packages/junction/test/colon-param.test.ts (red before, 5 pass after); junction suite 2854 pass, typecheck clean; no app in the tree registers a colon route. fli prove drives not run: port 8110 held by another session. · [router.ts § parsePath](packages/junction/src/transport/router.ts) · [http.ts § _methodNotAllowed](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [basecamp app.ts § enrollmentRoutes](packages/basecamp/api/src/app.ts) · [FJS-349](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-349) |
| <a id="fjs-1094"></a>FJS-1094 | litestone — **A `File` column read through the plugin and the same ref passed to `fileUrl()` answer two different URLs, because three functions build a public URL from a stored ref and one of them leaves out the bucket.** Measured 2026-09-12 with a ref carrying `bucket: 'shop'`, an R2 endpoint and no `publicBase`: `FileStorage`'s `resolve()` — the path every read takes, since `autoResolve` defaults on — answers `https://acct.r2.cloudflarestorage.com/Product/7/photo/ab.png`, and `fileUrl(ref)` answers `…/shop/Product/7/photo/ab.png`. The ref stores the bucket and `resolve()` never reads it. With a `publicBase` both agree, which is the common configuration and why nothing has noticed. The third builder is `S3Provider.publicUrl`, which now encodes the key per segment ([FJS-1076](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1076)) while the other two join it raw; no keyPattern produces a key where that differs, so it is latent. One owner is the fix — the provider already knows its addressing style and is reachable from both callers — and it wants a test PAIRED across the three with a `publicBase` and without. | 2026-10-11 | Cause: three builders of a File ref's public URL — FileStoragePlugin.resolve (every autoResolve read), fileUrl/fileUrls and S3Provider.publicUrl — each joined base + key by hand; resolve left out the bucket on the endpoint path, and none but the provider encoded the key. Fix: one owner, storage/ref-url.js refUrl({publicBase, endpoint, bucket, key}) (publicBase wins; else endpoint/bucket/key; key encoded per segment), called by all four, and S3Provider._objectUrl's path-style branch. Proof: test/file-url.test.ts pairs the four with and without a publicBase, a key needing encoding, and a ref with no address (red before, green after); litestone suite 0 fail. · [file.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js) · [storage/index.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/index.js) · [providers/s3.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/providers/s3.js) |

Fix: a 'number' kind for Int/Float (money is Int) in `schema-maps.js` and a `number` refusal in `query.js` naming equals/gte/lte; DateTime stays askable. Proof: `where-operators.test.ts` (every text op × Int/Float, paired with the numeric ops still answering), matrix grid int/float/money × contains → ref, litestone.test.ts text-ops case; litestone suite green. example/basecamp verify drives not run: ports 8110/8120 held by another session. · [query.js `TEXT_OP_REFUSALS`](packages/litestone/src/core/query.js#L257) · [FJS-892](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-892) |
| <a id="fjs-1517"></a>FJS-1517 | litestone · sierra — **A `@capability` column is charged to its grant whenever the payload NAMES it, `null` and unchanged included, and a generated create form names every column `make()` seeded — so on a model with one, the generated create form is refused for everybody who does not hold that column's grant, for a field the form does not even show.** | 2026-10-11 | Cause: the create path charged a @capability column's grant for every key the payload NAMED, and a generated create form posts make()'s whole record, so a seeded null (or the column's literal default) was refused for a field the form does not show. | <a id="fjs-2403"></a>FJS-2403 | litestone — **One stored Int past 2^53 reads back as a rounded number on bun:sqlite and an exact BigInt on the wasm engine, and node:sqlite refuses the bind: the engine contract states no integer width outside safeIntegers, so the page gets a value JSON.stringify throws on and the server never produces** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: validate() graded a plain Int only for shape (isInteger), so 2^60 was bound, stored as a rounded double and read back a Number on bun:sqlite but a BigInt on the wasm engine; only @scale/@money refused the range. Fix: validate() refuses a number past Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER on an Int that is not an array, @big, @scale or @money (DEFAULT_MESSAGES.intRange names @big), and buildValidationMap flags a model with such a column so the pass runs. Proved by audit-browser-engine.test.ts (test.failing turned test; the engine-read test now inserts by raw SQL) and the litestone suite, 6800 pass. · [validate.js](packages/litestone/src/core/validate.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2549"></a>FJS-2549 | oracle — **A publicWhen with two fields is emitted as OR, while checkAnswer's finding reads it as AND, so the person approves 'public where status is open and listed is true' and a listed draft is public** | 2026-10-10 | accessFor pushed each publicWhen entry as its own read term and joined the read terms with \|\|, so publicWhen { status: open, listed: true } emitted status == 'open' \|\| listed == true while checkAnswer's finding, the sentence the person approves, said *and*. The entries now conjoin into one parenthesized term, (status == 'open' && listed == true). Proved by oracle.test.js 'a publicWhen of two fields opens a row only when both hold (FJS-2549)': hiring's Job with a bool listed, read by a visitor on a real client, returns only the open listed job — red before the fix with the listed draft and the unlisted open job also returned. · [emit.js:348](packages/oracle/src/emit.js) · [answer.js:338](packages/oracle/src/answer.js) · [FJS-1881](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1881) |

CEILING in audit-browser-globals.test.ts lowered 36 → 22; what is left is client.js lock owner and writer id (FJS-2400), encryption (FJS-2398), tenancy/databases (FJS-2399), backup (FJS-2401). | <a id="fjs-2542"></a>FJS-2542 | conduit — **TargetAuth cannot express Basic, a secret in the body, a minted JWT, an OAuth service-account token or SigV4, so each of the next connectors needs a conduit-core edit — the signer seam FJS-D891 rules is unbuilt** | 2026-10-10 | Built the signer seam FJS-D891 ruled: one auth variant, { type: 'signed', signer, ref }, naming a function in ConduitOptions.signers (a name, since a descriptor is stored as JSON). The two open questions: the four old types stay, and a minted credential's expiry is the resolver's withCache TTL, which a 401 already invalidates. | <a id="fjs-2536"></a>FJS-2536 | basecamp — **Removing an Environment leaves its Apps serving on their machines** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: @@softDelete(cascade) stamps an Environment's Apps and tells no machine; only apps.remove retired one. The delete stays the cascade, so restoring the Environment brings its Apps back, stopped — calling apps.remove per App would have left them deleted (services.test.ts pins that shape). | <a id="fjs-2516"></a>FJS-2516 | junction — **A row written already due waits for the next commitment sweep: an Action created to run now (a lead's push to the CRM) starts up to a minute later, because the sweep is the only thing that dispatches a fire and nothing kicks it after the write — where the outbox, the same shape, is kicked after every committing call** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: only the cron sweep dispatched a commitment fire, so a row written already due (an Action due now) waited up to a minute. | <a id="fjs-d688"></a>FJS-D688 | junction — **Is a raw app.ws route the door with junction's protocol taken OFF it (no protocol key), or an opt-in beside the browser-socket default, and which of limits, upgrade, apiPrefix and a per-socket slot does it carry?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D688`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d688): **A** — `app.ws` IS the raw door; no `protocol` key. `connected` and the `{type:'error'}` frames belong to the handler set carrying `SESSION_SOCKET` (the marker `FJS-1830` already reads). Third argument `{ limits?, upgrade? }`: `limits` overrides `config.http.ws` field by field for this route (a refused frame on a raw route closes 1008 with the code as reason rather than sending a frame the peer cannot read), `upgrade(req)` returns a `Response` to refuse or nothing to proceed, runs before `server.upgrade`. `app.ws` applies `apiPrefix` as `app.post` does; per-socket state is `ctx.locals`, the name service contexts already use; a handler throw goes to `onError`. | <a id="fjs-d685"></a>FJS-D685 | litestone — **Does a write fold the app's global filter (and plugin read filters)? Today update/remove/delete and their Many forms reach rows the filter hides, so a per-tenant function filter does not scope writes** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D685`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d685): **A** — Fold both scopes into `update`, `updateMany`, `remove`, `removeMany`, `delete` and `deleteMany` (move them from `WALKS` to `NOT_RESTORE`). The filter is then one predicate for every verb that reaches a row, matching "every path that reaches a row applies every rule that guards it". The six cells promote to `on`; an update/delete of a hidden row answers `null`/`count: 0` like an absent id. `asSystem()` still does not lift it, so a job that maintains rows the app filter hides must say so with a flag. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d597"></a>FJS-D597 | junction — **When a write lands on a model another model's read policy reaches (Share under Page's shares.some, Page under Block's check(page)), does the Data boundary regrade the dependent ROWS per recipient, does the write announce a count-only 'changed' on every dependent model's channels, or does the app announce it?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D597`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d597): **B** — announce at the model level: litestone derives `db.$readDependents(accessor)`, the models whose read policy reaches this one, transitively, from the schema; the tap adds a count-only `changed` (gate mode, the frame bulk writes already send, `FJS-307`) on each dependent model's channels. | <a id="fjs-d596"></a>FJS-D596 | junction — **Is a channel bound to the row it is for, so joining and staying are graded at the Data boundary, or is a bound channel's every presence frame and send graded per recipient, or does admission stay the app's call with an evict that tells the leaver?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D596`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d596): **A** — bind the channel to a row: `channels({ rooms: { 'page:{id}': 'Page' } })` names which model a channel name stands for. A `join` on a matching name asks `$readAs` for that row and the principal (refused: no join, and `join` says so); after each committed write to a model the bound model's read policy reaches, the manager re-asks once per (room, cohort) and evicts the members who lost the row, sending each of them a `presence:sync` with no members, so their screen empties. Presence and `send` then need no per-frame grading, since membership stays true. The reach set (which models a read policy depends on) is the litestone fact `FJS-1738` also needs, so it is built once for both. Adds one option, and amends `FJS-D175`'s clause to say that a BOUND channel's join is graded. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d581"></a>FJS-D581 | junction — **What refuses a write made inside a read: true custom method — a write-refusing client handed over for read calls, a synchronous write guard Litestone grows, or a tap that only reports?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D581`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d581): **A** — Junction hands a read call a client that refuses writes: for an `isRead` custom call, `ctx.locals.db` is a Proxy whose model accessors throw on `create`/`update`/`remove`/`*Many`/`upsert`/`transition` by name, one owner in `withLitestoneDb` where the client is already scoped. No Litestone change and no new word. It covers only the handed-over client: `db.asSystem()`, an imported root client and a raw `$db` statement walk past it. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d568"></a>FJS-D568 | litestone — **How does a sealing model freeze a column from a LATER move than the seal — a move-scoped argument on @immutable, a second attribute, or no language change?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D568`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d568): **A** — `@immutable(after: <move>)`: the existing word with a scope. The column is writable until the named move's target, frozen from there and from every state reachable from it (`sealedStates`' own closure, generalized in `core/seal.js` to take a move name). Bare `@immutable` keeps its two meanings (create on a plain model, the seal on a sealing one). | <a id="fjs-d548"></a>FJS-D548 | junction — **Who owns a socket whose cookie bearer changes after the upgrade — the transport closing it, the client not sending that call over it, or neither?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D548`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d548): **A** — the transport closes the socket, and the client cycles it. `ctx.setCookie` on an HTTP response that changes a cookie the request carried closes every connection whose upgrade carried that name at that old value, through `disconnectSession`'s path (`channels.ts:781`: blunt by design, the reconnect re-resolves). Only a holder of the old value can trigger that close, so it closes nobody else's socket. The redeem half needs the client to know: the response carries a signal (one new wire name, e.g. `x-fjs-credential: changed`), and the client cycles its socket as `setToken` already does. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |

| <a id="fjs-2502"></a>FJS-2502 | litestone — **The generated system client is registered as the type `createClient()` returns, and it lists the client's members by hand, so eleven of them — `$retain` among them — are type errors in every app that regenerates its types, the scaffold's own retention job first.** | 2026-10-10 | The system audience's `LitestoneClient` now extends the package's own for every NAMED member it does not redeclare — `Pick<…, Exclude<NamedKeys<…>, declared>>`, named because the package's carries `[model: string]: unknown` and `Omit` over an index signature drops every name — so a member added to `index.d.ts` reaches a registered app with no second list to keep. The client audience is never registered and is unchanged. `registered-client.test.ts`'s consumer now calls `$retain()` and `$lock()` through `createClient()`; with the extend removed, tsc names both. 2,124 green across the seven typegen suites; maidtech's `bun run typecheck` clean after `db:types` · [typegen.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/typegen.js) · [index.d.ts](packages/litestone/src/index.d.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2501"></a>FJS-2501 | sierra · ui — **A held value outside a `suggested` (or never-held `open`) value set is marked unavailable and the field invalid, so `<Form>` refuses to save a record the Data boundary would accept — every edit of it, whatever field changed.** | 2026-10-10 | `withUnavailable` reads the binding's strength, which `x-values` already carried: a held value is disabled and marked `unavailable` (and so invalid in `<Form>`) only where the Data boundary refuses it — always under `required`, under `open` only when the source holds the row and the set narrows it out; otherwise it is pinned as an ordinary `(not listed)` option. A failed label read grades `open` as refused. [FJS-D225](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d225)'s *refuses the write regardless* now holds where it is applied. Two tests in `value-sets.test.js`, each beside the refused twin, both failing with the strength dropped at the call site; sierra 2,021 green. Driven in Chrome on maidtech's moved account 25: contact 4713 (`source` `google`, not on its list) saved an edit to another field with the code kept, where before it sent no request; contact 4689's source changed through the picker to `yelp` · [FJS-D225](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d225) · [resource.js withUnavailable](packages/sierra/src/resource/resource.js) · [Form.mesa optionsFor](packages/ui/components/forms/Form.mesa) · [valuesets.js](packages/litestone/src/core/valuesets.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2488"></a>FJS-2488 | ui — **On the terminal target DatePicker's calendar paints one day per row and its weekday labels run together, because the month is a CSS grid the terminal does not read** | 2026-10-10 | The month is a table: thead holds the weekday th (scope col), tbody a tr per week and a td per day, so the terminal lays a week as a row and each day takes an equal share under its label through its existing tr/td rule, with no runtime change. The candidate in this row (a week div with display: contents) was priced and cut: a div of buttons is a wrapping flow where [ 1 ] and [ 28 ] differ in width, so the columns drift, and the weekday spans still join into one run. The browser keeps its one grid (thead, tbody and tr display: contents, th/td padding 0), and the band's row-edge and preview rules now find a row by td:first-child/:last-child and td/tr :has() siblings instead of nth-child(7n) over a flat list that counted the labels. Proved by verify:tui (the week holding the 14th reads seven days on one row, each at its label's column; red on the old markup) and the ui datepicker specs, two offsets now read cellIndex, plus new pitch and row-edge rounding assertions (red with display: contents dropped, and with the old nth-last-child selector); ui suite 1320, example verify:ui 35. Not proved: the hover preview's first-day rule across a row boundary has no assertion · [DatePicker.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/DatePicker.mesa) |
