Proved by layout.spec.js (the root computes `stable`); css 539 pass, example verify:ui 35/35. · [reset.css](packages/css/src/foundation/reset.css) |
| <a id="fjs-2487"></a>FJS-2487 | mesa · sierra — **On the terminal target a route taller than the screen is squeezed into it: its rows paint over each other and nothing scrolls** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled scroll, not clip (the owner's 'okay next!' left the call to the session; the browser's behavior, and clip is its subset). A mount now goes into the renderer's document, documentOf(r) in runtime-terminal.js: an engine ScrollBoxRenderable the size of the screen, unfocusable, viewportCulling off because a culled node reports a stale place. A focus change is revealed after the frame lays it out (a postProcessFn: the least scroll that shows it, a hidden link by its run), PageUp/PageDown move a screen less one row through onKey, and the wheel is the engine's. Proved by specs/scroll.spec.mjs 12/12 (fixtures/Long.mesa), 9 deliberate breaks all red; the terminal drive 305; verify:tui reads /orders/'s 'Unable to connect.' and 'Placed between' whole at 30 rows (red with the mount on the root) and Tabs a day into view; mesa vitest 2283, typecheck clean · [shell.js](packages/sierra/src/terminal/shell.js) · [runtime-terminal.js](packages/mesa/src/runtime-terminal.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2318"></a>FJS-2318 | litestone · sierra — **The browser worker bundles the whole .lite parser and never parses — about 30% of the worker on every page load** | 2026-10-10 | The parser is out of the worker's load path. pointColumns/pointColumnNames moved to ddl.js and inferFromFk to schema-maps.js, which also breaks three import cycles. createClient imports parser.js lazily, only when there is text or a path to parse. A split bundle of the worker loads 473 KB instead of 657 KB (164 KB vs 210 KB gzip). Vite builds a worker as an IIFE, which inlines a lazy import, so Sierra builds the worker with its own plugin list: localDbWorkerPlugins (build/local-db-plugin.js) resolves litestone's public ./parser export and swaps in a stub that throws by name. On example the worker went from 659 KB / 205 KB gzip to 467 KB / 153 KB, and the offline shell from 961 kB to 920 kB over the wire. An ES worker without the stub was measured and rejected: it grew the shell by 9 kB and still precached the parser. So was a `#parser` package condition in litestone with a stub under a custom condition: Vite 8 honors it in an IIFE worker, but a worker plugin can set it only by mutating an array the page config shares, and a top-level condition stubs the page as well. Proved by test/engine-seam.test.ts (parser.js is not statically reachable from worker.js, client.js or ddl.js; a mutant fails and names the importer), litestone test:browser (text and parsed schemas both open), and example verify:shell 30/30 in a worktree on private ports, including that SQL answered offline. · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [schema-maps.js](packages/litestone/src/core/schema-maps.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2480"></a>FJS-2480 | litestone — **A row moved to another @@extensible scope must send its blob in the same write; the stored blob is not re-projected against the new scope's declarations** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: writeData refused a write changing the scope column without the blob, where FJS-D592 rules that the move re-mirrors the row. Fix: remirrorMovedRow (client.js) reads the stored blob off the one row the where reaches and rebuilds the mirror against the new scope's declarations; the blob is not rewritten, so the audit trail records no change to it. A move without the blob reaching several rows is refused by name (one SET carries one mirror), and the upsert fast path declines such a move so the slow path's update() takes it. Proved by test/extensible-scope.test.ts (14 tests; the move and the upsert move red before: refused with send 'props'); litestone bun run test 6765 pass 0 fail. · [FJS-D592](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d592) · [FJS-1758](#fjs-1758) · [test](packages/litestone/test/extensible-scope.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2446"></a>FJS-2446 | junction — **Three plugin surfaces attach by assignment, not app.claim: app.mail, app.channels, app.addOpenApiPaths, so a prior claimant of the name is overwritten in silence, the failure claim exists to refuse (Invariant 5)** | 2026-10-10 | mailerPlugin, channels() and openapi() each assigned their app slot (app.mail, app.channels, app.addOpenApiPaths), so a prior claimant was overwritten silently; openapi also re-assigned its own helper to wrap the cache reset. Each now goes through app.claim (assigning only on a bare test app with no claim, as backfill does), and openapi defines one helper that resets the spec cache and claims it once. test/plugin-contract.test.ts § battery slots go through app.claim: three refusals (red before, green after) plus the openapi cache-reset path; junction suite 2798 pass, typecheck clean. · [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) · [mail/index.ts](packages/junction/src/mail/index.ts) · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [openapi/index.ts](packages/junction/src/plugins/openapi/index.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2447"></a>FJS-2447 | junction — **The protocol half of the call-header allow-list is two constants, PROTOCOL_HEADERS for CORS and PROTOCOL_CALL_HEADERS for the frame merge, where the bridge-index entry promises one declaration with two readers** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: the CORS preflight list (PROTOCOL_HEADERS in middleware.ts) and the socket frame-merge allow-list (PROTOCOL_CALL_HEADERS in channels.ts) were two hand-kept constants. Fix: one declaration in core/context.ts, PROTOCOL_CALL_HEADERS (both readers) plus PROTOCOL_HTTP_ONLY_HEADERS (X-Service-Method, which the frame merge must never accept); cors() reads both, channels lower-cases the first. Pinned by the 'ONE declaration' case in test/client.test.ts (also checks the made-at/sent-at constants agree). Junction suite 2794 pass, typecheck clean; fli prove drive failures are open rows FJS-2131, FJS-1484, FJS-1805 or a port held by another session. · [middleware.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/middleware.ts) · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2448"></a>FJS-2448 | junction — **The Link header hand-spells $offset and $limit instead of directiveParams(), a second URL spelling of a directive** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: ctx.paginate's buildUrl hand-spelled `$offset=…&$limit=…` and re-encoded the request's other params through URLSearchParams, so a `$orderBy` already on the request came back as `%24orderBy` in next/prev. Fix: it parses the search with toolbelt's parseQueryString, drops the two window keys and writes the window with directiveParams + encodeQueryString (README example order follows the table: $limit then $offset). Proof: new test/index.test.ts 'carries the request's other directives and filters into next' (red before, green after), junction suite 2793 pass, typecheck clean. · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) · [http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2450"></a>FJS-2450 | junction — **PublicUrlError is mapped to 400 by hand in the webhooks route although the class already carries status 400 and the raw-route path runs toFrameworkError** | 2026-10-10 | The webhooks POST route caught PublicUrlError and answered 400 by hand, though the class carries status 400 and toFrameworkError (via httpStatusOf) already maps it on the raw-route path. Removed the try/catch and the import so the throw reaches the one owner; test/webhook-targets.test.ts now also pins the 400 body naming the private address. junction suite 2792 pass, typecheck clean. · [errors.ts](packages/junction/src/core/errors.ts) · [webhooks/index.ts](packages/junction/src/plugins/webhooks/index.ts) · [public-url.ts](packages/junction/src/core/public-url.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2459"></a>FJS-2459 | toolbelt — **A count is read with Number() rather than the round-trip rule the value half uses, so ?$limit=true answers one row, ?$limit=[] answers none, ?$limit=0x10 sixteen and a quoted 5 five, each 200** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: limit and offset were read with Number(), so true, [] and 0x10 became 1, 0 and 16 and each answered 200. Fix: toolbelt directives reads a count with countOf (a typed finite number, or decimal digits only), and invalidDirectives now reports a count outside that grammar beside the flags, so the bridge's existing refusal answers 400 naming the directive. A quoted 5 is still read as 5, since the table cannot tell it from a string 5 an internal caller sends. A garbage limit used to fall back to the default and is now refused; two tests that asserted the fallback were updated. Proof: audit-directives-vectors test.failing is now a passing test, toolbelt spec rows added, junction suite and typecheck clean. · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) · [query.js](packages/toolbelt/src/query/query.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2460"></a>FJS-2460 | toolbelt · junction — **A repeated or list-spelled scalar directive is dropped to the default in silence: ?$limit=5&$limit=6 and a socket bag { $limit: [5, 6] } both answer the default page with a 200, while ?$limit[]=5 answers 5** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: the bridge refused unknown and non-flag $ values but read a list-valued scalar directive through Number([5,6]), which is NaN, so it dropped to the default page with a 200. Fix: toolbelt/directives gains listedDirectives(params), which reports the count, text and flag directives and $first/$wrap given an array ($select, $populate, $orderBy are lists by nature and are exempt). bridge.refuseBadDirectives refuses them by name on HTTP and the internal/socket path alike. Proved by audit-directives-vectors.test.ts (test.failing dropped; covers ?$limit=5&$limit=6, ?$limit[]=5 and an internal { $limit: [5,6] }), a directives.spec.js row, junction suite 2792 pass and typecheck clean. · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2461"></a>FJS-2461 | toolbelt · junction — **A text directive that looks numeric is dropped in silence because the typed wire retypes it before the table reads it: ?$after=123 answers the ordinary page where ?$after=zzz is refused, and ?$asOf=20260101 answers unfiltered, the $search=603 class of FJS-2333 on two more rows** | 2026-10-10 | asText (the reader for $after, $search, $asOf) accepted only strings, so the wire's typed number/boolean (?$after=123, ?$asOf=20260101) read as not-asked and the ordinary page answered; it now returns the text of a finite number or boolean, which the number rule round-trips. Proved by audit-directives-vectors.test.ts (test.failing turned test) and a toolbelt directives.spec row; junction, toolbelt and sierra suites green. · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) · [query.js](packages/toolbelt/src/query/query.js) · [body.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/body.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2462"></a>FJS-2462 | junction — **?$select= answers 200 with rows that have no columns: normalizeSelect of an empty string is {} and litestone selects nothing** | 2026-10-10 | `parseQuery` turned `?$select=` into `{}` via `normalizeSelect`, and Litestone reads `{}` as select nothing, so the list answered 200 with column-less rows. `parseQuery` now treats an empty select map as absent, as `$search=` already is. Proved by `audit-directives-vectors.test.ts` (the `test.failing` is now a `test`); junction suite 2792 pass, typecheck clean. · [query-values.ts](packages/junction/src/core/query-values.ts) · [litestone.ts](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2465"></a>FJS-2465 | toolbelt · junction — **orderByPair reads $orderBy[0][name]=desc as { key: name, dir: desc } and marks the header, while normalizeOrderBy and the Data boundary refuse the same URL as field 0 with a 400; the kit comment says bracket notation yields a 0 key, but the kit's own encoder emits []** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: orderByPair descended through a numeric key on any object, so `$orderBy[0][name]=desc` (parsed to `{'0':{name:'desc'}}`, where `k[0]` names a member and `k[]` is the append) marked `name` while normalizeOrderBy and the Data boundary refuse column `0`. Fix: the numeric-key descent now applies to a real array only (directives.js orderByPair); a plain object answers its first key as the boundary reads it. Proof: audit-directives-vectors.test.ts test.failing turned into a passing test, toolbelt directives spec rows moved to the boundary's reading, ui Table browser drive gains an array push and a bracket-indexed push that marks no column (1301 passing). · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) · [query-values.ts](packages/junction/src/core/query-values.ts) · [query.js](packages/toolbelt/src/query/query.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2466"></a>FJS-2466 | junction — **bridge.internal({ query: { $limit: -1 } }) is not refused: clampPagination floors it to 0, so the in-process and socket paths answer no rows with a 200 where HTTP answers 400 $limit must be zero or greater** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: bridge.internal split a $-spelled query into directives but never ran refuseUnservableWindow, which toContext runs, so a negative $limit reached clampPagination and was floored to 0 (200, no rows) on the in-process and socket paths. Fix: internal merges fromQuery and opts.directives and runs the same refusal on the result (bridge.ts). Proof: audit-directives-vectors.test.ts test.failing turned into a passing test; junction suite 2792 pass, typecheck clean. · [bridge.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/bridge.ts) · [directives.ts](packages/junction/src/core/directives.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2476"></a>FJS-2476 | toolbelt · junction — **A directive flag given text that is no flag spelling (?$withDeleted=yes, =on, =2) reads as false and answers 200 without the rows asked for, where an unknown $ name is refused by name** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: asBool/truthy answered false for any text outside true/1, so ?$withDeleted=yes\|on\|2 answered 200 without the rows asked for, while an unknown $ name was refused. Fix: one flagOf grammar in toolbelt/directives (true/1/bare, false/0) shared by the directive flags and transportFlags; invalidDirectives() reports beside unknownDirectives; parseDirectives drops such a value rather than guessing false; the bridge's refuseBadDirectives (renamed from refuseUnknownDirectives) refuses it by name with 400. Proved by the directives spec row and audit-directives-vectors (?$withDeleted=yes\|on\|2 is 400, =0 is false); toolbelt, junction and sierra suites green. · [directives.js](packages/toolbelt/src/directives/directives.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2477"></a>FJS-2477 | litestone — **litestone's bun run test is red at HEAD by the sql-idents ratchet: cross-process.js has 11 hand-quoted ${…} identifiers against a CEILING of 9** | 2026-10-10 | cross-process.js wrote the events table's name as a hand-quoted "${EVENTS_TABLE}" at 11 sites against a ceiling of 9. The name is now quoted once through quoteIdent into a module constant (and the _at index name inline), so the file has 0 sites and its entry is removed from CEILING in sql-idents.test.ts; the SQL text is byte-identical. Proved by test/sql-idents.test.ts (45 pass) and test/cross-process.test.ts. · [sql-idents.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/sql-idents.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2478"></a>FJS-2478 | junction — **A raw route that throws a 4xx refusal is logged at ERROR as 'http error': HttpTransport's onError fires for every throw before errorResponse maps it, so a 404/409/429 a handler means is error-level noise beside a real 500** | 2026-10-10 | The transport announces every throw to onError before errorResponse maps it, and the app's onError logged each at ERROR 'http error' and emitted 'error', so a raw route's 404/409/429 read as a failure beside a real 500. app.ts onError now returns for any thrown value toFrameworkError grades below 500; a 5xx logs and emits as before, and the transport's own contract (onError for every throw) is untouched. Proved by test/raw-route-refusal-log.test.ts (red before: two ERROR lines for a 404 and a 409) and the junction suite, 2791 pass; fli prove's example verify and basecamp verify reds match FJS-2121 and FJS-2162. · [app.ts](packages/junction/src/core/app.ts) · [http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2481"></a>FJS-2481 | litestone — **migrate dev over a pending BLOCKED file writes a second migration for the same change, so each re-run adds another file that is also BLOCKED** | 2026-10-10 | cmdDev asked drift for the pending files and then created anyway, so a pending BLOCKED file (unapplied, refused by apply) was not history and the next create re-diffed the live database into a second identical file. cmdDev now refuses with exit 1 before create when unresolvedBlocks(drift.pending) is non-empty, naming each file. | <a id="fjs-2409"></a>FJS-2409 | cli — **blocks scores a blocker by how many rows it holds, never by how bad they are, so a ruling holding up an S2 security hole ranks below one holding up two S3 picker rows: D848 (FJS-2232, cross-tenant credential leak) is 44 and D674 (FJS-2021, FJS-2130) is 55, and FJS-1368, the one row between the register and FJS-009, is 40 at rank 83** | 2026-10-10 | next.js counted blockers by rows held, so one S2 behind a ruling scored below two S3s. | <a id="fjs-1433"></a>FJS-1433 | ui · sierra — **A generated create form still offers a @@transitions column as an ordinary enum select, so every state but the entry is a choice the Data boundary refuses** | 2026-10-10 | Re-probed: field-rules.js marks an x-transitions column (rule.transitions) and formFieldList skips it from the generated set unless only names it, so Form's no-children path (resource.formFields at Form.mesa:314) no longer offers the enum select. Proved by packages/sierra/test/machine-column.test.js (leaves the machine column out of the generated set; only: names it; a model with no machine unchanged), sierra suite 2019 green. | <a id="fjs-2201"></a>FJS-2201 | toolbelt · ui — **glow is quadratic on one long line: a 100k-character line takes 10–21 s to paint** | 2026-10-10 | Four rules rescanned a word run from every start inside it (`([\w]+)\(`, `([\w]+)\.`, yaml `([\w ]+):`, json `(".+"):`). The first three now start only at a run boundary (`\b` / `(?<![\w ])`), same matches; the json key is `("(?:[^"\\]\|\\.)*")(?=:)` after a `(?<!\\)`, so a key is its own token instead of the span to the last `":`. 100k chars now paint in ~30 ms in every language. Proved by glow.spec.js (timing bound plus text round trip, and the key-token row) and `fli prove` (css code). · [glow.js:153](packages/toolbelt/src/glow/glow.js#L153) · [Code.mesa:29](packages/ui/components/display/Code.mesa#L29) · [CodeInput.mesa:115](packages/ui/components/forms/CodeInput.mesa#L115) |
| <a id="fjs-266"></a>FJS-266 | repo — **The framework tells an app to run Biome and runs nothing itself.** `FJS-D32` adopted a linter and `@frontierjs/config` ships the rule set every scaffolded app extends, but this repo does not extend it — a framework that lints itself with nothing while telling its users to lint has two answers to one question, which is the shape Invariant 4 exists to prevent. It was not turned on because the number was measured first: Biome's `recommended` gives **7,249** findings on this tree, and the shipped curated set gives **~600**, of which 123 are `noUnusedImports` and 25 are `noGlobalIsNan`. That is a countable cleanup with a direction, not the taste argument `IDEAS/tooling-decisions.md` 1 was blocked on. Work: add the devDependency and a root `biome.json` extending the package, clear the findings, then add a `lint` phase to `scripts/ci.mjs` — in that order, since a phase added first is a phase everyone learns to skip. One unexplained finding to resolve on the way: **Biome cannot parse `packages/mesa/test/runtime.test.js`** | 2026-10-10 | The repo now lints itself: @biomejs/biome 2.5.8 is a root devDependency and a root biome.json extends @frontierjs/config (whose own biome.json became root:false, or Biome refuses two roots), excluding only what is vendored or specification (vendor, mockup, .jetty-cache, html, svelte). The measured number had drifted — 4,218 findings, 2,740 of them vendored bundles — and was cleared to zero by autofix (unused imports and variables, parseInt radix, hasOwn, Number.isNaN where the operand was already a number), hand edits (void returns, a catch parameter assigned, a duplicate key, dead private field) and 53 biome-ignore comments each carrying its reason. The shipped set gained four principled loosenings with README reasons: useIterableCallbackReturn skips forEach, noEmptyInterface and noImplicitAnyLet off, folder patterns without the trailing /** (peer range now >=2.2.0). The unexplained parse finding was a duplicate $$eachBlock import in mesa/test/runtime.test.js (and a stray CSS fragment in mesa/example/index.html). scripts/ci.mjs has a lint phase (biome lint --error-on-warnings, 2.6s, fast tier) that passes. Proved by the suites of config, toolbelt, css, mesa, sierra, junction, litestone, caravan, auth, conduit, mcp, orion, outpost, jetty, email-kit, ui, basecamp and vscode, and the example verify:offline/widget/jobs/cart/pay drives; autofix broke css's page-injected scripts and two sierra destructures, caught by those suites and reverted. · [packages/config/biome.json](packages/config/biome.json) · [IDEAS/tooling-decisions.md](IDEAS/tooling-decisions.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1839"></a>FJS-1839 | auth — **GET /auth/email/verify?token= consumes the single-use token on a GET and answers the whole SessionContext, so a mail scanner's prefetch burns the link before the person clicks it** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: the verify route was a GET that spent the single-use token and answered the whole SessionContext, so a mail scanner's prefetch burned the link. Fix per FJS-D616: it is POST /auth/email/verify with the token in the body, answering { ok: true }, and a GET there is 405; the mailed link lands on the web page, which posts it (the client's verifyEmail now POSTs, the scaffold, install and README mail links point at the web page, and the dead queryText reader is gone). Proved by test/audit-routes-email-verify-get.test.ts (test.failing flipped to test), routes.test.ts, client-auth.test.ts and the auth suite (559 pass); example verify:users and verify:oauth green, example verify and basecamp verify red for open rows FJS-1642/FJS-1376 and a port held by another session. · [plugin.ts](packages/auth/plugin.ts) · [auth.ts](packages/auth/auth.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1838"></a>FJS-1838 | auth — **confirmTotp is a six-digit oracle: no attempt ceiling, no limiter bucket, and no trail entry when a code is refused** | 2026-10-10 | confirmTotp had no ceiling, no limiter and no trail entry: the service never called reauthenticate and the provider threw a bare ReauthenticationFailedError on every miss. The service now takes the shared reauthentication bucket first (429 at login's 10 / 15 min), and the provider counts misses per pending enrollment, writes totp.confirm.failed to the trail on each, and deletes the pending credential at loginChallengeAttempts so the next try is setupTotp, which asks for the password. The count is in memory, not a column: the only caller is a session already holding the account and the service limiter is process-local too. Proved by the three former test.failing rows (audit-services-reauth-limit x2, audit-data-token-lifecycle x1) now plain tests, auth suite 559 pass, and example verify:users. · [auth.ts](packages/auth/auth.ts) · [services.ts](packages/auth/services.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1125"></a>FJS-1125 | litestone · cli — **`createTestEnv` types every table as `unknown`, so a test in a scaffolded app casts `env.system` and `actingAs()` to `any` to call a verb.** `TestEnv.system`, `.db` and `actingAs()` are the untyped `LitestoneClient` from `index.d.ts`, whose table accessors are an `unknown` index signature; the typed client an app has is `db/schema.d.ts`, written by `bun run db:types`, and `createTestEnv` is not generic over it. Measured 2026-09-13: `fli new --auth`'s `api/test/access.test.ts` fails `fli typecheck` with 13 errors (`TS18046 'env.system.user' is of type 'unknown'`) until it casts, which is what basecamp's `api/test/*.test.ts` already do (`env.system as any`, eleven times in `rollback.test.ts`). The scaffold cannot import the generated type instead: `db/schema.d.ts` does not exist until `db:types` runs, and a CI checkout does not run it. A type parameter on `createTestEnv` (and on `@frontierjs/testing`'s re-export) would let a test name the app's client and drop the cast; the first test every new app gets currently teaches `as any`. | 2026-10-10 | Cause: TestEnv's db, system, actingAs() and atLevel() were the hand-written LitestoneClient, whose every table is an unknown index signature, and createTestEnv took no type argument. Fix: TestEnv, ArrangeTools, TestPhases and createTestEnv in litestone's testing.d.ts take TClient defaulting to RegisteredClient, as createClient does, so a db/schema.d.ts the app includes types env.system and actingAs() with no call-site change and createTestEnv<Db>() names a client when nothing registers. The scaffolded api/test/access.test.ts drops both 'as any' casts: it references db/schema.d.ts, and an app with tests gets typecheck = 'bun run db:types && fli typecheck' so a clone has the file. Proved by litestone test/test-env-typed.test.ts (tsc over the generator's real output: registered, explicit, and unregistered-stays-unknown) and cli test/app-config.test.js; typecheck baselines unchanged (litestone 56, basecamp 12). The scaffold phase could not run end to end here: it fails earlier in auth:install on an installed litestone that rejects driver 'trail'. · [testing.d.ts `TestEnv`](packages/litestone/src/testing.d.ts) · [project/new.md](packages/cli/commands/project/new.md) |
| <a id="fjs-940"></a>FJS-940 | junction — **A key inside a `junction.config.js` section is still stashed and read by nothing, and the interfaces cannot be the list that would catch it.** [`FJS-939`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-939) closed the TOP-LEVEL half — a section nothing reads is refused by name at `start()`. A key one level down is not, so `middleware: { helmet: true, csrfs: true }` and `caravan: { pollIntervel: 1000 }` still merge silently. **Refusing them is not the same shape as refusing a section, and the reason is measured**: the TypeScript interfaces are erased at runtime AND have already drifted from what reads them — `basecamp` declares `middleware.cors.credentials`, which `JunctionMiddlewareConfig.cors` does not name and `applyConfiguredCors` reads and honours (the interface was corrected in `FJS-939`; the drift is the finding). So a nested check means hand-keeping a key list per section beside an interface nobody updates, which is the second origin `FJS-D199` exists to remove. **What would make it decidable is one owner per section for *what keys does this have***, read by the reader AND the check the way `JUNCTION_SECTIONS` now is — six sections, so six lists, and the subsystem that consumes each one is the only honest place for it. **Measured 2026-09-13**: [`FJS-D256`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d256) gave `middleware:` and `plugins:` that owner for what the FRAMEWORK declares — `loadConfig` maps each key one at a time and `test/config-surface.test.ts` grades both directions — but a caller's misspelled key in any section is still dropped in silence, which is this row | 2026-10-10 | A key inside a `junction.config.js` section was merged in silence because no list of a section's keys existed that the reader and the check shared. Per FJS-D483 each reader now owns its list: `MIDDLEWARE_CONFIG_KEYS` (which `loadConfig` loops over), `PLUGINS_CONFIG_KEYS` and `SERVICES_CONFIG_KEYS` in `config/index.ts`, and a plugin states `Plugin.configKeys` for the section of its name (caravan's `CARAVAN_CONFIG_KEYS`). `unknownKeys` is checked beside `unknownSections` in `applyConfigFile`, so one boot reports `csrfs` → `csrf` and `pollIntervel` → `pollInterval` by name. `JunctionCaravanConfig` had drifted from the reader (five keys missing) and is corrected. Proved by `test/authoring-keys.test.ts` (typo refused, correct spelling boots), `test/config-surface.test.ts` (list = interface), caravan's `junction-integration.test.ts` (list = keys the reader reads, junction refuses `pollIntervel`; red with `configKeys` stubbed), junction 2789 and caravan 333 green, `example verify:jobs` boots; example and basecamp configs load clean. `example verify` fails at FJS-1642 (open at HEAD); basecamp `verify` found :8120 held by another session. `app`, `attachments` and `conduit` stay unchecked (FJS-2483). · [config/index.ts](packages/junction/src/config/index.ts) · [FJS-939](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-939) · [FJS-D199](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d199) · [FJS-706](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-706) |

| <a id="fjs-2315"></a>FJS-2315 | litestone — **An update that removes a ref from a File[] without adding a file never deletes the removed file from storage** | 2026-10-10 | FileStorage.onBeforeUpdate picked its stash fields by items.some(isFileValue), so update({ photos: [a] }) over a stored [a, b] stashed nothing and b stayed in the bucket. | <a id="fjs-2412"></a>FJS-2412 | litestone — **A statement an operator stored cannot be run read-only: raw sql routes on its first word, so a stored report opening WITH runs on the write connection, and WITH x AS (…) DELETE writes through asSystem()** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: raw sql picks its connection by the statement's first word, so stored text had no way to say it may not write, and a WITH … DELETE ran on the writer. Fix: sql.read (client.js _runRawRead) takes a tag or (text, params[]) and runs with PRAGMA query_only ON on the write connection, set and restored inside one synchronous call — not the reader, which IS the writer for :memory: and inside a transaction. A write throws an error naming sql.read; refused off asSystem() under access rules like sql. Proved by three tests in litestone.test.ts (CTE select, CTE delete refused, in-tx, access-rule refusal); named params left out. · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |

| <a id="fjs-2470"></a>FJS-2470 | litestone — **groupBy's interval week has one meaning — a Monday-start %Y-W%W bucket, split in two at every year boundary — so a business whose week starts Sunday cannot ask for its own weeks, and no week is labelled by the day it starts** | 2026-10-10 | The week unit was STRFTIME('%Y-W%W'): Monday-start, labelled 2025-W52 / 2026-W00 so a week across New Year was two buckets, and no Sunday start existed. | <a id="fjs-2333"></a>FJS-2333 | junction · toolbelt — **A $search the query wire reads as a number is dropped, and the list answers every row, 200: ?$search=603 from curl or junction/testing's .query() returns the whole table** | 2026-10-10 | The $search reader in toolbelt's directive table was `asText`, so a bare `?$search=603` (typed to the number 603 by the query wire) or `=true` read as no search and the list answered every row. | <a id="fjs-2326"></a>FJS-2326 | mesa — **On the terminal target prose holding a link broke its line at the link's edges, and the words after a wrapped link started their row with a space** | 2026-10-10 | A link, and any inline tag something listens to, joins its run like other text (`phrasing` in `src/runtime-terminal.js`), so the engine wraps the sentence between words. Each chunk of the run remembers the text node it came from, and the run hands a press or move on its cells to that node (`relay`, `cellAt` over the engine's `lineInfo`). Tab still stops at a hidden link (`focusables` counts a node the run paints), and the focus cue inverts its cells, on every row it wraps across. The engine blurs a node it hides, and a link that joins a run after taking focus would lose it, so `hide` gives the focus straight back without firing focus or blur events. `verify:tui` found this: `/people/` focuses its Pay runs link before the first frame. The engine's OSC 8 `link` on a chunk is not used: an `href` here is a route path the app follows, and a terminal would open it as a URL. `specs/flow.spec.mjs` 44/44 (`FlowFocus.mesa` new), 10 deliberate breaks all red; drive 271. `fli dev:tui --frame /reports/` reads the sentence as one paragraph |

Fix in studio.js/studio.html: /api/table counts only when there is no cursor and the client keeps currentTotal across pages (S1); getDbStats's one raw loop now also yields live (soft-delete-excluded) counts and getRowCounts reads them, so /api/info scans each table once, the two loops agree, and nothing passes through $tapQuery, so the Query Log starts empty (S2, S4); boot hands its info to the first Overview load, expiring after 5 s (S2); /api/query reads a SELECT/WITH through LIMIT 1001 and answers { truncated, cap: 1000 } with the panel saying so (S6). Proved by test/studio-load.test.ts (red before, 3 green after; spawns the real CLI) and all seven verify:studio* drives. FJS-2322 holds the rest of Studio's list. · [FJS-2322](#fjs-2322) · [studio.js](packages/litestone/src/tools/studio.js) · [studio.html](packages/litestone/src/tools/studio.html) |
| <a id="fjs-2324"></a>FJS-2324 | litestone — **You may only grant what you hold guards the Capability[] column and not a key to a row holding one, so an admin assigns themselves a role with more than they hold and takes all of it** | 2026-10-10 | The escalation guard lived on the Capability[] column alone, so a key to a row holding one — a person's roleId, a Member join row, a nested connect, an implicit many-to-many — handed out the row's whole list unseen. Fixed at the owner of a caller-named parent key: hiddenParents (packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) grades every parent carrying a Capability[] through requireGrantSubset once the caller can read it, reading the row past every rule through the cascade sink; the belongsTo connect/connectOrCreate and the m2m connect/set paths, which set the key after it, call the same requireParentGrants, the m2m on both sides. | <a id="fjs-1245"></a>FJS-1245 | litestone — **A per-move `@gate` on `@@transitions` is a FLOOR on the model's `update` level and can only raise it, so a named move can never be more permissive than an arbitrary edit — and *anybody submits their own document, only a manager approves it* is not expressible.** | 2026-10-10 | By design, ruled FJS-D511 (D): a move's @gate stays a floor on the update level; many-may-submit, few-may-edit is a capability grant (@@capabilities covers moves, FJS-D140), not a lowered gate · [client.js:3695](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244) · [`FJS-D146`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d146) · [FJS-1486](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1486) · [jazzhr schema.lite § Offer](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/db/schema.lite#L594) · [client.js § gradeMove](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [schema.lite § Timesheet](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/db/schema.lite) · [hours.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/connectteam/api/test/hours.test.ts) · [FJS-1244](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1244) · [jazzhr `.probe/phase4/approver.ts`](../fjs-prototypes/jazzhr/.probe/phase4/approver.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-d511"></a>FJS-D511 | litestone — **Should a named move's @gate on @@transitions be able to LOWER the model's update level (a move-grain drop box), rather than only floor it?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D511`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d511): **D** — Keep the floor; *many may submit, few may edit* is a capability grant, not a gate. Everyone who makes the move sits at the update level, and `@@capabilities` (which covers moves by default, `FJS-D140`) grants `Timesheet.submit` to many roles and `Timesheet.update` to few. | <a id="fjs-2179"></a>FJS-2179 | mesa — **On the terminal target a `<p>` holding text and an inline element painted one piece per row, and a row narrower than its pieces painted them over each other** | 2026-10-10 | A box from the tag table is a `FlowBox` (`src/runtime-terminal.js`): each frame, before layout, it paints each run of text and text-only inline tags as one engine text built from their chunks and cell attributes, which the engine wraps between words, and hides the run; the run's own nodes stay, so live text, attributes and block markers keep writing to them. A box of only inline-level children is a row that wraps, so a button sits beside its words. White space collapses as a browser's does, across tag edges and at a line's start, and the emitter now keeps the white space between two siblings that may share a line. A button is a flow too, so a label's edge spaces collapse into its brackets, and a field is 20 cells wide unless `size` says otherwise. `specs/flow.spec.mjs` 28/28, 16 deliberate breaks all red; drive 255. `/invoices/` reads as prose. A link stays a node of its own, so prose breaks at a link's edges: [`FJS-2326`](#fjs-2326) |
| <a id="fjs-2317"></a>FJS-2317 | litestone — **@@trail added 100–170 µs to every audited write, twenty times the create it records, because each entry paid its own index commit** | 2026-10-10 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (R5), whose diagnosis was half right. Split on this machine: building the entry is ~3 µs, the bare JSONL append ~14 µs, and the index's BEGIN IMMEDIATE + INSERT + COMMIT ~90–120 µs — five B-tree pages (the table and four indexes) into the WAL per line, plus the checkpoints they bring. The create pipeline the audit blamed is not the cost, so no raw append path was built. Fix: fireLog queues per log table, and the entries one tick produced go down as one createMany, so one lock and one commit; a lone write is still one create. A batch that throws is retried entry by entry, so one entry a SQL trail's own model refuses drops that entry and not the others, and $logStats counts per entry. A SQL trail's batch announces one collection event where single creates announced rows. Ablation create/log-audit: +168 µs to +25 µs at load ~10. That bench writes in one tick, so the gain is for a request or job writing several rows; a request writing one row still pays one commit. Proved by trail-batch.test.ts (one createMany per tick, lines in order with the index naming each, the refused-entry fallback); log-flush-on-exit.test.ts unchanged · [audit-log.js](packages/litestone/src/core/audit-log.js) · [trail-batch.test.ts](packages/litestone/test/trail-batch.test.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-2297"></a>FJS-2297 | litestone — **A scoped caller writing a @secret column through `system: [col]` files a trail entry saying the field went null to null, and both row images omit it, where the same write as system logs `[redacted]`** | 2026-10-10 | The trail's images were the row as the caller reads it: the update prefetch and the row a write read back both pass through read(), whose field policy strips a protected column for a non-system caller, so redaction never saw the column and a scoped system: ['apiKey'] write logged null to null with images missing it. trailImage() in makeTable overlays the column's [redacted] or [personal] mark wherever the stored row holds a value the caller's row lacks, and is applied to the update prefetch and to the row writeUnit hands finishWrite for the trail — update, create, delete, soft remove, upsert — never to the row the caller gets. The same overlay now gives an asSystem image of a @hashed column its mark, which read() strips for everyone. test/trail-scoped-redaction.test.ts: update, create and delete, as system and through system: [], red on the three scoped cases with the overlay stubbed. Litestone suite 6105 pass; fli prove 11/15, the four failures being FJS-1642, FJS-1366, FJS-1382 and basecamp refusing a port 8120 another process holds. · [audit-log.js](packages/litestone/src/core/audit-log.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2308"></a>FJS-2308 | litestone — **A caller can put another row's stored ref in a File[] on create, and replacing that array later deletes the other row's file from storage** | 2026-10-10 | FileStorage passed a File[] item that was not a file value straight through as a stored ref, so a create could store another row's key and the row's next replace or delete had the provider delete the other row's file. FileStorage now refuses on create (and createMany) any File[] item that is not a file value, a bare JSON-string ref included, and on update keeps a ref item only when every row the where matches already holds that key, writing the stored ref in place of the caller's copy, and refusing when the rows cannot be read. test/file-array-ref.test.ts (create, createMany, upsert, update, updateMany, the substituted ref) is red with the fix stubbed; file-array-update.test.ts's carry-forward append still passes, and the suite is green · [file-storage.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js) · [external-ref.js](packages/litestone/src/plugins/external-ref.js) · [FJS-D666](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d666) |
| <a id="fjs-2314"></a>FJS-2314 | litestone · sierra — **The OPFS pool VFS refuses `journal_mode = WAL` in silence, so every autocommit write on a device was a rollback-journal cycle: 10–12 ms a row in Chrome** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (B1). The pool has no xShmMap, so the WAL pragma answers the previous mode and `synchronous = NORMAL` did nothing. Measured in Chrome over the pool, 300 autocommit inserts: DELETE/NORMAL 12.1 ms, PERSIST/NORMAL 6.3, PERSIST/OFF 0.113, MEMORY/OFF 0.063. Fix: an engine may declare `journal: { mode, synchronous }` (checked against SQLite's words in setEngine, since it is spliced into a PRAGMA); sqlite-wasm declares PERSIST/OFF, which keeps a journal that rolls back a killed tab — MEMORY would lose the file to one. Only a power loss mid-commit can cost the file, which is a cache (unsent writes live in IndexedDB). The wasm path no longer reaches applyWal, so its SharedArrayBuffer busy-retry cannot fire in a worker. Proved by the browser drive (new row: 30 single creates under 2.5 ms each, red at 10.1) and engine-seam.test.ts (a bad journal refused by name). · [sqlite-wasm.js](packages/litestone/src/engines/sqlite-wasm.js) · [databases.js](packages/litestone/src/core/databases.js) · [engine.js](packages/litestone/src/core/engine.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2313"></a>FJS-2313 | litestone — **Every non-system write ran one exists() per foreign key it named, whether or not the parent could hide anything, so a plain create naming two parents was three statements** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (R2). Fix: hiddenParents asks the parent table `tbl[NARROWED]()` — READ_RULES' own `narrows`, the same answer FJS-2312 uses — and when no rule narrows it and the gate admits the model (beforeRead, no query), the parent cannot be hidden and no exists() runs. A gate refusal falls through to the existing two-query verdict. The audit's proposed skip list left out soft delete, templates and the effective window, which would have admitted a create under a soft-deleted parent; deriving from READ_RULES cannot drift that way. Proved by fast-paths.test.ts (no exists() for an open parent; red on HEAD) and hidden-parent.test.ts unchanged. · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2312"></a>FJS-2312 | litestone — **One @@gate or @@allow anywhere in a schema turned the findUnique-by-pk and findMany({}) fast paths off for every model, and every verb scanned every model's shape for a policied aggregate** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (R3, R4). Both preconditions were client-wide (`ctx.hasPolicies`, `plugins.hasPlugins`), so in basecamp — every model gated — the paths were dead code. Fix: each READ_RULES row declares `narrows` (refused at load without one), and the fast paths run when no rule but soft delete narrows this model for this flavor, with the plugin beforeRead/afterRead hooks around the statement; a beforeRead that rewrites the args is honored by asking the shape again after it. buildSQL's own shortcut, which assumed the old preconditions, asks the same and only for a read verb. The aggregate scan is `ctx.hasPoliciedAggregates`, computed once. Measured A/B in a gated app: findUnique 3.7 µs against 5.3–6.7 on HEAD (load ~8). Proved by fast-paths.test.ts (path taken in a gated app, red on HEAD; gate refusal, afterRead, a plugin read filter, a rewriting beforeRead), verbs-rules and litestone suites, basecamp verify 349/349 in an isolated worktree. · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [args.js](packages/litestone/src/core/args.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2311"></a>FJS-2311 | litestone — **tenants.create() with no migrations directory ran every DDL statement autocommit: 3.6–4.0 s per tenant on a 188-model schema, and a statement that threw left a half-built file behind** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (P1, P2). Each statement was its own fsync. Fix: one BEGIN IMMEDIATE around the loop (PRAGMAs filtered, journal_mode cannot change inside one); on a throw, ROLLBACK and the file and its -wal/-shm are removed, as the migrations branch already did. createClient's fresh-database loop gets the same transaction. Measured on this machine at load ~10: 3.6 s to 0.34–0.66 s. The DDL is CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS, so an orphan left by a crash was completed on retry rather than wedging the id. Proved by tenant-create.test.ts (a forced mid-loop throw leaves no file and no registration and the id creates again; a budget on the scale fixture), both red before. · [tenant.js](packages/litestone/src/tenant.js) · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2310"></a>FJS-2310 | litestone · sierra — **A device never ran a migration, so a @@sync model gaining a column kept its old table and every write-through naming the column failed: the device held nothing and said so only offline** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (side finding), reproduced in Chrome: `table shelf has no column named aisle`. Fix: the worker marks the file with a hash of the DDL its schema generates, in PRAGMA user_version (readable before a client exists, no table for introspection to find), and discards a built file whose mark differs before createClient opens it — a device holds a window of the server's rows and its unsent writes live in IndexedDB (sierra pending.js), so it is refilled, not migrated. A file from before the mark reads 0 and is discarded once. Proved by three new rows in the browser drive (grown schema opens the same file, a write naming the new column lands, the old rows are gone) and an unchanged-schema reload still finding its rows; example verify:offline 55/0. · [worker.js](packages/litestone/src/browser/worker.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2309"></a>FJS-2309 | litestone — **After 500 distinct statements the statement LRU finalized the statements the findUnique-by-pk and findMany({}) fast paths held, and both threw "Statement has finalized" until restart** | 2026-10-09 | Found by the 2026-10-09 performance audit (R1); the findMany half only on a @@softDelete model, the only one with that path. Masked in gated apps only because FJS-2312 kept the paths off. Fix: the statement is probed once at table build (an @@external table may not exist yet) and fetched from readDb per call — one LRU hit. Since readDb routes to the writer inside the holder's transaction, the paths no longer stand down inside one. Proved by statement-eviction.test.ts (600 IN-list lengths, then both verbs, :memory: and file; four red before). · [client.js](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2296"></a>FJS-2296 | litestone — **A findMany `window` spec puts every column name into the SQL verbatim, so a scoped caller reads a @guarded column through `window: { p: { max: "pin" } }` and a crafted name runs any subquery; an unknown name answers 0 or null instead of being refused** | 2026-10-10 | The window builder spliced every name, number and keyword of a window spec into the pattern, and the client never asked whether a name was a column the caller may aggregate, so a scoped caller read a @guarded column through max, a quote in a lag name closed the identifier and appended a subquery, and an unknown name answered a constant. findMany now holds every name windowColumnNames lists to refuseAggregateKeys — function arguments at the value tier, partitionBy and orderBy at the naming tier — and buildWindowCols quotes each through quoteIdent under the model's columnMap, takes offset, n, ntile and frame bounds only as integers, direction and nulls only as their keywords, and a default only as a primitive. test/window-names.test.ts (five red before the fix, seven green after, incl. an @map case); the sql-idents ceiling for query.js lowered 75 to 62. · [query.js](packages/litestone/src/core/query.js#L2431) · [args.js](packages/litestone/src/core/args.js#L695) |
| <a id="fjs-2062"></a>FJS-2062 | litestone · mcp · junction — **A caller over JSON or /mcp cannot supply a File column at all: the tool schema asks for the stored reference object and the ORM refuses every object for a File column, so an app whose core flow is an upload cannot do that flow through its own agent** | 2026-10-10 | Per FJS-D666 (A): FileStorage reads a base64 data URL or { name, type, data } as bytes (readValue → readInline; non-base64 refused by name, @accept grades the sniffed bytes), and generateJsonSchema's create/update modes point a File column and a File[]'s items at a new FileInput def instead of the stored FileRef, so the /mcp create tool describes the bytes. A caller's FileRef object is refused as 'a stored reference is written by the server, never sent', not by the atomic operators. typegen's Create/Update type File as FileInput; junction's 413 names http.maxBodySize. Proven by packages/litestone/test/file-json-value.test.ts (14) and packages/mcp/test/file-column.test.ts (4: photos_create over /mcp with a data URL and with { name, type, data } writes the row and the PNG bytes to local storage; the ref sent back is refused, nothing written). litestone 6092/0, mcp 158/0, junction 2748/0, sierra 2019/0, toolbelt 668/0 · [FJS-2061](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-2061) · [FJS-1824](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1824) · [Charter PLAN § Phase 8](../fjs-prototypes/charter/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-d666"></a>FJS-D666 | litestone — **How does a JSON or /mcp caller supply a File column: an inline data-URL value FileStorage reads as bytes, an upload route whose ref the create then accepts, or both?** | 2026-10-10 | Ruled [`FJS-D666`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d666): **A** — an inline value: a data URL (RFC 2397, `data:<mime>;base64,…`) or `{ name, type, data }` with base64 `data`, which `readValue` decodes into bytes like a Buffer, `@accept` sniffing the bytes and not trusting the claimed type. The input schema for a File column is derived to describe that value rather than `FileRef`. The bound is junction's `http.maxBodySize`, the one a browser's multipart upload already meets, so it is not a new option. Its default is 256KB and base64 adds a third, so about 190KB of file gets through untouched: a phone photo (2–5MB) is refused until the app raises that setting, which is the same raise a form upload of that photo already needs. Today the 413 reads *Request body exceeds 262144 bytes* and names nothing to raise, so it names `http.maxBodySize`. One call, and the server stays the only author of a ref. · [IDEAS/owed-rulings.md](IDEAS/owed-rulings.md) |
| <a id="fjs-2198"></a>FJS-2198 | toolbelt · ui — **The json and predicate walkers have no depth bound: diffDocs and searchDoc are O(depth³) (20.7 s and 43 s on a 12 KB document 2000 deep), every json walker overflows the stack at 100k, and the predicate parser escapes deep nesting as RangeError rather than ParseError** | 2026-10-10 | No depth bound: a path key is as long as its path and every marked row made one per ancestor (cubic work, 20 s at 2000 deep), json walkers overflowed the stack at 100k, and the predicate parser recursed per `!`/`(` into RangeError and scanned linearly for duplicate object keys. Fixed with one MAX_DEPTH=100 in toolbelt json: treeRows, expandToDepth, sameValue, keepUnchanged, diffDocs, searchDoc and patchText refuse deeper documents with a RangeError naming the bound, tryParse reports it as {ok:false}, and exceedsDepth lets Json.mesa show a notice instead of walking. parse.js counts nesting through ternary/not and throws a ParseError past 100, and the duplicate-key check is a Set. Proved by json.spec (100k-deep refusals in under 2 s, depth 99 still walks, tryParse), predicate.spec (ParseError for 50k '!' and 20k '(', 20k keys under 1 s, counter released after a throw) and the Json browser drive (#abyss, 100k deep). · [json.js:1023](packages/toolbelt/src/json/json.js#L1023) · [Json.mesa:151](packages/ui/components/display/Json.mesa#L151) · [JsonInput.mesa:142](packages/ui/components/forms/JsonInput.mesa#L142) · [parse.js:152](packages/toolbelt/src/predicate/parse.js#L152) |
| <a id="fjs-2145"></a>FJS-2145 | junction — **A socket closed for backpressure reaches the peer as 1006, not the 1013 the transport claims, so client logic keyed on 1013 is dead** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: holdFrame closed an over-cap socket with 1013 'backpressure', but the close frame queues behind the same full buffer, so the peer saw 1006; the client has no branch keyed on 1013 (it rejects pending calls on any close). Fix per owner ruling: the claim is deleted — ws.close() passes no code or reason, and send-queue.ts, config/index.ts and http.ts no longer name 1013. Proved by ws-backpressure.test.ts (asserts no code claimed; red against the 1013 close), junction suite 2748/0 and typecheck clean. Drives: verify, basecamp verify and verify:offline failed on example db state (5 existing users, needs --reset), verify:live carries open FJS-1382/1371; none touch the close path. · [send-queue.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/send-queue.ts) · [client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |
| <a id="fjs-2159"></a>FJS-2159 | junction — **A foreign (crossProcess) event's broadcast grading is unproved: no test puts a write from a second process through the per-recipient grade onto a socket** | 2026-10-10 | Coverage gap, no code defect: the foreign-event path already grades per recipient (litestone's deliverForeignEvent dispatches into the same $tapEvents set announceDataWrites grades). Added a test to test/realtime-grading.test.ts that spawns a real second process writing to a crossProcess file and asserts the owner's socket gets the row while an anonymous and a stranger's get nothing; with sendGraded bypassed in announceDataWrites it goes red (anon receives the row), restored it is green. Junction suite 2748 pass, typecheck clean. · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-2207"></a>FJS-2207 | toolbelt · sierra — **runAroundHooks shares one cursor across next() calls, so an outer around hook that retries reaches the inner call directly and skips every inner around hook** | 2026-10-10 | runAroundHooks kept one cursor shared across every next() call, so an outer hook calling next() a second time (retry) jumped straight to inner(), skipping each around hook below it. | <a id="fjs-2240"></a>FJS-2240 | junction — **A throw in HttpTransport._handleRequest after the route matches but before _execute (parseQuery, _buildContext) escapes the try, so it answers Bun's bare 500 with no errorResponse mapping and no correlation id** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: parseQuery and _buildContext ran between the route match and _execute's try, so a throw there reached only Bun's server-level handler (bare 500, no status mapping). | <a id="fjs-2288"></a>FJS-2288 | litestone — **A retention sweep is one blocking statement that first reads every doomed row into memory whenever any plugin is attached, and createClient runs it before the app listens — so the first boot after a window is declared over an existing table takes the process down: 22.8 minutes to listening and 28 GB resident for 26M rows** | 2026-10-10 | The sweep was one DELETE per table, preceded by a SELECT * of every doomed row whenever pluginRunner.hasPlugins was true, and createClient awaited it before resolving. runSqliteRetention (retention.js) now takes RETENTION_BATCH (1000) rowids per statement through createdAt's mapped column, reads them back only where hears(model) says something listens, and yields a macrotask between batches; PluginRunner.hearsDeletes is true only for a plugin whose onAfterDelete is not the inherited empty one, so a gate reads nothing, and the client asks the cascade sink's cascadeHeard() for a child with a listener. The boot pass takes one batch a table and drains a backlog after createClient resolves; $retain() waits for that drain, and $close() stops it between batches. advise rule retention-without-an-index names a swept model with no index leading with createdAt (a @@softDelete model's @@index is partial and does not count). test/retention.test.ts § a sweep is batches that yield (boot leaves a 5,007-row backlog for the drain, a closed client stops quietly, 2,005 rows go as 1000/1000/5 with the event loop running between, a model nobody hears is never SELECT *'d, the gate hears no delete) and test/advise.test.ts § retention-without-an-index; litestone 6047 pass, studio drives green; example verify fails at subDetail.changePlan (FJS-1376) and basecamp verify refuses a non-empty dev db (FJS-2162), neither reachable from this diff. · [retention.js](packages/litestone/src/core/retention.js) · [client.js _runRetention](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [api/bin/volume.ts](../fjs-prototypes/serpgrid/api/bin/volume.ts) · [PLAN.md § Q6](../fjs-prototypes/serpgrid/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-2284"></a>FJS-2284 | junction — **Under strategy row a @@commitment on a model scoped through its parent (no tenancy column of its own) still cannot fire: due() has no column to answer a tenant from, so the fire is dispatched with tenant null and the app's principal holds no claim** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: due() answered a tenant only from a column on the declaring model, so a model scoped through its parent (no column, generated tenancy deny delegating to a parent) returned no tenant and the sweep dispatched with tenant null. Fix: due() walks the model's generated tenancy delegation denies to the model carrying the column, selects the column through that relation chain, and answers the parent's tenant (junction's sweep already dispatches row.tenant, unchanged). Proved by test/commitment.test.ts 'a model scoped through its parent answers the parent's tenant' (red before, green after); caravan commitments suite green. · [client.js due()](packages/litestone/src/core/client.js) · [commitments/index.ts](packages/junction/src/plugins/commitments/index.ts) |
| <a id="fjs-1994"></a>FJS-1994 | litestone · junction — **A @@commitment on a model whose update gate is 8 parses, migrates, boots and is swept, and can never fire: no principal grades 8 and the fire never moves through asSystem()** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: the commitment fire makes its move as a graded caller (gate, row policy and the move's own @gate all apply; 8 and 9 are sentinels no principal holds), and nothing at parse compared the two, so the sweep dispatched a fire that failed on every Caravan retry. Fix: parser.js validate() refuses a @@commitment whose target model's update gate position is 8 or 9 (cascading from the left as the runtime does), or whose move is @gate(8\|9), naming the model, the level and the way out (lower to 7 and admit the system principal by row policy, mark the move @system, or drop the commitment). Proof: two new tests in litestone commitment.test.ts go red with the check stubbed and green with it, covering model gate, cascaded position, the hopped target model, and move @gate; caravan commitments.test.ts's 'gate the app's principal does not clear' case moved from gate 9 to 7 since 9 is now refused at parse; example and basecamp schemas still parse clean. The transition-level (serpgrid Scan) half is the same check; the row-tenancy tail stays FJS-2231. · [commitments/index.ts](packages/junction/src/plugins/commitments/index.ts) · [chatwoot schema.lite SlaClock](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/db/schema.lite) · [schema.lite Scan](../fjs-prototypes/serpgrid/db/schema.lite) |

| <a id="fjs-2230"></a>FJS-2230 | print-kit — **A print job whose `Runtime.evaluate` goes unanswered wedges the two jobs after it the same way, so a failed print is not contained to one job** | 2026-10-10 | Cause: the queue, not Chrome. A job that failed on an unanswered call then asked the same wedged browser to close its tab (inTab) and awaited the drive's close(), which sends Target.closeTarget first — each a further 30 s bound, so the failed job held the queue 93 s and the two jobs behind it hit their 30 s test timeout: the offline test and the two after it, as Transit saw. Fix in src/printer.js: an unanswered error drops the tab without asking, and discard() does not hold the queue for the close (close() still awaits it). Proved by test/print-kit.test.js 'a wedged browser', which SIGSTOPs the printer's Chrome: the next job printed at 93.7 s before, under 40 s on a fresh browser after; fli prove green. |

| <a id="fjs-2273"></a>FJS-2273 | mesa — **A `{@const}` stayed in scope past its block on the DOM path: `{#if n}{@const x = n * 2}…{/if}<p>{x}</p>` compiled the outer `{x}` to a memo declared only inside the branch, so it threw a ReferenceError that the render contained into a blank; and a `{@const}` reading no state was shadowed by an outer signal of the same name** | 2026-10-09 | `constDeclaration(ctx, value)` in compiler.js is the one reading of a `{@const}` for both targets — parse, rewrite, memo or plain, the statements that declare it — and it registers each name in the open `constScope(ctx)`, which `buildBlock` opens around its body and closes after, putting the outer accessors back. | <a id="fjs-1779"></a>FJS-1779 | litestone — **Auto-factories cannot seed a quarter of the models a generated schema declares: withParents().createOne() writes @scoped edges as columns, ignores cross-field @@check and @required(where:), leaves @type'd Json empty, and draws one-letter slugs that collide** | 2026-10-09 | Fixed 2026-10-09 in litestone, one cause at a time, each with a test in test/factory-schema-rules.test.ts. seeder.js: one seq counter per model shared by every clone (the Customer inside withParents and the one at the top drew the same values); a composite relation fills every column of its key and claims them from a single-column sibling; the first column of a non-optional @@arc and a key under @required(where:) get a parent though nullable; a pin satisfies one relation to its model and a second relation to the same parent gets its own row. testing.js: _fieldValue is shared by a model's columns and a type's members, so Json @type carries the type; @required(where:) is read as required; DateTime @date draws a date; @scoped/@edge are skipped; _satisfyChecks reads a @@check as the SQL subset a schema writes and moves the row until it passes (orderings, AND/OR, IN, IS NULL, identities, a now()-stamped sibling read with the clock, two predicates made to agree); the regex sampler draws a digit of seq per class and six characters for an open quantifier. Measured by a ladder sweep over the 21 Charter freehand schemas: 323 of 323 models seeded where the drive had 139 of 176. **Amended 2026-10-09:** Charter's own `db/schema.lite` was outside that sweep, and its `access.test.ts` stayed red with 100 rows from one App rule, `@@check("ownerId IS NOT NULL OR runId IS NOT NULL")`. There were two causes. That rule was not one the seeder read as insisting on a parent, and `_satisfyChecks` never moves a key, so neither half could build the row. And `verifyGateLadder`'s create row built parents in `_freshParents`, which skipped every nullable key: the `@@arc` and `@required(where:)` rules above never reached a create. seeder.js `insistedKeys(def)` is now the one set both paths read, adding the first key of a `@@check` whose every OR branch is `key IS NOT NULL`. | <a id="fjs-1063"></a>FJS-1063 | junction — **`record().ready` resolves `null` for a refusal and for a row that is not there, so every detail screen has to write one vague sentence about two different answers.** `fetch()` inside `record()` catches and returns null with the reason discarded ([client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts)), and `ready` is documented as `Promise<T \| null>` — so this is a GAP against a stated contract rather than a violation of one, which is why it is S3 and not higher. The cost is at every caller: a 403 and a 404 are the same value, so the best a screen can say is *it may not exist, or you may not have access to it*, which is what `core/crud-templates.js` now writes because it cannot do better. **Basecamp's screens are the comparison and they had to solve it outside this API** — `verify:screens`' last section exists to prove that one URL gives *not found* against a 404 and *could not load* against a dead API, two sentences, and it gets them by keeping its own `loadError` off a call that throws. A caller that wants the distinction currently has to make a second request that does. **Two candidate shapes and they are different claims**: `ready` REJECTS and callers catch (matches `find`/`get`, breaks anyone relying on the documented null), or the view carries `error` beside `get()` and `ready` keeps resolving (additive, and the reason survives for the push path too). The second is the one that does not move a documented contract, and choosing is the work | 2026-10-09 | `record()` now carries the reason beside `get()` (`FJS-D695`): `error()` answers `{ status, code }` — 403 a refusal, 404 a row not there (also a `removed` push), 0 nothing answered — and `ready` keeps resolving `null`. It is set by `fetch()` and the composed read, cleared by the next successful read, and re-emitted to subscribers because a refresh or `changed` re-read can be refused after the first load; sierra's offline stub gains `error`, and the generated detail page in `crud-templates.js` now says *not found* / *no access* / *could not load* instead of one vague sentence. Proved by `test/nodes.test.ts` `resource().record(id).error()` (six cases, red before) and the cli `generated-mesa` suite; the example drives failing under `fli prove` are open rows (`FJS-1642`/`FJS-1376` at subDetail.changePlan, `FJS-2027`, `FJS-1382`) and none reaches `record()` · [client/index.ts `record`](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [crud-templates.js](packages/cli/core/crud-templates.js) · [FJS-533](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-533) |

The synthesized model now carries @@gate("8"), as the auth models do. buildTableForModel wraps a trail table's reads and creates in the plugin runner's before-hooks, the way makeTable does. The trail's own writes go through ctx.jsonlTableCache, which skips that wrapper. The synthesized gate is left out of schemaDeclaresAccessRules, because no SQL statement reaches the jsonl file and a schema with no gates keeps db.sql. | <a id="fjs-712"></a>FJS-712 | junction — **The realtime layer's cohorts never collapse, its live stores grow unbounded, and three protocol halves promise a frame the wire never carries.** Umbrella for the remaining realtime findings, `D7`'s second face. **`realtime-9`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-764`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-764)**: the key is the principal's VALUE now, memoized on the session object, and 100 sockets of one person went 606.7 µs → 75.5 µs against a real Data boundary while 100 distinct people did not move. **`realtime-10`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-766`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-766)**: a live store loaded with a `limit` and no `orderBy` grows without bound, because `limit` trims only under an ordering — `load({}, {limit:20})` with 3000 pushes reached `len=3003 stale=2983` and 107 → 221 MB of RSS; wants a trim in arrival order, or a refusal by name for a live load with a limit and no ordering. **`realtime-11`** — split out and **closed as [`FJS-765`](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-765)**, where the reproduction corrected it: the ungraded half had gone with `FJS-672` already and what was left is the opposite failure — every service but the winner was told NOTHING. As filed: two services over one model announce every write TWICE, the second under the other service's event name and UNGRADED, because the `serviceFor()` index is last-claim-wins and the suppression compares against the winner — `orders.create(customerId=5)` reached an anonymous socket as `orders2 created`. Wants a model → SET of services index, each announcement graded with its own accessor; it is the same index `FJS-700` reads. **`realtime-12`**: the backpressure close code 1013 never reaches the wire — the peer sees 1006 — because closing a socket whose buffer is full tears down abnormally, so any client logic keyed on 1013 is dead, the same class as the dead 4001 branch in `FJS-702`; wants an `overloaded` frame before the close, or the 1013 claim deleted. Isolation itself is good: 40 MB to a sleeping reader left a healthy reader at 200/200. **`realtime-13`**: two replicas share events only if the schema declares `announce crossProcess`, and the default `inProcess` drops them silently with nothing warning — a write on B reached a socket on A after 156 ms under crossProcess and never under inProcess. The cross-process path is also the ungraded one of `FJS-672`. Wants a boot warning when channels are configured under `inProcess`. **`realtime-14`**: malformed and unknown frames are silently discarded with no `protocol_error`; a `service_call` with no `id` executes and answers an id-less result; and a `meta.id` that is an object is `String()`d into `id=[object Object] not found`. Promote any of these to its own id when it is worked | 2026-10-09 | Split, not fixed: the three halves still open were filed as their own rows -- FJS-2145 (realtime-12, 1013 never on the wire; owner ruled: delete the claim), FJS-2146 (realtime-13, inProcess drops cross-replica events silently), FJS-2147 (realtime-14, malformed and id-less frames). realtime-9, -10 and -11 had closed as FJS-764, FJS-766 and FJS-765 · [channels.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/channels.ts) · [transport/http.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/http.ts) · [send-queue.ts](packages/junction/src/transport/send-queue.ts) · [client/index.ts](packages/junction/src/client/index.ts) · [nodes.ts](packages/junction/src/client/nodes.ts) · [FJS-631](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-631) · [audit § 3.7](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/afba85f4-efe9-4959-92ca-a1b010dd2a16) |

| <a id="fjs-1999"></a>FJS-1999 | junction — **A bearer that is forged, expired or revoked is anonymity on HTTP: the holder of a dead rating link is answered as a stranger, a list as 200 of nothing and the write as a 403 from the rule, so a page cannot say 'this link has expired' — FJS-1830 made a dead session a refusal and the rule never reached bearerClaim** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: bearerClaim answered a presented token that found no live row with an empty principal, so a dead link read as a stranger (list 200 of nothing, write a 403 from the rule) — FJS-1830's refusal never reached it; isLive also read Date.now() instead of the client's clock. Fix: the resolver throws Unauthorized (401, one message for forged, expired and revoked) for a presented token that does not resolve; no token stays {}; expiresAt is read off db.$now(). Proved by test/bearer-claim.test.ts (both new cases red before the fix; junction suite 2687 pass, typecheck clean); chatwoot csat.test.ts pin moved to 401, not rerun. fli prove: failing drives are the open-tagged FJS-1484/1708/915/1382; basecamp verify wants an empty db; example verify exits 1 with no assertion naming bearers (no bearer in it). · [litestone.ts bearerClaim](packages/junction/src/core/litestone.ts) · [chatwoot core/rating.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/src/core/rating.ts) · [csat.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/csat.test.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-2135"></a>FJS-2135 | litestone — **now() + <duration> works in @derived only: @@allow, @@scope and field @allow refuse it at startup, so a per-caller 'due within 2 days' row policy still has no spelling** | 2026-10-09 | The refusal in checkExpr existed because neither interpreter knew the shift node. Fixed at the owners: toolbelt's evaluate gained a shift case and an exported shiftInstant (calendar months overflow as SQLite's modifier does, so @derived and the policies agree), and compileSql binds shiftInstant(ctx._now) as a parameter, so both halves compute the moved instant with one function. Also fixed on the way: a field @allow('read') shaped rows through evalJs with no clock, so any now() there answered NULL and stripped the field -- applyFieldPolicyTo now reads atOneInstant(ctx). Proved by test/policy-interpreters.test.ts (SQL and JS agree over d/min/mo/yr offsets, plus @@scope and field @allow), a toolbelt predicate spec row, and the full litestone suite. fli prove: only open-tagged and already-registered example/basecamp drives fail. · [policy.js](packages/litestone/src/core/policy.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1209"></a>FJS-1209 | orion · toolbelt — **The flow expression's TEXT grammar is a subset of what the engine runs: thirteen forms compile and seven parse, so `template`, `array`, `object`, `pipe`, `let` and `match` can be stored and never written as a line.** A backtick is even tokenized as `TK.TEMPLATE` and read by no parser, so a template literal is a token with no grammar behind it. | 2026-10-09 | The shared predicate parser read neither form: the lexer ended a template at its first backtick and kept no raw source, and the flow dialect of parse.js had no case for TEMPLATE or a brace. Per FJS-D513 the tokenizer now finds a template's close outside its ${…} holes and keeps raw, parse.js reads template (literal runs plus holes parsed as full expressions, lambda scope inherited) and object (name or quoted keys, repeated key refused) in the flow dialect only, and a policy refuses both by name; orion's text.ts compiles them and emit.ts writes them back, keeping a string part after a string part as a hole so the tree round-trips exactly. Proved by emit.test.ts round-trip rows and text.test.ts (orion 626 pass), policy-no-assembly.test.ts (litestone 5974 pass), toolbelt 617 pass, and example verify:automations, whose data field now opens as the language. array, pipe, let and match remain documents. · [`FJS-D271`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d271) · [emit.ts](packages/orion/src/engine/expression/emit.ts) · [parse.js](packages/toolbelt/src/predicate/parse.js) · [tokenize.js](packages/toolbelt/src/predicate/tokenize.js) · [FJS-1198](ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md#fjs-1198) · [`FJS-D513`](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d513) |
| <a id="fjs-2005"></a>FJS-2005 | litestone — **@derived can compare a column to now() and nothing else: now() takes no offset and the expression language has no duration arithmetic, so 'due within 2 days' has no schema spelling and cannot be filtered, sorted or paginated by on the server** | 2026-10-09 | The expression grammar (toolbelt predicate/parse.js) read now() and stopped, so 'dueAt < now() + 2d' failed with 'Expected RPAREN, got +'. now() now takes a ± whole-number duration in the @@commitment literal (ms s min h d wk mo yr), parsed to a { type: 'shift', expr: now } node of its own so an interpreter that does not know it throws rather than dropping the offset; compileDerived (litestone policy.js) emits it as a strftime modifier on NOW_SQL's clock, so the field filters, sorts and paginates on the server. @allow/@@scope/@field-allow refuse it at startup in checkExpr by name. Proof: litestone test/derived-duration.test.ts (read, filter, sort, negative offset, bad unit/fraction, refusals), full litestone and toolbelt suites green; fli prove's remaining failures are the example/basecamp harness and open FJS-1366/FJS-1382, none of which use the syntax. · [parser.js](packages/litestone/src/core/parser.js) |
| <a id="fjs-2011"></a>FJS-2011 | litestone — **The verbs × rules grid's six `globalFilter × write` cells are blind: `observe()` snapshots through `asSystem()`, which does not lift the global filter, so the hidden row is invisible to before/after and `moved` is 0 whatever the write did** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: `observe()` snapshotted through `asSystem()` accessors, which lift neither the global filter nor soft-delete, so the row a rule hides was invisible before and after and `moved` read 0. Seven `globalFilter × write` cells now read OFF and are pinned `D685:OFF` under FJS-D685, which asks whether a write should fold the global filter; `softDelete × delete/deleteMany` read OFF as the documented purge hatch and are `-`. | <a id="fjs-2010"></a>FJS-2010 | litestone — **`findMany({ recursive })` walks THROUGH a row the global filter or a plugin read filter hides, so a visible descendant is reached by a hidden path and its depth names the hidden row** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: `recursive` was absent from the verb sets of the `globalFilter` and `pluginFilters` rules in READ_RULES, so `visibleWhere('recursive')` folded neither scope and the CTE's anchor and step carried only soft-delete/policy; a visible descendant was reached through a hidden row. | <a id="fjs-1996"></a>FJS-1996 | junction — **junction's SMTP mailer refuses the From every help desk sends under, Support <support@acme.test>, which the Resend adapter beside it accepts, and MailMessage has no other place for a name; it also writes a header past SMTP's 998-octet line as one line while refusing a fold, and writes no Message-ID when none is given while send() answers an id no header carries** | 2026-10-09 | Three wire defects in packages/junction/src/mail/smtp.ts, each fixed at the writer. (1) assertMessageAddresses graded every address with assertAddress, a bare addr-spec, so Support <support@acme.test> threw at send; it now grades with assertMailbox, which takes local@domain, <addr>, Name <addr> and "Quoted, Name" <addr> (an unquoted name holding @ , ; stays refused as two addresses in one string), the envelope (MAIL FROM, envelopeRecipients) carries the address alone, and formatMailbox re-renders the name as an atom, quoted-string or encoded-words so nothing typed reaches a header verbatim. (2) Every header line now goes through foldHeader, which folds at whitespace past 78 and refuses a run still past 998 octets, and encodeMimeHeader cuts a non-ASCII value into 45-byte encoded-words so a long subject is foldable. (3) messageIdOf mints <uuid@from-domain> when no Message-ID is stated; sendMessage writes it, sendMail and sendMailBatch return it, and createSmtpMailer's send()/batch() answer it in place of crypto.randomUUID(); the MIME body is now built before MAIL FROM, so a refusal there leaves no transaction open. Proved by test/smtp-message.test.ts 'a help-desk reply on the wire' (7 cases, 6 red before), read off a capturing SMTP sink; junction 2666 pass, typecheck clean. · [smtp.ts](packages/junction/src/mail/smtp.ts) · [mail/index.ts](packages/junction/src/mail/index.ts) · [chatwoot threading.test.ts](../fjs-prototypes/chatwoot/api/test/threading.test.ts) |

| <a id="fjs-1496"></a>FJS-1496 | basecamp · outpost — **Nothing builds an image from a git source.** `buildInitialSteps` gives a git or no-source container app "Build image" and "Push image", which the runner sends as a command-less `/exec`, and outpost acknowledges that as success. `deployments.create` now refuses such an app with a 400 (see basecamp CHANGES 2026-09-28), so the release no longer shows two green steps followed by a Docker Hub pull error.
