| <a id="fjs-1173"></a>FJS-1173 | basecamp — **`api/test/compute.test.ts`'s `afterAll` times out at 5 s, so `bun run test` in basecamp is red on one unnamed failure** | 2026-10-08 | Cause: the file drives every provision and destroy step by hand, while `servers.provision`/`servers.destroy` dispatched the real `server:provision`/`server:destroy` jobs onto a running `fleet` queue. Fix: `beforeAll` pauses the `fleet` queue, so *the job has not run* is true by construction rather than by timing — the 1612 fix shape, not a longer grace. Fix is docs: README leads with the generated form and shows the wired shape; headers match AGENTS.md § Forms. No auto-write mechanism added — callbacks differ per control (event vs value vs {value}). Proof: new test/docs-form-wiring.mjs (red on README before, green after, with accept/refuse control pair), wired into ui's test script; packages/ui bun run test green via fli prove. · [README.md](packages/ui/README.md) · [Form.mesa](packages/ui/components/forms/Form.mesa) · [AGENTS.md](packages/ui/AGENTS.md) |

| <a id="fjs-1136"></a>FJS-1136 | mesa — **A server render hands a component `undefined` for an explicitly-undefined prop where the browser build applies the declared default.** | 2026-10-09 | Same cause and fix as FJS-1538: a prop's undefined, at mount or on a later push, resolves to the declared default in a server render and the browser alike; test/prop-undefined-default.test.js renders through renderComponent for let, const and deferred defaults. · [render-component.js](packages/mesa/src/render-component.js) |
| <a id="fjs-1538"></a>FJS-1538 | mesa — **A prop passed as `undefined` replaces the child's declared default — `<Logo source={settings.image} />` with no `image` renders no logo — where Svelte, whose `export let x = default` Mesa's syntax is, falls back to the default; nothing documents which Mesa means.** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: the first render read the declared default, but the pushProps effect the parent emits after it wrote the parent's value into the child's signal, so undefined replaced the default (server render runs the same effect, which is FJS-1136). The prop registry now carries each prop's fallback (makeExternalProperty 4th arg, propDefault from step 5b for defaults reading later declarations); pushProps, $push and $apply resolve undefined through it, null still clears. Proved by test/prop-undefined-default.test.js (8 of 11 red at HEAD, green now) and the full mesa vitest suite; documented in VISION § 3.1. |
| <a id="fjs-1495"></a>FJS-1495 | mesa — **An unresolved `<Hero />` compiles clean and fails the prerender with *`Hero` does not exist during a server render — a build has no browser. Guard the read with `typeof Hero !== 'undefined'`*: the hint is for a browser global, and following it ships the page with the block silently missing.** | 2026-10-09 | The compiler emitted a component call for any PascalCase tag without checking the name was bound. New checkComponentTagsBound (mesa compiler.js) refuses a tag naming nothing in either script or an each/snippet/await/@const header, naming the tag, its position, and the import/autoImport ways to bind it. Proved by compile-errors.test.js § a component tag must name a binding (red at HEAD); three VISION.md examples that used unimported components fixed; mesa, ui, sierra suites green. |
| <a id="fjs-2065"></a>FJS-2065 | ui · sierra — **A non-null column with a default is badged "(Optional)" in an edit form, and blanking it is refused "must be a number" — the badge reads create's rule, the refusal a patch's** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: `schema.required` is the create-required set, so a non-null defaulted column was badged Optional on an edit while a blank was refused. Fix: `blankRefused(rule, mode)` in field-rules.js is read by `requiredFor`, `resource.requiredFields(record, mode)` and `validateAgainstFields`, and `<Form>` passes its write mode. | <a id="fjs-1983"></a>FJS-1983 | ui — **The data-confirm panel (and ConfirmationPopover) cannot be seen or clicked inside a Drawer or Modal: both are native modal <dialog>s in the top layer and the panel is portaled to <body> beneath them** | 2026-10-09 | ConfirmPanel portaled to <body>, beneath a modal Drawer/Modal in the top layer and inert behind it; it now portals into the anchor's nearest open <dialog> (still fixed: an open .dialog/.drawer is transform: none). That exposed a mesa defect fixed with it: a delegation root read ownership from the root registry, so a handler that closed its own portal inside the app's root released that root mid-click and ran again at the outer root (ConfirmationPopover's onconfirm fired twice); _makeDelegatedHandler now records the nodes each event was delivered to. Proof: ui confirm-in-drawer.spec (4/8 red before) and mesa nested-portal.spec (2 hits per click before, 1 after); ui and mesa suites green, verify:ui green. · [ConfirmPanel.mesa](packages/ui/components/overlay/ConfirmPanel.mesa) |
| <a id="fjs-2093"></a>FJS-2093 | mesa — **A parent that both watches a path of a let ($: draft.notes, () => {}) and passes that let down with bind: (<Child bind:record={draft} />) never re-renders a reassignment of it from an event handler (draft = { ...draft, notes: 'n1' }), and the watch sees the OLD value — drop the watch or the bind: and it works** | 2026-10-09 | A let with a $: path watch compiled to a let $$proxy_x that an effect reassigned, and only a path read through it subscribed to anything; a bare read, the prop pushed to a child, kept the object from before a reassignment, and with bind: the child wrote that stale object back. The proxy is now a memo over the let's signal ($$proxy_x()), the watch handler reads its value untracked so a reassignment runs it once, and bindProp unwraps a watch proxy to its raw target so the child's echo stops at the signal's equality check instead of looping to the cycle guard. Proof: packages/mesa/test/watch-bind-reassign.test.js, three cases red on the pre-fix compiler and runtime; mesa's whole suite and its three browser drives green; example verify:site green, example verify fails only FJS-2028's three known checks. |
| <a id="fjs-2031"></a>FJS-2031 | litestone — **A Boolean write stores the text 'false', '0', 'no' or 'off' as true — the value is coerced by truthiness, not parsed** | 2026-10-09 | serializeBooleans (core/query.js) wrote value ? 1 : 0, so the text 'false' was stored as 1; loadRows and the where filter both parsed it. A write now goes through booleanBind: true/false, 1/0, or text parseCell reads as a boolean ('true'/'false'/'1'/'0', the set loadRows takes), and anything else ('no', 'off', '', 2, an object) is a ValidationError on the field; an @edge Boolean uses the same function. test/boolean-filter.test.ts § a Boolean column written as text was red before (Received: true); suite 5924 pass. · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1969"></a>FJS-1969 | mesa — **A top-level const reading a member of a local handle (`const list = r.list()`) is computed once — `[...list.rows]`, `list.loading && …` — and nothing warns, though its getters read signals** | 2026-10-09 | Cause: FJS-D212 keeps `const list = r.list()` eager, so a static `const` over one of its members (`[...list.rows]`, `list.loading && …`, `list.rows[0]`) is computed once at setup while the handle's getters read signals, and nothing said so. Fix: `analyzeScript` (packages/mesa/src/compiler.js, beside `opaqueValues`) warns for each top-level non-derived `const` reading, outside any function, a member of a binding whose initializer's value is a call (`memberReadOn`). The warning names `$: x = …` to follow the value and destructuring at the call (`const { port } = load()`) as the quiet spelling for a meant snapshot, since that form names no handle (decision-rules answered before the edit; it coins nothing). A call through the handle and a derived handle stay quiet. Over 1149 .mesa files in packages, example and fjs-prototypes it fires 4 times, all prototype read-once bugs. Proof: test/const-promotion.test.js § a const reading a member of a call-result handle, red at HEAD; `bun run test` in mesa green, `example: verify:site` green. `example: verify` failed twice on different assertions, with the shared 8110 peer in play, and this change leaves example's compile unchanged (zero new warnings there). |

A declared auth default now fills only an absent or undefined key (an explicit null is written; on a required column it is refused by name); the claim column row tenancy generates keeps filling a null, marked fillsNull in buildAuthDefaultMap, since the tenancy create deny passes a null column. Proof: packages/litestone/test/auth-default-null.test.ts, red before on every create path, and its tenancy case red with fillsNull stubbed off; litestone, basecamp and orion suites green. · [PLAN.md](../fjs-prototypes/ela/PLAN.md) |

| <a id="fjs-2018"></a>FJS-2018 | cli · mesa — **A Resource for a model named Form fails the build: the compiled module names its component after the file, and the file imports the kit's Form, so rolldown refuses 'Identifier Form has already been declared' — the dev server serves it** | 2026-10-08 | Same defect as FJS-1751, one shape over: an instance-script import is hoisted to module scope beside the component function, and `safeComponentIdent` avoided only what `<script module>` declares, so `Form.mesa` importing the kit's `Form` emitted two `Form` bindings. `moduleScopeNames` now also collects the instance script's import locals, so the component becomes `Form_Component` and the import keeps its name. No generator alias is needed, and ELA's `KitForm` alias can go. Proof: `test/emission.test.js` § the component function name, which fails at HEAD, and a real `vite build` of `Form.mesa` importing a `Form`, which gave 4 PARSE_ERRORs with the HEAD compiler and now builds. |
| <a id="fjs-1751"></a>FJS-1751 | mesa — **A component that renders itself compiles on the dev server and fails the build: the compiled module declares the component under its file's name, so import PageTree from './PageTree.mesa' inside PageTree.mesa is 'Identifier PageTree has already been declared', and Mesa documents no way for a component to name itself** | 2026-10-08 | `safeComponentIdent` avoided `<script module>` names and not the instance script's imports, which are hoisted to module scope; dev hid it because the HMR wrap renames the component to `__mesaOrigFn`. `moduleScopeNames` now collects the instance imports, so `import PageTree from './PageTree.mesa'` inside `PageTree.mesa` compiles to `PageTree_Component` and recursion builds as well as serving. Dev and build agree because the name is free at the source, and `hmr.js` says so where it renames. The spelling is now documented: an AGENTS.md wrong-guess row (`<svelte:self>` / `<mesa:self>` → import your own file), and the `<mesa:self>` warning names the import. Proof: `test/emission.test.js` (one case mounts a three-level self-rendering tree), a real `vite build` of it, and 0 of 268 `.mesa` files in example, ui and sierra compiling any differently. |
| <a id="fjs-1940"></a>FJS-1940 | litestone — **A bare check(rel) takes the operation of the rule it sits in, while the access guide's reference line says it delegates to the related model's READ policy — so @@allow('create', check(inbox)) asks whether the caller may create an INBOX, and only the case where the parent has no row policy at all is warned, at boot, by createClient** | 2026-10-09 | A bare check(rel) compiles with node.operation ?? op (policy.js, case 'check'), so it asks the parent the rule's own operation, and the access guide's reference line said read. The reference line now gives both forms and § Delegating says what the bare one asks; a new advise rule, check-without-an-operation-in-a-write-rule (warn), names every bare check() in an @@allow/@@deny holding an operation other than read and offers check(rel, 'read') or the write op stated outright. No .lite in the tree trips it. Proof: test/advise.test.ts § check-without-an-operation-in-a-write-rule, red for create/update/delete/write/all/'read, create', inside a conjunction and on a deny, before the rule; silent on a read rule and a named op. | <a id="fjs-2069"></a>FJS-2069 | litestone — **`asSystem()` skips `@version` even when the caller supplies one, so a job that read a row, did slow work and wrote it back overwrites a person's save in between, with no error** | 2026-10-09 | prepareUpdate in src/core/client.js skipped the whole @version branch under ctx.isSystem, so a version the caller supplied was dropped unread and a job that read, worked and wrote back overwrote a save in between. Per FJS-D659 the branch now runs for a system caller whenever a version is present: a moved row throws VersionConflictError, a non-integer throws VersionRequiredError, and an absent one stays exempt for migrations. test/litestone.test.ts § @version — runtime, two new tests red before the fix; litestone suite 5910 pass. docs/CONSISTENCY.md lost-update row and the README updated. · [FJS-D659](DECISIONS.md#fjs-d659) · [consistency.md](IDEAS/consistency.md) |
| <a id="fjs-1921"></a>FJS-1921 | litestone — **A retention sweep deletes a row and leaves its File objects in storage for ever: $retain() is a raw DELETE, and FileStorage cleans up only from the ORM's delete hooks, so a database's declared retention never reaches the bytes** | 2026-10-09 | runSqliteRetention ran a bare DELETE, and FileStorage releases objects only from onAfterDelete, which the sweep never called. With plugins installed the sweep now adds RETURNING * and gives each table's removed rows to pluginRunner.afterDelete, the hook an ORM delete fires. The boot pass moved after pluginRunner.init and is awaited, and $retain() resolves to the same summary once the objects are gone (index.d.ts, and the example and basecamp jobs, await it). test/retention.test.ts § a swept row takes its files with it covers $retain() and the startup pass against a real local provider: red before, red again with onRemoved stubbed, and the litestone suite passes (5908). · [runSqliteRetention](packages/litestone/src/core/retention.js) · [storage/file-storage.js](packages/litestone/src/storage/file-storage.js) |

## See also

- `ISSUES_ARCHIVE.md` — closed rows aged out of § Closed. Same table, verbatim. Read when tracing a specific id, never cold.
- `CLAUDE.md` § Live hazards — traps that are **correct behavior**; knowing them saves a day. Not issues.
- `DECISIONS.md` — settled rulings. A `FJS-D##` closing here lands there.
- `IDEAS/` — roadmap and design records for work not started. `IDEAS/overview.md`'s wave 0 is the subset of this file that happens to be argued in an ideas file.
- `HANDOFF.md` — per-session narrative, newest first. History, not a ledger.
- `VERIFYING.md` — how to promote a `stale?` row to `open` or `closed`.
